Linear superposition as a core theorem of quantum empiricism
Yurii V. Brezhnev ∗

Abstract
. Clarifying the nature of the quantum state | 𝚿 ⟩ |\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle is at the root of the problems with insight into counter-intuitive quantum postulates. We provide a direct—and math-axiom free—empirical derivation of this object as an element of a vector space. Establishing the linearity of this structure—quantum superposition—is based on a set-theoretic creation of ensemble formations and invokes the following three principia: ( I ) quantum statics, ( II ) doctrine of the number in the physical theory, and ( III ) mathematization of matching the two observations with each other (quantum covariance). All of the constructs rest upon a formalization of the minimal experimental entity—the registered micro-event, detector click. This is sufficient for producing the ℂ {\mathbb{C}} -numbers, axioms of linear vector space (superposition principle), statistical mixtures of states, eigenstates and their spectra, and non-commutativity of observables. No use is required of the spatio-temporal concepts. As a result, the foundations of theory are liberated to a significant extent from the issues associated with physical interpretations, philosophical exegeses, and mathematical reconstruction of the entire quantum edifice.



https://arxiv.org/abs/1807.06894

1.
Introduction and Summary

… somewhat curious that, even after

nearly a full century, physicists still do not

quite agree on what the theory tells us …

— G. ’t Hooft

[
86
, p. 5]

It is almost a crying shame that we are

nowhere close to that with quantum mechanics,

given that it is over 70 years old now

— C. Fuchs

[
63
, p. 32]

The contradiction between the fundamental nature of quantum theory
(
QT
) and the phenomenological feature of its mathematics

[
123
]
is likely to never cease instigating the attempts to
overcome it. As H. Putnam had said, “Human curiosity will not rest
until … questions [of the nature of the
QT
-formalism] are
answered”.

The subject-matter and leitmotiv of what follows is that the linear
superposition and theory’s axioms have an origin—they are
derivable, and it is entirely empirical. The theory is thereby
demystified, and the interpretative challenges that accompany the
exegeses of
QT
are a
nonexistent
problem coming from “a
confusion of categories”
[
129
, p. 89]
, i. e., from the
“semantic confusion”
[
160
, p. 10]
. A direct outgrowth of
this ideology is not only a derivation of the superposition
principle (page
•
‣
6.3
) but also the axiom-free production
of the ‘chief’ quantum formula—the Born rule
p=|\mathfrak{a}|^{2}

[
27
]
.

1.1.
On foundations of quantum theory

The debates concerning the foundations of quantum mechanics (
QM
)
hitherto “show no sign of abating”
[
156
, p. 222]
,

[
148
,
103
]
, and despite widespread scepticism

[
104
,
54
,
50
,
66
,
130
,
107
]
it is
generally acknowledged that the problem is a real one

[
154
,
3
,
103
,
126
,
33
]
—it is not
something made up or “just a dispute over words”

[
2
, p. 5]
—and sometimes “has been regarded as a very
serious one”
[
107
, p. 418]
,
[
133
,
189
]
. Say,
R. Penrose has expressed (2004) an even more radical “conviction
that present-day quantum mechanics has no credible ontology, so that
it
must
be seriously modified”.

In recent decades the discussions have even worsened
[
63
,
64
]
, and current research has intensified, due to the
tremendously increased and formerly inconceivable technological
possibilities of operating with individual micro-objects and the
urge to implement the idea of quantum computing
[
42
,
2
]
.

The reason for this state of affairs remains the same as it was
before. Unlike the classical theories—e. g., thermodynamics or
relativity theory, “Ma di assiomatizzazioni della teoria quantistica
ce ne sono moltissime”
[
3
, p. 30]
and the
QM
-axiomatics
itself seems wholly divorced from human language
[
131
,
44
,
3
,
70
,
20
,

,
85
,
103
,
132
,
148
,
160
,
162
,
107
]
. Quantum postulates are not
merely formal. They are phrased in terms of linear operators on a
complex Hilbert space
{\mathbb{H}}

[
9
,
15
,
41
,
66
,
89
,
129
,
131
,
97
,
104
]
and, with that,
literally not a single word here can be brought into conjunction
with reality by means that have
at least some kind
of
relationship with the classical description. What is more, it is
very well known that the abstract character of these terms is
required by the essence of the point (covariance) and, at the same
time, that the attempt to link them with physical images is imposed
by a decree and results in the famous paradoxes associated with such
concepts as causality, (non)locality, and realism
[
71
,
190
,
6
,
7
,
8
,
20
,
65
,
69
,
103
,
108
,
70
]
. All of that causes a problem with
interpretations of
QM
.

It is well known that the theory has steadfastly resisted any unique
ontological reading and, in particular, reconciliation between
interpretations. This is reflected not only in the voluminousness of
the literature. The differences in viewpoint are often based on
points of principle
[
16
,
153
,

,

, and even

An interesting fact. Cambridge University Press has published a

[

]
containing an arresting electronic

[
64

and now
covers the 1995–2011 years. It characterizes the state of affairs

(!) and in

]
), it also

[
154
]
pursue the same goals.

It is worth mentioning that the quantum challenges had led, quite a
while ago, to attempts to revise even the formalizing the logic of
our thinking
[
21
,
166
]
—a very nice mathematical
theory dating back to von Neuman in the 1930’s

; termed quantum logic

.
There are handbooks on that subject

.

The lack of transparent motivations for mathematics—a pressing
requirement of physics—means that
QM
-formalism is hard to
distinguish from a “cook book of procedures and rituals” (J. Nash),
a “user-manual”
[

, p. 1690]
,
[

]
,

[

, p. xiii]
, or from “a library of … tricks and
intuitions”
[

]
. Therefore the “dissatisfaction regarding
comprehension” and the “need for interpretation that is alien to an
exact science”
[
198
, pp. 7–8]
lead to the fact that “we
admit, be it willingly or not, that quantum mechanics is not a
physical theory but a mathematical model”
[
162
, p. 1701]

or that “nature imitates a mathematical scheme”
[
87
, p. 347;
Heisenberg]
. De facto,
QT
“is in a sense like a
traditional herbal medicine used by “witch doctors”. We don’t

really
understand what is happening” (J. Nash) and “we have
essentially
no

grasp on why the theory takes the

[

, which raises
the suspicion that “something is wrong with the theory” (H. Putnam)
and that “this quantum skyscraper is built on very shaky ground”

[

.

At the same time, well-founded opinions have long been known to the

,

.

1.2.
Formula of superposition

Contrariwise, the “dominant role of mathematics in constructing
quantum mechanics” has lead to that mathematical “assumptions are
usually considered to be physical”
[
162
, p. 1691]
. That is
to say, “there has been a substitution of concepts”

[
199
, p. 295]
and “one of the consequences of quantum
revolution was the replacement of explanations of physical phenomena
by their mathematical description”
[
199
, p. 296]
. These
characteristics convey, in the best possible way, the
dissatisfaction with the fact that quantum physics “was actually
reduced to a physical interpretation of the Hilbert space theory”

[
162
, p. 1690]
. The
{\mathbb{H}}
-space in itself is a fairly
cumbersome mathematical structure, and even determines a crucial
principle—superposition of states
[
44
]
. It is thus not
surprising that this principle becomes “one of the vague points … the [Dirac] argument is difficult to consider as rational … the
physical principle simply fits underneath it” (excerpt from the
preface to the Russian edition of
[
157
]
).

Mathematics of the
{\mathbb{H}}
-space contains three constituents: a
vector space, the inner-product add-on over it, and topology. The
two latter ones invoke the first one which is completely independent
(algebra) and begins with the formula

(
1
)

|\boldsymbol{\psi}\rangle=\mathfrak{a}\cdot|\boldsymbol{\varphi}\rangle+\mathfrak{b}\cdot|\boldsymbol{\chi}\rangle\;.

This is the pivotal expression of quantum theory. Comprehending its
genesis is tantamount to comprehending the nature of the

linearity
of
QM
.

In (
1
), there occur the complex numbers
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}\in{\mathbb{C}}
, symbols of operations
\cdot
and
+
, and also
vectors
|\boldsymbol{\psi}\rangle
,
|\boldsymbol{\varphi}\rangle
,
|\boldsymbol{\chi}\rangle\in{\mathbb{H}}
. It is clear
that until an empirical basis for all these devices is found, the
interpretation of abstraction (
1
) and questions of the kind
“Quantum States: What the Hell Are They?” (55 times in

[
64
]
) will remain a problem. To all appearances, the
problem is considered so difficult—“quantum states … cannot
be ‘found out”’
[
148
, p. 428]
—that the non-axiomatic
meaning of these symbols was not even discussed in the literature.
In the meantime, not only is the situation far from hopeless, but it
also admits a solution. The present work is devoted to gradual
progress towards an understanding of formula (
1
). Stated
differently, equality (
1
) becomes an empirical ‘theorem’
(p.
•
‣
8.3
).

•

The main part of the challenge is not only to ascertain what is
being added/multiplied in (
1
), but also to realize primarily

what ‘to add/multiply’ is
, and “Where Mathematics Comes
from”
[
102
]
at all.

“What does it mean, physically, to “add” things?”
[
63
, p. 178;
D. Darling]
. More than that, aside from the symbols

\{\mathfrak{a}
,
\mathfrak{b}
,
|\boldsymbol{\psi}\rangle
,
|\boldsymbol{\varphi}\rangle
,
|\boldsymbol{\chi}\rangle
,

\cdot\mkern 1.0mu
,
+\}
the expression (
1
) contains the sign of
equality
=
(see also
[
11
, pp. 29, 30(!)]
,
[
120
]
)
and, surprising as it may seem, it conceals one of the key
points—the 3
-
rd principium of quantum theory
(
III
, p.
III
).

The guiding observation is based on the fact that the only thing
that we have access to are the microscopic events, and therefore “we
have little to begin with other than what an experimental physicist
would call experiments with a single microsystem”

[
109
, p. 5]
.

“[W]e must recognize that the focusing on individual elements
whatever these may be is absolutely indispensable for all our
thinking. … What may be regarded as an individual event?”

.
R. Haag
[
74
, p. 302]

Consequently, we must begin from individual events and from
collecting them into ensemble formations. It is precisely in this
context that we will use the word empiricism—quantum
empiricism of micro-acts of perception—and it is in this respect
that
QT
has a statistical nature. As Einstein had put it, “It may
be a correct theory of statistical laws, but an inadequate
conception of individual elementary processes”
[
85
, p. 156;
Einstein]
; see also
[
47
, pp. 38–40]
,

[
85
, Chs. 7–8]
,
[
47
, p. 40]
,
[
131
,
165
]
. Such a viewpoint has been long championed by L. Ballentine

[
12
]
and H. Groenewold
[
88
, p. 468]
and
justified in detail by G. Ludwig
[
109
,
110
,
111
,
112
]
. A. Leggett proposes accordingly the “extreme statistical
interpretation”
[
105
]
,
[
154
, p. 79]
in the sense
that “to seek any further “meaning” in the formalism is pointless
and can only generate pseudoquestions”. With that, he overtly
applies such characteristics as “complete gibberish”

[
105
, p. 70]
and “verbal window dressing”

[
154
, p. 79]
.

The difficulty is, of course, in creating the object
|\boldsymbol{\psi}\rangle

itself. A step-by-step characterization of this procedure
(sects.
3
–
8
) and key words to what follows
have been reflected in the (sub)section titles listed in the
Contents.

1.3.
Physics
\rightleftarrows
mathematics.
Doctrine of numbers

Thus the situation appears to be one whereby the physics itself
faces inconsistencies in its foundations and the mathematical
superstructures are difficult to reconcile with its motivations
(physical principles)
[
22
]
. But, on the other hand,
attempts to axiomatize an interface between them
[
77
]
only
conceal a deeper insight
[
189
]
. M. Born had called
attention to the fact that “probable refinements of mathematical
methods will not suffice to produce a satisfactory theory, but that
somewhere in our doctrine is hidden

a concept” and
T. Maudlin was more definite: “physicists have been misled by the
mathematical language they use to represent the physical world”.

In other words, we observe an overemphasis on the role of the
ready-made math-structures—algebras, spaces, and the like—and an
under-evaluation of ‘seemingly naïve’ empirical aspects voiced
in the ordinary language
[
82
]
. The situation is no
different from that which H. Weil had characterized in the
introductory section to
[
185
, p. 10]
as follows.

“All beginnings are obscure. Inasmuch as the mathematician operates
with his conceptions along strict and formal lines, he, above all,
must be reminded from time to time that the origins of things lie in
greater depths than those to which his methods enable him to
descend”.

The “origins” are expressible of course only in the natural
language; sect.
2
is devoted to this.

What we propose below is an implementation of the idea that the
postulational view must be abandoned and replaced by a negation of
the prior existence of both the physical “preconceived notions”

[
110
, p. 328]
and the mathematical structures. Physics and
mathematics should be created ‘from scratch’. Paul Benioff calls
this idea “a coherent theory of physics and mathematics”

[
22
, p. 639]
,
[
63
, p. 33; P. Benioff]
. Then, due
to the initial absence of mathematics, the introducing of
mathematical structures is almost ruled out, proofs must get
replaced by an empirical inference, and semantics of physics—the
language of physical reasoning—is initially under a linguistic
ban. It cannot exist a priori. That is to say, even the
natural-language conjunction of mathematical terms with physical
adjectives (and verbs
[
79
, p. 3102; “to happen, to be, to
exist”]
) becomes far from being free, as with the classical
description’s language (sects.
2.1
,
5.4
,
6.4
,

6.5
). R. Haag, on 1
-
st page of the work
[
73
]
,
emphasizes:

•

“we

should not consider [“vocabulary of Quantum
Theory”] as sacrosanct. … every word in the vocabulary is
subject to criticism”.

Returning to the ensemble formations, it is only they that have to
come to the fore, and argumentation should be subordinated only to
the low-level microscopic empiricism. Predominance of the empirical
over the theoretical will then immediately touch on the closest
creature of the latter—the notion of a number—since numbers do
not come ‘from the sky’, and the theory will have to be a
quantitative one.

Despite the overflow of abstracta in
QT
, the
doctrine of
number
—
\lceil
number
\>\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}\>
unit
\rceil
, to be precise
(sects.
7
and
9
)—has, it seems, not yet
entered foundational discussions
[
72
]
. Consequently, the
numbers turn into a kind of ‘problem of numbers’
(principium
II
), and we are thus led
to the necessity of revising the take on the foundations themselves:

\lceil\text{quantum fundamentals}\rceil\quad\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}\quad\lceil\text{the problem/\hskip 0.0ptdoctrine of the number}\rceil\;.

This paradigm shift is a unique trait of the quantal (not the
classical) view of things and a substantial part of the following is
devoted specifically to that.

In the outline of the present work, the workflow will constitute
re-creating the structure of a linear vector space. More precisely,
the producing an
a priori unknown
mathematics, which

will be found to be
an algebra of such a space with a complex
conjugation. As a matter of fact, we provide an answer to Haag’s
question “How do we translate the description of an experimental
arrangement into mathematical symbols?” in the context of his own
“idea of basing the interpretation of quantum theory on the concept
of “events” which may be considered as facts independent of the
consciousness of an observer”
[
74
, p. 295]
.

The main point to be immediately emphasized is that the
mathematization of the discrete micro-acts of observations is quite
a nontrivial procedure (
112
), and the further strategy,
along with the structure of this article, can be schematized as
follows.

(sect.
2
)

\framebox[256.0748pt]{$\begin{array}[]{c}\text{natural language, prolegomena to the quantum}\\
\text{(no math and ontology here)}\end{array}$}

\downarrow\qquad\qquad\downarrow

(sects.
3
–
4
)

\hskip-56.9055pt\framebox[256.0748pt]{$\begin{array}[]{c}\text{accumulation of micro-events,}\\
\text{low-level quantum empiricism, ensemble mixtures}\end{array}$}\hskip-56.9055pt

\downarrow\qquad\qquad\downarrow

(sect.
5
)

\framebox[256.0748pt]{$\begin{array}[]{c}\text{mathematization of ensembles' empiricism}\\
\text{(how the math comes about)}\end{array}$}

\downarrow\qquad\qquad\downarrow

(sect.
6
)

\framebox[256.0748pt]{$\begin{array}[]{c}\text{quantum superposition and {QM}-linearity}\\
\text{(no physical concepts and numbers here)}\end{array}$}

\downarrow\qquad\qquad\downarrow

(sect.
7
)

\framebox[256.0748pt]{$\begin{array}[]{c}\text{algebraic structure `the numbers ${\mathbb{R}}$ and ${\mathbb{C}}$',}\\
\text{binary and unary operations}\end{array}$}

\downarrow\qquad\qquad\downarrow

(sect.
8
)

\framebox[256.0748pt]{$\begin{array}[]{c}\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptquantum states\penalty$\rceil$ $=$
$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptlinear vector space\penalty$\rceil$}\\
\text{formula
$|\boldsymbol{\psi}\rangle=\mathfrak{a}\cdot|\boldsymbol{\varphi}\rangle+\mathfrak{b}\cdot|\boldsymbol{\chi}\rangle$}\end{array}$}

\downarrow\qquad\qquad\downarrow

(sects.
9
–
10
)

\hskip-56.9055pt\framebox[256.0748pt]{$\begin{array}[]{c}\text{naturalness and inevitability of abstracta,}\\
\text{observable quantities and their values,}\\
\text{quantum statistics and Born's rule \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{br2}{}{}]}}\\
\text{(\emph{no} interpretations here)}\end{array}$}\hskip-56.9055pt

This box-diagram cannot be reduced or restructured. For
example,

•

superposition foregoes numbers
, and measurement and
physical properties follow
strictly after
the

|\boldsymbol{}\text{ ket}\rangle
-vectors have been created.

By and large, the aforesaid ideology is supported by the common
belief—often certainty even
[
50
]
—that
QM
is

not
perturbative, its linearity is
not
associated with
linear approximation of something else, and, in general, it is

not
extensible (ultimate
[
38
]
and non-deformable)
and must be free of interpretations
[
50
,
65
]
. All of
these concerns, in one way or another, are directly related to the
derivation of formula (
1
).

2.
Points of departure

In the Beginning was the Word

— A. Zeilinger

[
190
,
01{:}05^{\prime}47^{\prime\prime}
]

Most of the time the apparatus is empty and

sometimes you have a photon coming through

— A. Zeilinger

[
190
,
12^{\prime}39^{\prime\prime}
]

Since empiricism is in essence supra-mathematical
[
32
]
, i. e.,
it is concerned with
meta
mathematics
[
96
,
142
]
,
its mathematization—theory construction—should begin not
with postulates and definitions, but rather with the semantic
formation of an object language (of ‘the Quanta’), of vocabulary,
and “may only be described by “words” and not by a theory”

[
109
, p. 106]
,
[
111
,
113
]
. As A. Peterson and
K. Popper had observed, “Math can never be used in phys until have
words”
[
19
, p. 209]
, i. e., “we cannot construct theories
without using words”
[
139
, p. 12]
. Therefore, relying on the
established understanding of the underlying causes for the quantum
eye on physics
[
131
,
9
,
66
,
70
]
, up
until the end of this section we will adopt the natural-language
meaning of the words observation, system, state, numbers (!), plus
and to divide, physical influence, transition, large/small,
micro/macro, etc. Their contents will later be defined more
precisely or entirely changed. For instance, the sense of the word
‘state’ will be drastically transformed, to which we are drawing
attention in advance. Accordingly, a degree of informality—it
has been clarified in Remark
10
—is inevitable here, but
“the lack of precision … is a necessity”
[
81
, p. 48]

at the moment.

2.1.
Variations as micro-level transitions

We will (and ‘must’
[
96
, Ch. 3]
) first view the concept of
a system at an intuitive level
[
46
, sect. 1.1]
—there is
what is referred to as an isolated system
\mathcal{S}
.

S

Let us tentatively (a priori) relate
the concept of a
state
to the associated context describable
by the words ‘the system
\mathcal{S}
can
vary
,
be
different
or
in different states
’. That is to say, system

\mathcal{S}
is always in a certain state
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
belonging to the
set
{\mathfrak{T}}=\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},\ldots\}
, each element of which is
admissible for
\mathcal{S}
, and all of them are different from each
other:
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\neq{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
.

In other words, the concept of a quantum system may not have a
precise/axiomatical definition at the moment. Or, if it comes to
that, the system is what’s being constantly varied when observed,
and “varied” is the key word here.

The statement “states are different” does not require a
consideration when
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
and
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
, referred to as
state, are the abstract elements of an abstract set

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},\ldots\}
. However, in order to tie its elements
to reality, we have to introduce the criteria of
coincidence/distinguishability of one from the other. Criteria may
not come otherwise than from observational procedures, without which
it is impossible to either detect states, or claim that they differ,
coincide, or that they are, if any.

On the other hand, the nature of micro-phenomena shows that
observations are always associated with irreducible intervention in
the system, manifesting in what is known as
transition

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\rightsquigarrow{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}
(or destruction). As an
example, observations at accelerators are literally the
destructions, and bulk at that. Due to a lack of criteria, there is
no sense in attributing to this concept the adjectives small/large,
(in)significant/partial, or collocations such as ‘comparison of
destructions at instants
t_{1}
,
t_{2}
’. Let us proceed from the idea
that initially there is nothing but the transition. Transitions may
actually occur without destructions

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\rightsquigarrow{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
, however.

Two different
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
may be destroyed into new

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}
,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}
, as well as into the combinations of the
old/new. Thus, strictly speaking, the sense of words ‘different,
new, …’ eludes us in this case, which is why even the
identification of
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-elements and the
{\mathfrak{T}}
itself, as a
set, becomes questionable. Therefore, besides the formal writings

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}={\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
and
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\neq{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
for

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\in{\mathfrak{T}}
, the
physical

distinguishability/equivalence (recognizability

{\not\approx}{/}{\approx}
) needs to be established. As to the
identification (and to the identity) in this regard, see
von Neumann’s reasoning “
One might object against

II

…” on page 302 of his book
[
131
]
. The sole thing that
distinguishability may rely on is the transition acts. In turn,
variation is a key element in transitions, which is why we will
begin constructing with distinguishability.

Let us take the still virtually unlimited way
\mathscr{A}
of
intervening
\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to8.33339pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}
in
\mathcal{S}
and attempt to
introduce distinguishability
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\not\approx{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
as

\mathscr{A}
-distinguishability. Due to the fact that micro-transition

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to8.33339pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}
is not pre-determined,
initial states
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
undergo arbitrarily free changes. Next
time, the results will be different and absolutely arbitrary (the
term “different” is understood to mean
\neq
), and each act is
indiscernible from a case in which it contains ones similar to
itself within itself. It would be natural to associate such a case
to the absurd, which is unrelated to the meaning of the words
‘physical observation’, and to discard the given
\mathscr{A}
.

Non-meaninglessness arises only if we impose the negation of random
combinations of
\neq
and
=
in transitions, at least for a part of

{\mathfrak{T}}
, i. e., introduce the preservation acts

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to8.33339pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
. The ‘preservation’ should
be read here as indestructibility of state, i. e., as a
(
=
)-coincidence under the secondary act
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\rightsquigarrow{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\rightsquigarrow{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
. Otherwise,
the vanishing difference between ‘preservation’ and ‘variation’
leads to a linguistic chaos
[
59
, p. 232]
. This means that
the destruction
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\rightsquigarrow{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}
may not be
considered as a 1-fold one. State
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}
on the right should
be examined for changeability and transform into the left part of
the subsequent transition:
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\rightsquigarrow{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}
. Thereby the structure
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}
with the
binate
entity
‘before/after’ or ‘on the left/right’ becomes the key one, and we
consider it an initial object in subsequent constructs. The
preserved states are, by definition, those that pass the
reproducibility test.

Thus logic requires to begin with the transition compositions

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}\cdots\;,

wherein the cases like

(
2
)

\cdots\quad{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\quad\cdots

are ruled out (a ban on changing of what has been unchanged), and
the never-ending sequence

(
3
)

\displaystyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}\cdots\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}\cdots

(non-recognisability of states) must be terminated

(
4
)

\displaystyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}\cdots\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\;,

yielding a ‘finiteness’ (
=
realisticness) and the concept of
conserved/distinctive
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-states. The terminology

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-event
[
50
]
could be used instead.

Freedom of elements in sequence (
4
), including the choice of

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-states, is not limited by anything besides the ban
on (
2
). Therefore this arbitrariness, which is physically
never recognizable, curtails the generic chain (
4
) into the
shortened one

(
5
)

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}\boxed{\cdots\cdots}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\;,

which is identical to the scheme

(
6
)

\boxed{\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}

with certain
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\in{\mathfrak{T}}
.

Discussions on “what happens … [and] “how””

[
131
, p. 217]
at the very microscopic level are extremely
widespread in the literature
[
69
,
7
,
91
,
108
,
114
,
126
,
198
]
(see
[
6
,
9
,
150
,
33
]
for the exhaustive references), although it is not
difficult to predict the fact that the attempts to understand the
inner structure of box (
6
) will only lead back to an
identical box; so, the “turtles all the way down” (ascribed to
W. James), followed by the great Wheeler’s slogan “No tower of
turtles” (1989).

Indeed, the uncontrollability of micro-changes is universally known,
yet describing them as a process in time
t\mapsto t+\varepsilon
will
start employing the language terminology—functions, arithmetic
operations, the physical words, etc—that has not yet been created
even for the fixed instants
t_{1}
,
t_{2}
. However, what may be
associated with fixed time are only non-temporal entities, for which
we have nothing but transitions (
5
). The attempt to manage
them, i. e., to control intervention in
\mathcal{S}
, results in looping
or ‘measuring the measurement’, let alone the ambiguity of this term
itself.

“[I]t is not meaningful to speak of a measurement “at time

\tilde{t}
.” … the real physical meaning of the time parameter
… has nothing to do with the notion “time of measurement.””.
“[T]he description of the measurement process in quantum mechanics
in terms of “pre-theories” is not possible”

.
G. Ludwig
[
109
, p. 288]
,

[
110
, p. 340]

See also
[
110
, p. 365]
,
[
112
, p. 150]
,

[
113
, p. 100]
,
[
134
, pp. 644–646]
, and

[
141
]
. Just as before, the physical assessments such
as‘abrupt’, ‘(ir)reversible’, ’(non)simultaneous’, “immediately
following …”
[
131
, pp. 231, 410]
, or the
‘weak/nondemolishing’ (measurements
[
114
]
), etc are
unacceptable here. No temporal process may be present in the
foundations of the theory
[
109
, sects. VII.4, 6]
,

[
110
, Chs. III, XVII]
,
[
111
]
, since it is
immediately not clear ‘And what exactly are we having at instants

t_{1}
or
t_{2}
?’. In the reverse direction—
\lceil
time
\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}

measurement
\rceil
—the situation is also rather indefinite since the
““Time” is not an entity to which the operations of measurement,
direct or indirect, apply”
[
141
, p. 5]
.

Remark
1
.

All said above means that attempts to deduce
QM
dynamically

[
154
, 10
\boldsymbol{\cdot}
Reconstructions]
are beforehand
doomed to vicious circles ‘round the boxes’ and time
t
like
attempts to ‘vindicate’ dynamically Lorenz’s contraction instead of
kinematic postulates of the relativity theory
[
31
]
. Consistent
theory must rest either on ‘irreducible’ elements (
6
) or
upon ‘boxes’ of a different kind. In the latter case, the theory
becomes a particular
model with interpretation
; e. g., the
Lindblad equations
[
183
,
184
]
, decoherence

[
90
,
194
,
150
,
152
]
, stochastic dynamics, and
other statistic-dynamical models
[
6
,
198
]
. Anyway, an
ability to model and understanding are not the same thing, and this
point was repeatedly emphasized in the literature

[
3
, sect. I.2]
,
[
162
,
154
]
with regard to

QT
.

That said, if theory is built
as a fundamental one, rather
than as a model

[
154
, p. 144]
, with a primary entity

changeability

\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to8.33339pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}
, the box (
6
)
may only be involved in it as the initial starting point and as an

indescribable

object with the absolute rather than
with a relative sense. Elements of reality, in whatever
understanding—say, Bell’s “
be
ables”
[
20
]
—may
not exist before/after/inside/outside of the box. It can be only the

structureless abstractio
. Accordingly, the notions of
preparation, of measurement, of ‘interaction with’, and of a
physical process are meaningless without construction (
6
).

These statements are clearly in agreement with the fact that any
reasoning must not contradict the formal logical rules

[
96
]
, hence, there must exist
[
142
,
159
,
22
]
the empirically undefinable logical atoms. A. Peres writes

[
135
, p. 173]
: “While quantum theory can in principle
describe
anything
, a quantum description cannot include

everything
. In every physical situation
something
must
remain unanalyzed”. Or as Pauli had put it, “Like the ultimate fact
without any cause, the individual outcome of a measurement is … not comprehended by laws”. Specifically, the set
{\mathfrak{T}}
and
transitions-arrows
\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to8.33339pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}
are also the atoms. “… preexisting concept … We cannot formulate the theory without this
concept”, concludes B. Englert
[
50
, p. 2]
. From the
aforesaid, we may formulate the following tenet.

I

Quantum statics should
forego quantum dynamics.

.
(
The
\boldsymbol{1}
-st principium of quantum theory
)

The rationales do not end here, and will be later amplified once we
begin to exploit the terminology that is usually taken for granted
from the outset; viz, the quantitative descriptions

[
63
, p. 178]
. If they arise not as numerical
interpretations of something, but out of an experiment, then
observation should be the beginning, and the ‘manufacture of
numbers’—the end. In other words, the model ‘theory with boxes’
other than (
5
)–(
6
) implicitly implies the logical
sequence
\lceil
model of process
\rceil

\rightarrowtail

\lceil
numerical interpretation
\rceil
, in which empiricism holds a role
other than primary. It is clear that, regardless of the model, such
a situation will always remain unsatisfactory in the physical
respect.

2.2.
Observation

The sequences addressed above lead to the following outcome.

•

Any meaningful micro-act
\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to8.33339pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}
either saves a
state (
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to8.33339pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
) or turns
it into a conserved one (
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to8.33339pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
).

The two extremes do not contradict this fact. The
first—maximally rough observations—is when all states are
destroyed into a certain one:
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\rightsquigarrow{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{0}
(‘whatever and however we watch, all we see is one and
the same’). In this, the state
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{0}
is not destroyed:

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{0}\rightsquigarrow{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{0}
. Another extreme is when
none of the states are destroyed:
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\rightsquigarrow{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
. This is the case of ideal (quantum) observation, but,
due to the absence of any changes, it is indistinguishable from the
case of if observations are entirely absent.

Situated in between these extremes lies a simplest case with two
distinctive states

(
7
)

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\;.

Of course, these are prohibited from transitioning into each other.
Because there is still the free admissibility of transitions

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to8.33339pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}
,

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to8.33339pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}
, we can turn the
semantic sequence

\big\lceil\text{arbitrariness}\quad\rightarrowtail\quad\text{preservation}\quad\rightarrowtail\quad\text{distinctive ${\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$'s}\big\rceil

into the more rigorous scheme

(
8
)

\big\lceil{\mathfrak{T}}=\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu,\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu,\mkern 1.0mu\ldots\}\big\rceil\;\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{+}}}\;\big\lceil\mathscr{A}\text{-observations}\big\rceil\quad\rightarrowtail\quad\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu,{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\mkern 1.0mu,\ldots\}=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\subset{\mathfrak{T}}\;,

which gives, even though partially, rise to the concept of a
physical distinguishability (‘distinguo’). It is formally defined
only on the subset
{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
: the statement

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\not\approx{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}
is equivalent
to (
7
). To avoid overloading the further notation, we do
not use symbols like
\approx_{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
and

\not\approx_{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
; the context is always obvious.

O

By a
physical observation

\mathscr{A}
or, in short,
observation
we will mean such
interventions
\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.90286pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}
, in which the ‘never-ending’
chaos (
3
) is replaced by chaos with the notion of
preservation, i. e., ‘chaos with rule (
6
)’:

(
9
)

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.90282pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu,\quad\text{where\penalty\kern 2.77802pt\ignorespaces\penalty\hskip 0.0pt}\quad{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.90282pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\;.

The set of
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-objects
{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
with the
property

(
10
)

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.90282pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.90282pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\ldots

is discrete, and the
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}
themselves are termed

the eigen
(proper)
for observation
\mathscr{A}
. They
defines
\mathscr{A}
and do not depend on
\mathcal{S}
. No logical
connection between
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
(the left of (
9
)), family

{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
, and system
\mathcal{S}
exists.

(The comprehensive terminology here is this: micro-act of
observation by instrument
\mathscr{A}
. The zig-zag arrow

\smash{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to8.33339pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\hfil}}}
is replaced with the straight one
\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}
.)
Expressed another way, introduction of the conception ‘the eigen’ is
equivalent to the following informal, yet minimal motivation:

at least some
certainty instead of
total

arbitrariness.

Two instruments
\mathscr{A}
and
\mathscr{B}
may have arbitrarily different
eigen-states
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}
, …,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{n}\}

\neq

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1},\ldots,{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{m}\}
. Accordingly, as regards
observation
\mathscr{B}
, the (distinctive) states
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}\}

do not differ, in general, from the ‘regular’
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
’s, i. e.,
from those chaotically destroyable into the
\mathscr{B}
-eigen states:

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{j}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.67369pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{k}
. All kinds of
instruments {
\mathscr{A}
,
\mathscr{B}
, …} are thus defined by
aggregates {
{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
,
{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}
, …}. The
number
|{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}|
of corresponding

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-objects therein may be an arbitrary integer. There
are also no (logical) grounds for restricting/prescribing the
composition of
{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
. Any element of
{\mathfrak{T}}
may be
the conserved one for a certain instrument. Parenthetically, the
notion of an eigen-state—in different forms—is sometimes present
in axiomatics of
QM

[
137
,
21
,
126
]
.

In a generic case, the chaos present in (
9
) leaves open the
problem of correlating the recognizability

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\not\approx{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
(or
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\approx{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
)
with physics. Clearly, the issue is linked to the ambiguity of the
term
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-state itself, which is used in
pt.
S
—an important point—due to the
need to start with something, since building the mathematical
description without some sort of a set is impossible.

Remark
2
.

Informally, metalinguistic semantics—association of meanings
with texts
[
113
]
—is in general as follows. Inasmuch as we
are receiving
different

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-responses to each
micro-act
\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to3.125pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 3.58795pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}
, let us say that ‘on the other side
from us there is something that can also be different’, and all of
that is to be described. This reflects our intuitive perception of
reality, which, both at the micro- and the macro-level, boils down
to pt.
S
and to an ineradicable pair:

\lceil
something outside
\rceil

\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{+}}}

\lceil
that which can be
different for us
\rceil
. If we give up either of these semantic
premises—“something outside” (
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
) or “can be different”
(
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{j}
)—then, as above, we face a linguistic dead end,
as the possibility for reasoning disappears. There must be two sides
present. Because of this, the arrow
\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to3.125pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 3.58795pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}
must be
accompanied by ‘some things’ to the left and the right of it. The
low-level set
{\mathfrak{T}}=\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
, …,

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}
,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}
, …} does arise. Then the
arbitrary elements
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\in{\mathfrak{T}}
(unrestricted chaos) are
assigned to the left of this act instead of ‘some thing’ and the
micro
\not\approx
-distinguishable
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-objects
(
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{j}\not\approx{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}
) to the right. Put
another way,

•

what’s being abstracted is not “concrete things”

[
66
, p. 27]
or “behaviour of things”

[
25
, p. 414]
but a primitive element of perception—a
micro-event—the
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-click. Other than ‘the click’, no
entities, such as very small objects/particles, fields, or, much
less the knowledge, human psychology, “personal judgments”, “memory
configuration”
[
52
,
53
]
, “mysterious interaction
… brain of the observer”
[
134
, p. 645]
,
[
139
, p. 11,
thesis 3]
, agents, their belief/consciousness
[
121
,
176
,
168
]
, etc, may exist in empiricism. This is a kind of
“
Radical Empiricism
… [by] William James”
[
64
, pp. 289,
…]
. The “click … and nothing more”
[
154
, p. 42;
Č. Brukner]
is a kind of experimental the
zero-principium of
QT
. Therefore the initial math-premises of
QT
should contain nothing but the

\not\approx_{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
-distinguishability and formalization
(
9
)–(
10
).

Ideas of “a click (signal) in a counter”
[
178
, p. 758]

have, time and again, already been expressed in the literature

[
25
]
,
[
63
, A. Peres]
, and we draw attention to
answers of Č. Brukner on pp. 41–43 in
[
154
]
, his work

[
30
, p. 98]
, and page 635 in
[
189
]
. “Having
grown up collecting clicks … I would start with “clicks” as the
only point of contact between observer and observed”, wrote
J. Summhammer in
[
64
, p. 261]
. It may be added that the
micro-observation, as such, is terminated at the eigen elements; one
and the same
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{j}
has always remained on the right.

As a result, the minimal entity
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.90286pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{j}
constitutes, mathematically, an ordered pair

({\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{j})
of elements of the set
{\mathfrak{T}}
, which
are labeled by the symbol
\mathscr{A}
that is equivalent to the

{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
-family (
8
). Accordingly, the
customary physical notion of the observation is substituted for a
micro-event, an act. ‘Physics should forget’ about processes or time
of interaction when observing, about the interaction itself, and
about anything but
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.90286pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
. This
object represents a completed formalization of the
empirical/laboratory notion of a quantum micro-event—a detector
click. The click is sometimes considered from the information
viewpoint as an information bit
[
189
]
. However it cannot
be such a (classical) bit with a reified content because it is
completely unpredictable. The next (different) click does entirely
negate the previous one, and the information bit is in turn a
concrete thing—the bit. For the same reason, there cannot be any
information behind the single micro-event. It is ‘too small and too
momentary’ to possess or to carry information about something
inasmuch as even the ‘something’ is composed from elementary clicks;
see below.

2.3.
Numerical realizations

Is there a possibility to rely exclusively on the inflexibility of
the eigen-type elements (
10
)? Or to define the sought-for
ultimate distinguishability
\not\approx
through the

\mathscr{A}
-(micro)distinguishabilities

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{j}\not\approx_{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}
? Let us
formulate a thesis.

T

There is no (linguistic) means of
recognizing the system
\mathcal{S}
to be different
(pt.
S
), other than through the results of
its destructions into the
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}
,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}
,
…}-objects of observational instruments
\mathscr{A}
.

Granted, the stringency of this linguistic taboo
(
T
) must be accompanied by something
constructive, and we will adopt the following programme, which
reflects the fact that the unequivocal description may only take the
form of a quantitative mathematical theory.

R
\phantom{\bullet}\bullet

Out of the primary (‘proto’)elements

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},\ldots\}\in{\mathfrak{T}}
, one constructs a new set

{\mathbb{H}}
, of which the elements

(
11
)

|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}={\boldsymbol{\mathbin{\mathchar 8712}}}(\mathfrak{a}_{1},|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}\rangle;\mathfrak{a}_{2},|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{2}\rangle;\ldots)\in{\mathbb{H}}

are said to be (number)
representations
in the ‘reference
frame for instrument
\mathscr{A}
’, and
\mathfrak{a}_{s}
are the numerical
objects. The distinguishability relation

\not\approx_{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
is carried over to
{\mathbb{H}}
and admits
an
\mathfrak{a}
-coordinate realization there—symbol
\not\approx
.

\bullet\bullet

No preferential or preordained observational reference frame

\mathscr{A}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2},\ldots\}
—instrument the
absolute—exists.

Identification (
11
) is always tied to a certain family

{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
. Accordingly, images of

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}
—symbols
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{s}\rangle
—are present in
(
11
), and character
\boldsymbol{\mathbin{\mathchar 8712}}
is also no more than a
symbol here. Even though coordinates
\mathfrak{a}_{s}
are declared to be
numbers or aggregates of numbers, there is no arithmetic stipulated
for them yet. The number is as yet a name for
\mathfrak{a}_{s}
.
Distinguishability
|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\not\approx|\boldsymbol{}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}\rangle
of
two representatives

{\boldsymbol{\mathbin{\mathchar 8712}}}(\mathfrak{a}_{1},|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}\rangle;\mathfrak{a}_{2},|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{2}\rangle;\ldots)=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad{\boldsymbol{\mathbin{\mathchar 8712}}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{1},|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}\rangle;\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{2},|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{2}\rangle;\ldots)=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}|\boldsymbol{}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}\rangle

by means of numbers
\mathfrak{a}_{k}\neq\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{k}
and mathematical
implementation of (
11
) and of the
{\mathbb{H}}
-space, i. e., a
‘coordinatization’ scheme have yet to be established. This will
comprise the meaning of the word ‘constructs’
(sects.
7
–
8
), which may not be even
linked to the mathematical term mapping yet, since
no math of

QM
exists at the moment
. It immediately follows that the question
about number entities—specifically, about (
11
)—is
nontrivial in physics.

II

To speak of an exact
correspondence between experiment and mathematics
(
\lceil
observation
\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{+}}}
measurement
\rceil
) makes no sense until
there has been detailed a
mechanism for the
emergence
of what is understood by number.

.
(
The
\boldsymbol{2}
-nd principium of quantum
theory
)

In other words, we wonder what an empiricist/observer understands
(semantics) by the word (syntax) ‘number’. The underlying message
here implies that the reliance upon the all-too-familiar arithmetic
elucidates nothing.
There is no arithmetic in
interferometers/colliders
—there are only clicks there—and
empirical nature of the arising that construction (along with the
measurement) must be scrutinized.

From pts.
T
,
R
,
and
II
it also follows that the
search for a description through hidden variables, over which
something is averaged, is indistinguishable from the utopian
attempts to find out an intrinsic content of boxes (
5
).

2.4.
Macro and micro

The task becomes more precise at this point. Instead of nonphysical
identity/noncoincidence (
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}={\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
or

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\neq{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
) of two abstract elements
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
,

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
of the abstract set
{\mathfrak{T}}
, we need the concept of a
physical
\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\sim$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\sim$}\hss}\lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\sim$}}
-equivalence (
\not\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\sim$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\sim$}\hss}\lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\sim$}}
-distinguishability) of

{\mathbb{H}}
-representatives
\{|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle
,
|\boldsymbol{\Phi}\rangle
, …}. That is, there
must hold either relation
|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\sim$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\sim$}\hss}\lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\sim$}}|\boldsymbol{\Phi}\rangle
or its negation

|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\not\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\sim$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\sim$}\hss}\lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\sim$}}|\boldsymbol{\Phi}\rangle
for all
|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle,|\boldsymbol{\Phi}\rangle\in{\mathbb{H}}
. The
primitive set
{\mathfrak{T}}
, initially required by
point
S
, must disappear from the ultimate
mathematics of symbols
|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\in{\mathbb{H}}
. Therefore elements

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\in{\mathfrak{T}}
are henceforth named
primitives
.

Let us sum up the fallaciousness of a metaphysical belief in the
meaningfulness of the wording ‘there is a quantum state’, i. e., the
belief in that the existence of a state has some math-numerical
form.

•

There is no a priori way to endow the term (quantum) state of
system
\mathcal{S}
with any meaning
[
107
, p. 419]
. It may not
have a definition and any predefined semantics. This term should be
created. Meanwhile, one cannot get around the concept of the
(micro)observation
\mathscr{A}

[
30
, pp. 98–100]
,

[
134
, p. 646]
,
[
22
,
15
]
. Essentially, no
one thing, including
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
, or the
{\mathfrak{T}}
-set
itself, can be the primary bearer of data about
\mathcal{S}
.

“There is an entirely new idea involved, … in terms of which one
must proceed to build up an exact mathematical theory” (P. Dirac

[
44
, p. 12]
).

There is no escape from quoting K. Popper: “… language for the
theory; … it remains (like every language) to some extent vague
and ambiguous. It cannot be made “precise”: the meaning of
concepts cannot, essentially, be laid down by any definition,
whether formal, operational, or ostensive. Any attempt to make the
meaning of the conceptual system “precise” by way of definitions
must lead to an infinite regress, and to merely
apparent

precision, which is the worst form of imprecision because it is the
most deceptive form. (This holds even for pure mathematics.)”

[
139
, p. 13]
.

The notions of a physical observable and of its observable values
are also ambiguous at this point
[
109
, p. 5]
. Their
ambiguity is even greater than that of state due to questions such
as ‘what is being measured?’ and even ‘what is a measurement?’.
Nonetheless, up until the end of this section, we will not discard
the term state within the context of pt.
S
.

Irreproducibility of outcomes, i. e., the ‘turnability of

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-primitives into the various’ leaves only one option: ‘to
take a look at
\mathcal{S}
again, once again, …’. In other words, to
seek the source of description in repeatability. It is necessary,
then, to move to the subject of macro- rather than
micro-observation. This intention fits perfectly with the undefined
verb “constructs” in pt.
R
, and the
following paradigm should be understood as the macro.

M

The only way of handling the
uncontrollable micro-level changes is the treatment of the results
of repeated destructions, accompanied by what we shall call the
common physical macro-setting (experimental context):

(
12
)

\begin{array}[c]{@{}c@{}c@{}c@{}cc@{}c@{}cc@{}}&{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}&\;\;\cdots&{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}&{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}&\;\;\cdots&{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}&\cdots\cdots\\
\mathscr{A}&\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}&\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}&\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}&\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}&\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}&\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}&\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\;\mathscr{A}\\
&{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}&\cdots&{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}&{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}&\cdots&{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}&\cdots\cdots\end{array}\quad\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{+}}}\quad\lceil\text{common macro-environment }\mathbf{M}\mkern 1.0mu\rceil\;.

To be precise, we should have to (and we shall do) indicate the
different
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}
, …} here because the same
ingoing
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
’s in (
12
) is a preassumption—which
we eschew throughout the work. This point will be very fully
addressed further below (sects.
2.5
,
2.6
, and

3.1
).

The importance of repetitions and distinguishability had long been
noted (Bohr, von Neumann et al
[
87
]
) and, recently, it was
particularly emphasized in the work
[
195
]
. The words
‘copy/repeat…/distin…’ occur 90 times therein.

Thus empiricism of quantum statics forces us to operate exclusively
with such formations of copies
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
, …,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
, and
this is the maximum amount of data provided by the
supra-mathematical problem setup.
All
further mathematical
structures may come only from constructions like (
12
) and
from nothing else. Getting ahead of ourselves, let us once again
turn our attention to the fact that implementation of this idea

is not short-length
—“the mathematization process (cor) is
not simple”
[
113
, p. 24]
, and sects.
3
–
9

are devoted specifically to this; see, e. g., the
chain (
112
).

One can once more repeat (sect.
1.3
) that much of what
follows does not and cannot contain the mathematical definienda and
proofs as they are usually present in the literature on quantum
foundations. Instead, there appears the step-by-step

inference
of objects as they result themselves: numbers,
operations, groups, algebras, etc. The only instrument that may be
applicable here is the empirical inference.

The common macro-environment
\mathbf{M}
in (
12
) is also
viewed as a supra-mathematical notion
[
142
]
, the
mathematical implementation of which is yet to be created. The same
considerations regarding qualitative adjectives are applicable as to
the physical convention
\mathbf{M}
as well as the transition acts in
sect.
2.1
. Representations (
11
) will be the
formalization of the meaning
\lceil
observation
\rceil

\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{+}}}

\lceil
data on system
\mathcal{S}
\rceil
, but now with no references to the
elementary acts in (
12
). The physical distinguishability
criteria
|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\not\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\sim$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\sim$}\hss}\lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\sim$}}|\boldsymbol{\Phi}\rangle
may not be formulated yet
because the physical attributes are not yet available, but

|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{s}\rangle
-elements have already appeared in (
11
) as
prototypes of explicitly distinguishable
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}
.

Remark
3
.

Dual form of the typical quantum statements like ‘
\mathcal{S}
is a

micro
-system and
\mathscr{A}
-instrument is a

macro
-object’ (N. Bohr) is identical to the initial premise
‘observation does always destroy a system’. It follows that there is
actually little need for that terminology. Indeed,
QM
-micro has no
internal structure and, hence, an oft-discussed issue about boundary
(and limit

∗
⁣
∗

∗⁣∗\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\boldsymbol{{{*}{*}}}

∗
⁣
∗

∗⁣∗\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\boldsymbol{{{*}{*}}}

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\boldsymbol{{{*}{*}}}

According to A. Zeilinger, “… no limit. The
limit is only a question of … money and of experience”

[
190
,
13^{\prime}09^{\prime\prime}
]
.
) between micro and macro

[
148
,
85
,
12
,
9
]
is devoid of sense; “The
notions of ’microscopic’ and ’macroscopic’ defy precise definition”

[
20
, p. 215]
. Therefore this may be a matter only of
‘different macro’—the ‘smaller/bigger’; i. e., when they describe
certain models.

As a (partially philosophical) aside, we note that what is
understood by observational randomness does, in fact, boil down to
distinguishability, and more specifically, to postulating the
micro-chaos (
9
). In considering the denial of (
9
)
as an impossible proposition, we arrive at the

M
-paradigm and conclude that the only way
to deal with that which is contemplated for the subject-matter of a
physical description must be the treatment of micro-acts as
assemblages
[
125
, Lect. 6]
. In other words, and in accordance
with the outline of the clicks’ analysis set out below, the
determinism of micro-processes (micro-ontology)—much less the
microscopic time-arrow—is meaningless as a concept, since they are
not processes but rather structureless acts that have not even any
relationship to each other. Since there are no physical phenomena as
of yet, the claim that “phenomenon
-
1 appears to be the cause that
precedes phenomenon
-
2 as the effect” is no more than a collection
of words. To attribute physical content and mathematical formulation
at the micro-level to them is impossible in principle; the ‘problem
of boxes’ noted above. Accordingly, the cause of (classical)
macro-indeterminism is the absurdity of the notion of its twin
concept—micro-determinism—and the unavoidable repetition of
the arrows
\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}
(
M
). N. Bohr puts the
point very definitely: “there can be no question of causality in the
ordinary sense of the word”
[
87
, p. 351]
, and Heisenberg
adds that “l’indeterminismo, … è necessario, e non solo
consistentemente possibile”
[
3
, sect. IX.4]
. See
also
[
125
, p. 223]
.

2.5.
Quantum ensembles and statistics

Let us call the upper row in (
12
), as a collection of the

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-copies, (quantum) homogeneous
ensemble

(Kollektiv, by von Mises
[
125
]
). We will designate it,
simplifying when needed, by

\{\underbrace{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\>{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}}_{\text{$N$
times}}\}\equiv\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{N}\equiv\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{N}\;,

where
N
is understood to be an arbitrary large number. Scheme
(
12
) dictates also to consider the generic ensembles

(
13
)

\big\{\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\}_{n_{1}}\;\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\}_{n_{2}}\;\cdots\cdots\big\}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\{\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\>{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\>{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots\}

as collections of homogeneous sub-ensembles. Ensembles are
symbolized in the same manner as sets but, for typographical
convenience, without the numerous commas and internal parentheses

\{\}
in (
13
); for example,

\big\{a\mkern 1.0mub\cdots b\{b\mkern 1.0muc\mkern 1.0mua\}\cdots\big\}=\{a,b,\ldots,b,b,c,a,\ldots\}=\quad\cdots\quad=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}\{ab\cdots bbca\cdots\}\;.

Scheme (
12
) is the first point in which numbers emerge in
theory, and conversion

\lceil{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\text{-ensemble
\eqref{mix}}\rceil\quad\rightarrowtail\quad(n_{1}\mkern 1.0mu,n_{2}\mkern 1.0mu,\ldots)

into the integer collection anticipates a
numerical

\mathscr{A}
-measurement of

\mathcal{S}
. Quantities
n_{s}\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
,
however, should not be associated with such, as they are potentially
infinite. The minimal way of creating the knowingly finite numbers
out of independent and potential infinities
n_{s}
(without loss of
their independence) is to divide each of them by a greater infinity,
which is a ‘constant’
\Sigma
for the entire ensemble (
13
).
It is clear that one should put

(
14
)

\Sigma\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=n_{1}+n_{2}+\cdots\quad\text{and}\quad\Big\{\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{1}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{n_{1}}{\Sigma}\mkern 1.0mu,\quad\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{2}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{n_{2}}{\Sigma}\mkern 1.0mu,\quad\ldots\Big\}\quad(\Sigma\rightsquigarrow\infty)\;,

and that ensemble (
13
) does not provide any numerical data
besides the relative frequencies (
14
). All the other data are
functions of
\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{s}
. An independence of the theory from the
ensemble’s
\Sigma
-constant, i. e., the scheme

\Sigma\rightsquigarrow\infty
, is also implied to be a principle,
and it can be only the semantic one. Without it—the

\Sigma
-postulate of infinity—there can be no question of a
rational theory, i. e., empiricism will not turn into a mathematics
(sects.
5.1
–
5.2
). In turn, the concepts “closely,
limit, the limiting frequencies”, and the like will arise later when we
obtain the state of space as a Hilbert one
{\mathbb{H}}
and topology on it

[
28
]
.

Thus the
M
-paradigm (
12
) does not
only give birth to a concept of numerical data in the theory per se,
but also converts their
\mathbb{Z}^{+}
-discreteness into the

{\mathbb{R}}
-continuum of real measurements. Namely, numbers

\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{s}\in{\mathbb{R}}
are the statistics
(\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{1},\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{2},\ldots)
of
destructions
\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.90286pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}
into the ensemble of primitives

\{\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\}_{n_{1}}
,
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\}_{n_{2}}
, …}.

2.6.
Distinguishability and numbers

Distinguishability of the two ensembles now turns out to be the

{\mathbb{R}}
-numerical, i. e., it is determined by the difference between

\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}
-numbers. As a result, and according to
pt.
R
, the two elements

|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\not\approx_{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}|\boldsymbol{}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}\rangle
of

{\mathbb{H}}
will differ in the numbers
\mathfrak{a}_{s}
and

\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{s}
, if the latter turn out to be the bearers of
different statistics

(
15
)

\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{j}(\mathfrak{a}_{1},\mathfrak{a}_{2},\ldots)\neq{\tilde{\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}\mkern-1.0mu}{}_{j}(\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{2},\ldots)\;.

In consequence, distinguishability
\not\approx
is carried over to

{\mathbb{H}}
with an extension to the non-eigen objects, but it is
inherently incomplete, since it does not take into account the most
significant fact—arbitrariness of transitions (
6
).

The collection
(\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{1},\ldots)
, as a final result of transitions

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}\}
, actually ‘knows nothing’ about
their left hand side, much less about its uniqueness
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
.
For instance, if under the equal
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-statistics

\{\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{s}\}
for the two families

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\text{ ?}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}\alpha_{s}\}_{\scriptscriptstyle N}
and

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}\alpha_{s}\}_{\scriptscriptstyle N}
(collectivity of
