{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\text{ ?}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
’s), we would claim

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\text{ ?}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}={\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
, this would mean a mass control
over transitions (
9
). Instead of a ‘black box’ above, we
find that prior to acts
\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.90286pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}
all the undefined

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\text{ ?}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
’s were equal to
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
. This, however,
is the declaration of a property: ‘prior to observation the system

\mathcal{S}
was/dwelled in …’. With any continuation of this
sentence, it is pointless and prohibited if one theoretically
accepts that, prior to observation, nothing exists and there are no
properties (sect.
2.1
). The words “initial state of
\mathcal{S}
”
thus make no sense. The indeterminacy of the ingoing

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\text{ ?}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
’s is therefore mandatory, and numbers

(\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{1},\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{2},\ldots)
required for recognition are manifestly
insufficient. Considering that the micro-changeability of single
primitives
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
also means nothing
[
7
]
,

[
9
, p. 493]
,
[
107
, p. 419(!), left column]
, only
a generic ensemble

(
16
)

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\text{ ?}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\text{ ?}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}\quad\rightarrowtail\quad\{\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\>{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\>{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots\}=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}\mathfrak{A}

can be an intermediary in the sought-for translation of

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
’s onto representations
|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle\in{\mathbb{H}}
under
construction (
11
).

In the accustomed physical terminology, the above is expressed in
the sequence

(
17
)

\displaystyle\lceil\text{state}\rceil\;\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to23.41695pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 5.16663pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}_{\text{quant}}$}}\;

\displaystyle\;\lceil\text{state${}^{\prime}\rceil$}

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;\lceil\text{measurement}\rceil\;.

The removal of the intermediate component here, i. e.,
switch to the sequence

(
18
)

\displaystyle\lceil\text{state}\rceil\;\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to20.36133pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 5.16663pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}_{\text{class}}$}}\;

\displaystyle\kern 3.33344pt\cdots\cdots

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;\lceil\text{measurement}\rceil

amounts to the rejection of micro-destructibility and of
unpredictability. Even with the classical framework, this
supposition is questionable, since the notion of a ‘change when
observed’ disappears. The relationships between the dual
concepts—(micro/macro)-scopicity, big/middle/small, etc—do also
get lost. That is the reason why, developing Heisenberg’s asking the
question “… is it … I can only find in nature situations which
can be described by quantum mechanics?”
[
87
, p. 325]
, we
conclude that, strictly speaking,

•

all observations

, regardless of (the
envisioned physical) macro/meso/micro characteristics, do have the
structure (
17
), i. e., are quantum.
No non-quantal
observations exist
.

With their idealized ‘roughening’, the classical description appends
numerical
\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}
-statistics to (
18
), which is when the
left/right sides of (
18
) become indistinguishable with
respect to the arrow symbols. The arrows may then be replaced with
the equivalence

(
19
)

\lceil\text{state}\rceil\quad\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to20.36133pt{\hfil\hbox{$\hbox to0.0pt{\raise 1.93747pt\hbox{${\dashleftarrow}\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081{\dashrightarrow}$}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.3194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}_{\text{class}}$}\quad\lceil\text{$\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}$\penalty-\hskip 0.0ptstatistics numbers}\rceil\;.

Supplementing the right hand side here with the concept of numerical
values
\{\alpha_{s}\}
for all of the observables
\mathscr{A}=A(q,p)
(or
for phase variables
\{q_{1},q_{2},\ldots;p_{1},p_{2},\ldots\}
), this side will
turn into an exhaustive numerical realization of the left hand side.
Criterion
\approx
, then, turn into the
{\mathbb{R}}
-number equality
=

of all the
\mathscr{A}
-statistics or into an equality of phase
distributions
\varrho(q_{1},\ldots;p_{1},\ldots)
. This is a situation of the
classical (statistical) physics
(
C
lass
P
hys), i. e., when ‘the physics is
initially identified’ with quantities being numerical in character:
the particle coordinates/numbers, the number values of field
functions, etc. The ill-posedness of such a paradigm—the core
motive of
QT
—is discussed further below at greater length in
sects.
6.4
,
6.5
, and
7
. Consequently,
‘classicality’ is not and cannot be regarded as a primitive in the
logical construct. In both these cases,
distinguishability
\not\approx
depends on the concept of

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-states.

Remark
4
.

From this point onward, by state we will strictly mean
representations (
11
). So it makes
no sense to speak of
transitions between states
, much less of “transition from possible
to actual”
[
19
, p. 189; Everett]
,
[
152
,
90
,
194
]
. The writing
|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\rangle
and its typical the
wave-function collapse interpretation are not correct. Indeed, in
treating transition
|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}\rangle
as a state-to-state
destruction, its left hand side cannot carry any information about

\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathscr{A})}
-frequencies for other events

|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{s}\rangle
,
much less
about amount of
destruction from envisioned
\mathscr{B}
-observations

|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to2.95139pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 3.58795pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}$}}|\boldsymbol{\beta}\rangle
. Such
‘
\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathscr{B})}
-amounts’ are always present at the
experimental interpretation of the
|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle
-symbol. For this
reason, the concept of a state should not be used as a correct term
at all
[
113
]
; the terminology, however, has been settled.

The motivation given above—
S
(system,
primitives),
O
(observation) and

R
(representations),

T
(taboo), the semantic principia

I
(
QM
-statics) and

II
(numbers) complemented below with
the principium
III
—is sufficient
for further creating the basis of mathematical formalism of
QM
.
These tenets should hardly be regarded as postulates, at least in
the common meaning of the phrase ‘postulates of a physical theory’,
since they are of natural-language nature and are, as we believe,
the points of departure for reasoning whatever the approach to the
micro-world. It is clear that they are directly concerned with the
familiar dialogs, which reflect, in the words by Bohr, “[Einstein’s]
feeling of disquietude as regards the apparent lack of firmly laid
down principles …, in which all could agree”

[
149
, p. 228]
.

The underpinning of
QT
must thus begin, at least to a large
extent, with a simplification/reducing the terminology in use and
putting the language and the semantics of observations/numbers in
order, rather than in giving the ‘improved’ postulates or
definitions.

“The task is not to make sense of the quantum axioms by heaping more
structure, more definitions, …, but to throw them away wholesale”

.
C. Fuchs
[
62
, p. 989]

“Simplicity is implicit in the basic goals of scientific inquiry.
… only simple theories can attain a rich explanatory depth. … the Basic Propert[ies] should indeed be very simple”

.
N. Chomsky
[
36
, pp. 4–5]

As was underscored above, these (organizing) principles do not
stipulate for pre-determined mathematics and physics, with the
exception of the linguistic/metamathematical understanding

[
96
,
142
]
of how to look at the mathematical axioms,
structures, rational theories, and their interpretations altogether.
See also Remarks
7
and
10
, and
sects.
5
,
10
.

3.
Ensemble formations

Your acquaintance with reality grows literally by buds or

drops of perceptions. … they come totally or not at all

— W. James (1911)

Are billions upon billions of acts of observer-

participancy the foundation of everything?

— J. Wheeler
[
186
, p. 199]

The key corollary of the macro-paradigm (
12
) is not merely
the appearance of numerical data in the theory, but also the fact
that the further construct must rely not on isolated primitives but
on their aggregates being considered as an integrated whole, i. e., as
a set. This causes a choice for the ensemble notation.

3.1.
Mixtures of ensembles

Returning to the analysis of transitions

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\text{ ?}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}\alpha_{s}
, one gets that the lower row in
(
12
) comes actually from indeterminacy

\begin{array}[]{c}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\}_{n_{1}}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\}_{n_{2}}\cdots\cdots\\[2.15277pt]
\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\\[2.15277pt]
\;{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\text{ ?}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\text{ ?}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\text{ ?}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu\mkern 1.0mu\cdots\cdots\end{array}

and thus (
12
), by virtue of (
16
), should be replaced
with the scheme

(
20
)

\begin{array}[]{c}\{\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots\}\\[2.15277pt]
\mathscr{A}\;\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\;\mathscr{A}\\[2.15277pt]
\big\{\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\cdots\big\}\end{array}\;,

wherein the composition of the upper ingoing row may not be
predetermined. Fundamentally, according to (
17
), it may not
be withdrawn from (
20
), yet at the same time the meaning of
the row can in no way be aligned with the adjective ‘observable’ via
typical empirical/physical words: properties, readings,
quantities/amounts, and other ‘observable’ characteristics. Such a
non-detectability is the equivalent of a box that may be prepended
to the scheme (
6
):

(
21
)

\boxed{\cdots\cdots}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\;\smash{\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}}\;{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\;.

If
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
’s serve as
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
in (
21
), then we
have the schemes of precedence and of continuation:

\boxed{\cdots\cdots}\;\smash{\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}}\;{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\;\smash{\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{B}$}\hfil}}}\;{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\qquad\text{or}\qquad\boxed{\cdots\cdots}\;\smash{\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{B}$}\hfil}}}\;{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\;\smash{\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}}\;{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\;.

Let an observer capture the fact of any distinguishability in the
penultimate
\mathscr{A}
. Section
2.1
tells us that this may only be
the distinguishability of objects

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2},\ldots\}
; hence, this very
\mathscr{A}

turns into an observation (pt.
O
). The

M
-paradigm then gives rise to the numbers
of
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-events
(n_{1},n_{2},\ldots)
and, thereupon, their
relative frequencies
(\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2},\ldots)
by the rule
(
14
). If subsequently micro-observations
\mathscr{B}
are to
follow, then a composite macro-observation
\mathscr{B}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}\mathscr{A}
has
been formed, and frequencies
\{\varrho_{j}\}
cannot but impact on
statistical characteristics of these later
\mathscr{B}
-observation’s
micro-events. However, being an ingoing ensemble for
\mathscr{B}
, each
homogeneous
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}\}_{n_{s}}
is
indistinguishable from an indefinite ensemble

\{\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots\}_{n_{s}}
, since the concept
of ‘
\approx_{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
-sameness’ is unknown for
\mathscr{B}
.
Instrument
\mathscr{B}
is ‘aware of only its own

\approx_{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}
and cannot know
what
it destroys’,
or that the source-object consists of one and the same

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}
. Rejection of this point brings us once again
(p.
2.1
) to attempts at ‘penetrating the black box’ of
transitions (
5
), i. e., to attempts at creating the physics of
a more primary level. According to pts.
O

and
M
, an instrument produces nothing more
than its own ‘destruction list’; in this case,

(\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\}_{m_{1}},\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\}_{m_{2}},\ldots)
. This list
is completely independent of the preceding one since, according to
pt.
R
∙∙
, there cannot be restrictions
on
{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
and
{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}
. In case the set

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}\}_{n_{s}}
transits into
collection
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{k}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{k}\}_{n_{s}}
, this means
that
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}
has always transited into one and the same

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{k}
every time (under the convention

\Sigma\rightsquigarrow\infty
), and there takes place merely a
coincidence
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}={\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{k}
of eigen-primitives in
the lists
{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
and
{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}
.

If
\mathscr{B}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}\mathscr{A}
is proceeded with a third observation

\mathscr{C}
, the preceding analysis is repeated recursively with the
same result; only the values
\{\varrho_{j}\}
will be changed. In
consequence, only the following two ingoing types for macro-scheme
(
20
) are conceivable:

\displaystyle\big\{\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots\big\}\quad

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{l}\text{indefinite ensemble}\\
\lceil\text{no statistics}\rceil\;,\end{array}

\displaystyle\big\{\{{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}\}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(}\varrho_{1}\scriptscriptstyle)}\mkern 1.0mu\{{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}\}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(}\varrho_{2}\scriptscriptstyle)}\cdots\big\}\quad

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{l}\text{ensemble mixture}\\
\lceil\text{with statistics
$(\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2},\ldots)$}\rceil\;.\end{array}

It is reasonable to regard case (
3.1
) as a ‘non-interfering’
mixture of the system’s
\mathscr{A}
-preparations

\{\mathcal{S}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(}\varrho_{1}\scriptscriptstyle)},\>\mathcal{S}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(}\varrho_{2}\scriptscriptstyle)},\>\ldots\}\quad\mathrel{\hbox{\scalebox{1.3}[1]{\hbox{$\Leftarrow\mkern-13.0mu\Rightarrow$}}}}\quad\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(}\varrho_{1}\scriptscriptstyle)},\>{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(}\varrho_{2}\scriptscriptstyle)},\>\ldots\}\;,

to each of which one assigns the positive number
\varrho_{s}<1

referred to as its statistical weight. These weights—“an element
of reality”
[

, p. 649]
—are all that is inherited from
the preparation
\mathscr{A}
, and subsequent micro-observation acts

\mathscr{B}
are performed again on indefinite ensembles (
3.1
).

It is clear that in the view of transitions
\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}
in scheme
(
20
), this situation is a derivative of (
3.1
) and this
very type (
3.1
) is crucial
[
15
, p. 53]
. In other
words, if preparation is regarded a concept as essential as
observation (pt.
O
), we still remain within
the framework of the binate essence of the transition:

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.5903pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{B}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.5903pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{B}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\;.

Its left hand side should always be seen as an undetermined
primitive, even though we treat/call it the preparatory
(micro)observation. See also “preparation-measurement reciprocity”
in
[

]
.

3.2.
Ensemble brace

According to pts.
R
and

M
, representations (
11
) must
reflect all information about physics of the problem:
primitives/incomes, transitions (‘arrows’
\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}
), and outgoing
statistics. All that data are contained in scheme (
20
),
which is why the maximum that the model of a future mathematical
object—it characterizes everything we get while watching the

\mathcal{S}
—can rely on is the
ensemble brace
:

(
26
)

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\vbox{\hbox{${\left\lgroup\vbox to22.81938pt{}\right.}$}}\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\{\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots\}\\[2.15277pt]
{\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\;\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\;{\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\\[2.15277pt]
\big\{\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\cdots\big\}\end{array}\vbox{\hbox{${\left\rgroup\vbox to22.81938pt{}\right.}$}}

(or a couple of ensemble bunches).

It is immediately seen that (
26
) carries the radical
difference between situation (
17
) and its ‘roughening’
(
19
); because of the upper row. The enormous arbitrariness
within the brace and arrows
\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.90286pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}
is ‘programmed’ to
give birth to the different processing rules of statistics and to
effects being typical for
QM
. Thanks to the maximality of
(
26
), it is only this row that encodes all the sought-for
cases of distinguishability
\not\approx
. In particular, by varying
the upper row, while the lower one remains unchanged, we get into a
situation when
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-statistics
(\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{1},\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{2},\ldots)
is
found to be the same for
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
and

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\tilde{\Xi}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
, and meanwhile,

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}\not\approx\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\tilde{\Xi}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
.

The further problem is thus as follows. With the indefinite

\mathfrak{A}
-ensemble (
16
) in hand, i. e., with the upper row of
(
26
), is it possible, based on the principles described
above, to bring the still incomplete relation
\not\approx
to the
maximal quantum-physical distinguishability of states?

4.
Why does domain
{\mathbb{C}}
come into being?

… quod ideo sint imaginariae, … quod ideo sint …

tum certe forent reales ideoque non imaginariae

∗
∗
∗
∗∗∗\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\boldsymbol{{{*}{*}{*}}}

∗
∗

∗∗∗\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\boldsymbol{{{*}{*}{*}}}

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\boldsymbol{{{*}{*}{*}}}

… this is why they are imaginary. Were they …,
they would certainly be real and therefore not imaginary.

— L. Euler
(1736)

… denn die imaginären Größen
existierten doch nicht?

— D. Hilbert
(1926)

The first priority, in the
\not\approx
-distinguishability of
objects (
26
), is to separate the closest and unconditional
criterion—the outgoing
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-statistics. To do this,
let us split the lower row into families

\big\{\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\}_{\infty_{1}}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\}_{\infty_{2}}\cdots\big\}
, where

(
27
)

\infty_{1}+\infty_{2}+\cdots=\infty\;,

and, subsequently (rather than the reverse, otherwise (
3.1
)),
taking into account the ‘arbitrariness of arrows’, we also split the
upper row:

(
28
)

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}=\vbox{\hbox{${\left\lgroup\vbox to22.81938pt{}\right.}$}}\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{}c@{}c@{}c@{}l@{}}\big\{&\{{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}\}_{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\infty_{1}$}}&\qquad\{{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}\}_{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\infty_{2}$}}&\cdots&\big\}\\
&\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}&\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}&\\
\big\{&\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\cdots\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\}_{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\infty_{1}$}}&\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\cdots\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\}_{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\infty_{2}$}}&\cdots&\big\}\end{array}\vbox{\hbox{${\left\rgroup\vbox to22.81938pt{}\right.}$}}

(the indication of observation
\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.90286pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}
is omitted
further below, since it has been mirrored in primitives

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
). Hereafter, infinities

stand for cardinal
numbers (a number of elements, possibly finite) of their own

(

(

)

\begin{array}[]{c}\{{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}{\cdot}{\cdot}{\cdot}\}_{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\infty_{1}$}}\\

that differ from each other in the upper-row composition.

4.1.
Continuum of quantum phases

Cardinality of the
{\mathfrak{T}}
-set cannot be finite. This would entail
finitely many
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-primitives for all kinds of
instruments. But finiteness of this number
n_{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathfrak{T}}}
would mean
an exclusivity of its value that does not follow from anywhere. At
the same time, all the
\mathfrak{A}
-ensembles (
16
) are subsets of
the set
{\mathfrak{T}}
(boolean

2^{\mathfrak{T}}
); any finite portion
of it is ruled out. Hence, the endless variety of upper rows in
(
29
) is uncountable.

Aside from the numerical
\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}
-statistics,
program
R
does also require an association
of the numerical objects with each row

\mathfrak{A}=\{\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\>{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\>{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots\}_{\scriptstyle\infty}\quad\mathrel{\hbox{\scalebox{1.3}[1]{\hbox{$\Leftarrow\mkern-13.0mu\Rightarrow$}}}}\quad\cdots\;,

because primitive’s symbols must disappear in the ultimate
description. To avoid introducing the structures ad hoc, we will
produce numbers here—the upper row—in the same manner, in which
statistics were producing in sect.
2.5
—the lower row.
Indeed, the genesis of the concept of the number must be single in
theory. That is, we should again take into account the presence of
copies of primitives and write

(
30
)

\cdots\quad\mathrel{\hbox{\scalebox{1.3}[1]{\hbox{$\Leftarrow\mkern-13.0mu\Rightarrow$}}}}\quad\big\{\underbrace{\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime}}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}}\>\cdots}_{K\text{ times}}\big\}\;,

and numbers per se will come into being by the
\Sigma
-convention
like (
14
), i. e., through the cardinal ratios

(
31
)

\varkappa^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{\infty^{\prime}}{\infty}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\varkappa^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}}{\infty}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\ldots\;.

Now, the discreteness of micro-transition acts is embodied in
(
30
) with the sequence
({\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}
,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}
, …),
and the uncountability of micro-arbitrariness is inherited by
attaching the symbolic ‘quantities’—‘countless’ characters

(\infty^{\prime}
,
\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}
, …)—to elements of this sequence. The
global discreteness says that there are no grounds to assume a more
than countable infinity
\aleph_{\textsf{o}}
for the set
{\mathfrak{T}}
, i. e.,

|{\mathfrak{T}}|=\aleph_{\textsf{o}}
. The infinity of the family (
30
),
hence, has the type

2^{\aleph_{\textsf{o}}}=\aleph\;,

i. e., it is continual
[
100
]
. Parenthetically, the

2^{\aleph_{\textsf{o}}}
is the only known way of introducing the
continual (more than discrete) mathematical infinity. Which
possibilities exist for the form of row (
30
)?

The trivial case
\mathfrak{A}=\big\{{\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime}}}\big\}
,
i. e.,
K=1
in (
30
) drops out at once, since element

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}
would always go into the same primitive:

(
32
)

\begin{array}[]{c}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\cdots\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\}_{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\infty_{1}$}}\\
\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\quad\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\\
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\cdots\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\}_{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\infty_{1}$}}\end{array}\quad=\quad\begin{array}[]{c}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\}_{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\infty_{1}$}}\\
\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}{\mkern-1.0mu\cdot{\cdot}\cdot\mkern-1.0mu}\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}{\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\\
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\}_{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\infty_{1}$}}\end{array}\;.

But this is tantamount to the identity

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\equiv{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}
, which robs of any meaning the
concept of the transition
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.90286pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}

whatsoever. We get a single number here—the number of

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}
-clicks—and arrive thereby at classical
statistics, the physics of which is inadequate with respect to the
interference patterns. Hence the following options are admissible
for the formations (
30
):

(
33
)

\big\{\underbrace{\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime}}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}}}_{K=2}\big\}\mkern 1.0mu,\quad\ldots,\quad\big\{\underbrace{\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime}}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}}\mkern 1.0mu\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}}\>\cdots}_{3\leqslant K<\infty}\big\}\mkern 1.0mu,\quad\ldots\quad\\
\ldots,\quad\big\{\underbrace{\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime}}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}}\mkern 1.0mu\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}}\>\cdots}_{K=\infty}\big\}

with minimal
K=2
. If some of the infinities

(\infty^{\prime},\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime},\ldots)
are finite here or countable, this does not
change the total continuality
\aleph
. The extreme case

K=\infty
—a countable infinity of continuums—does also change
no this count because of
\aleph+\aleph+\cdots=\aleph

[
80
]
.
All of these infinities may be even countably duplicated without
augmenting the continuum since

\aleph\cdot\aleph\cdots=\aleph^{\aleph_{\textsf{o}}}=\aleph
.

What can one say about relationship of cases (
33
) between each
other? Do we have to deal with their total arbitrariness or with
only one of these schemes? The latter case—the
sameness/indistinguishability of upper rows in (
29
)—would
correspond to the structural staticity of theory. Or, whether one
(unrecognizable upper) row should differ (why?) from another in the
number (what?) of defining primitives

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime},\ldots\}
(which ones?)?

Suppose the variability of
K
. That is, consider the simultaneous
existence of, say, the
K=\{2,3\}
rows

\big\{\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime}}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}}\big\}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\big\{\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime}}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}}\big\}\;.

However each of the
2
-row is a particular case of the
3
-row with
a cardinal number
\infty^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}=\penalty\hskip 0.0pt0
:

\big\{\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime}}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}}\big\}=\big\{\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime}}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}\}_{\{\infty^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}=0\}}\big\}\subset\big\{\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime}}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}\}_{\infty^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}}\big\}

(the case in point is sets). Therefore these situations are
structurally indistinguishable from each other, and the
K=2
theory
is a
sub
theory for
K=3
. So, the cases
K=\{2,3\}
are
actually not mutually exclusive; rather, they form an embedding. We
thus have arrived at the one cumbersome and common construct akin to
the Russian dolls
2\supset 3\supset 4\supset\cdots
. Hence the
minimal 2-theory will always be present inside of all the higher
orders
K>2
as an ‘independent (sub)world’. For this reason, the

K=2
theory must be created in any way; incidentally, it will
enclose the
K=1
case.

On the other part, we have no criteria to terminate the sequence

2\rightarrowtail 3\rightarrowtail\cdots
at some intermediate

K<\infty
. Such a cut-off does immediately arise an issue of the
questionable empirical exclusivity of a certain ‘world integer

K\geqslant 3
’ that defines the number of ‘physically inaccessible’

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-objects. Besides, these options would be related to a
certain topological dimension
K\geqslant 3
that has an unmotivated origin.
We thus conclude that the non-minimal options
K=3
,
K=4
, … in
(
33
) should be dismissed.

Remark
5
.

A few remarks may be made in connection with case
K=\infty
. It is
related to a conglomerate of infinities, which has the form of a
discretely infinite family of continual infinities
\{\varkappa^{\prime}
,

\varkappa^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}
, …}, and things would have been even ‘worse’ had
the staticity of the schemes (
33
) been changed to variability.
Such formations would need to be equipped with topology and with
associated concepts of convergence and of limit. But all this
touches on principally unobservable numerical entities, for which it
is not clear how to motivate the further reductions to ‘finite
mathematics’ as required: dimensions, finite approximations, finite
numbers (which ones?), and the like. More to the point, all of that
would pertain to the global structural parameters of the theory
prior to constructing it per se, not to mention the physical models.
To put it plainly, such an assumption would result not in a theory
but in a theory of theories, and so on ad infinitum which should be
somewhere terminated in some way. For these reasons, we leave the
case
K=\infty
aside, though it might be worth elaborating on it.
However, in sect.
7.6
, we will give a further
justification of that the number domain of the theory is what it has
already been known in
QM
.

As a result, one has a choice: the structural staticity
K=2
or
entirely non-structured/undetermined set of outgoing primitives

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{j},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{k},\ldots\}
, i. e., extremely complex case

K=\infty
. We do choose
K=2
. This option might have been adopted
even before on the ground that the most minimalistic construction,
which set-theoretically gives rise, as a minimum, to the minimal
numerical object—a single number—corresponds to the minimal

K=2
in (
33
). The maximal case is problematic, while the
mid-ones are ruled out. That is to say, all possible assumptions
regarding the upper row structure in (
29
) are
indistinguishable from a case just as if the row contained two
primitives only
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}
,

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\}=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}
. Functionality of the
symbol
\cup
, having regard to inclusion of the
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}
’s copies, is unchanged as is; see sect.
5.1

further below.

We establish also that, in the following writing

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\infty_{1}^{\prime}}\cup\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{1}}\quad\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\large$\scriptstyle\dashrightarrow$}}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\raise 3.87495pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\large$\scriptstyle\dashrightarrow$}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\large$\scriptstyle\dashrightarrow$}}$}\quad\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\}_{\infty_{1}}

of the scheme (
29
), none of the primitives
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
,

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}
coincide with
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}
. Otherwise, the
unrestricted adjunction of identical transitions

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}
to (
29
) would mean
indeterminacy of both the number
\varkappa_{1}
and the actual
statistics
(\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{1},\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{2},\ldots)
.

Let us take into account that numbers (
31
) are
mathematically generated by the standard scheme:
\lceil
(ordered)
integers
\rceil

\rightarrowtail

\lceil
(ordered) rationals
\rceil

\rightarrowtail

\lceil
(ordered) continuum
\rceil
. The natural
ordering
<
is always present here and, as is well known

[
169
, p. 52]
, can be isomorphically represented by the
set-theoretic inclusion
\subset
on a certain system of sets. That
inclusion (
=
‘to be contained in’), in turn, is directly
concerned with the semantics of sect.
2
. The natural-language
term ‘accumulating’—‘the old is being nested into the new’—is
formalized to create sets by the cumulative ensembles (see
sect.
5.1
).

We now conclude that all kinds of schemes (
29
) form an

\aleph
-continuum, for which there is no reasonable rationale for
equipping it with a topology other than the standard order topology
of the 1-dimensional real
{\mathbb{R}}
-axis or its equivalents. Call the
quantity
\varkappa\in{\mathbb{R}}

quantum phase
.

It should be added that in considering some two upper rows in
(
29
) as infinite sets

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\infty^{\prime}}\cup\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}}\quad\text{and}\quad\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\tilde{\infty}^{\prime}}\cup\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\tilde{\infty}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}}\;,

and comparison of cardinals

(\infty^{\prime}

\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime})
do ‘serve’.

The
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-objects (
37
) remain and they, as a family,
exhaustively inherit the problem’s physics. The quantities
\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{s}

are the really observable (unitless) numbers—the percentage
quantity of events—which are declared by instrument/observer to be
the distinguishable
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-objects. The quantities

\varkappa_{s}
are the internal and unremovable degrees of freedom.
Figuratively speaking, the
\varkappa
’s may be speculatively
referred to as phases, but they may not be associated with an actual
quantity of something. Not only is any material or the classical
treatment of these ‘amounts’ impossible, but it is fundamentally

prohibited
since the converse would have meant endowing the
nonexistent boxes (
5
)–(
6
) with a notional content
or asserting the nature of their origin. Justification is only
allowed here for the fact of their existence, which is mirrored by
the presence of the left hand side in the concept of the transition

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.90286pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
(Remark
2
).

In view of numerous and ongoing discussions of the
meaning

to the quantum state
[
133
]
, note that,
for the same reason, any its (even merely similar)
classical/ontological and causal ‘visualization mechanisms’

[
160
, p. 137]
as the wave function of a certain real
matter, of a hypothetical observable, of an ‘objective knowledge’,
or of the classical data (whatever this all means) are—and this we
stress with emphasis—pointless. This is why, strictly
speaking, without further theoretical conventions,

•

it

is impossible
[
50
, p. 13]
to
make/prepare, observe/read-off, transmit or measure/approximate a
state, or to endow it with the property of being known/unknown, or
physically recognize/compare/distinguish it from the other.

We will be repeatedly turning back to this matter in
sects.
6.3.1
,
6.4
,
6.5
, and
10.2
. The present
thesis has no undergone a change even with regard to the word
‘statistics’ in the Born rule
[
27
]
, if only because the rule
is a substantial—two-to-one—reduction of the

(\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu},\varkappa)
-data. The state will itself, when created as a
mathematical object, determine the meaning

of all of
these words (see sect.
5.3
) with an appropriate concept of
the physical distinguishability (sect.
2.4
). Cf. the works

[
114
,
140
]
and the “methods to directly measure general
quantum states … by weak measurements” in
[
115
]
and, on
the other hand, the statements in sect. 15.5 of the book

[
9
]
.

All the
\varkappa_{s}
and
\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{s}
are independent of each other,
except for relation
\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{1}+\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{2}+\cdots=1
. Taking into account the
admissible renormalization of both
{\mathbb{R}}
-numbers, the pair

(\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu},\varkappa)
can be topologically identified with a point on
the complex plane:

\vbox{\hbox{\footnotesize$\Big($}}\begin{smallmatrix}\displaystyle\varkappa\\[1.29167pt]
\displaystyle\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}\end{smallmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\footnotesize$\Big)$}}\;\rightleftarrows\;(\lambda,\mu)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}{\mathbb{C}}\;.

That is, the domain
{\mathbb{C}}
is at the moment just a 2-dimensional
numeric continuum without algebra of complex numbers. Notice that
the pairs of
{\mathbb{R}}
-numbers is a starting point—different from
ours—to the
QT
in ref.
[
67
]
. More than that,
impossibility of the real-number
QM
became a subject of the direct
experimental test to distinguish between the complex-number and
real-number representations of
QT
: on photonic systems
[
191
]

and the superconducting qubits
[
124
]
.

The issue of the numerical domain over which the quantum description
is being conducted—the real
{\mathbb{R}}
, the complex
{\mathbb{C}}
, the
quaternions
\mathsf{Q}
, or whatever—is non-trivial and continues
to be the subject of study
[
76
,
1
,
67
,
10
,
111
]
. The complexity
{\mathbb{C}}
is often motivated by quantum
dynamics (Schrödinger’s equation)
[
89
, p. 132;
Stueckelberg]
,
[
170
]
, however, such a motivation is
inconsistent, and as we have seen, there is no need for it. The
rigidity of the
{\mathbb{C}}
-domain points to the fact that, in
particular, the quaternion
QM
also has no place to originate from

[
9
, sect. 10.1]
, although it was the object of
theoretical constructs in the 1960–70’s
[
57
]
. Note that
even the most comprehensive works
[
111
, p. 217]
,

[
89
, p. 131]
,
[
21
, p. 234]
,
[
22
,
67
]
, and
[
10
]
(!) observe a difficulty in the full
substantiation of the
{\mathbb{C}}
-domain in
QT
. Within the last decade,
this theme had also attracted the particular attention of the
information-theoretic approach to
QT

[
128
,
67
,
40
]
.

The above-outlined emergence of the numerical quantities in theory
is as yet draft at the moment, and will be refined further below in
sect.
7
.

5.
Empiricism and mathematics

Set theory does not seem today to have

… organic interrelationship with physics

— P. Cohen & R. Hersh

[
37
, p. 116]

… physics has … to say about the foundations

of mathematics …“if we believe in ZF there

is nothing for physics to say” is not right

— P. Benioff

[
63
, p. 31]

Up to this point we have dealt, roughly speaking, with a single
abstract aggregate
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
isolated from the others.
However, the constructional nature of the ensemble brace
(
34
) entails the following closedness relation between
them. Every brace
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
is composed of some others in
infinitely many ways (for remote analogies, see

[
6
, sect. 11.2]
), i. e., it is a union

(
39
)

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}=\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}\cup\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}\;,

and, to put it in reverse, any union of two brace is a third
object-brace. In assemblages (
39
), the operation
\cup
,
which generates them, is commutative and associative:

(
40
)

A\cup B=B\cup A\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad(A\cup B)\cup C=A\cup(B\cup C)\;,

and these 2- and 3-term relations not only are not a formal
supplement, but should be read as the
structural
properties
in general. Let us address the matter more closely.

5.1.
Union of ensembles

Consider the lower
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-rows of brace
(
28
) and experimental forming the new real

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-ensembles from them. Let the procedure of that
forming be denoted by
\mathsf{U}(A,B,\ldots)
, where
(A,B,\ldots)
are the
ensembles per se. Its essence is such that it is comprehensively
determined by the following minimum. A rule that involves the

fewest
(i. e., two) number of arguments
\mathsf{U}(A,B)=\text{ ?}
and a rule of the
repeated
applying

\mathsf{U}
to itself:
\mathsf{U}(\mathsf{U}(\ldots),\ldots)=\text{ ?}
.
Obviously, we should write

(
41
)

\mathsf{U}(A,B)=\mathsf{U}(B,A)\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\mathsf{U}\big(\mathsf{U}(A,B),C\big)=\mathsf{U}\big(A,\mathsf{U}(B,C)\big)\;,

which is of course merely the empirical rephrasing the standard
properties (
40
) of operation
\cup
. However, the converse
is logically preferable: empiricism (
41
) is formalized into the
abstract properties (
40
). If we now attach the upper
‘quantum’ primitives to the low
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-rows—a requirement
of sect.
2.1
—then the operationality of actions with the
resulting
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-braces would be just like that of

\mathsf{U}
, i. e., (
41
). In other words, we carry over (and had
already used everywhere) properties (
41
) to the general
operation on
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-brace, without distinguishing between
the essences of symbols
\cup
and
\mathsf{U}
. ‘Micro-operationality’
of empiricism and its formalization are confined, at most, by the
rules (
40
)–(
41
).

Let us temporarily discontinue using the numerical terminology as
applied to
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-objects. They differ from each other due to
relationships between their ‘innards’, rather than because of our
assignment of differed symbols
(\lambda,\mu)
to them. The brace
are comprised of elements that are combined into sets and are added
to them. In the language of the abstract logic, we are dealing with
the fact that transitions
x
form the brace
A,\mkern 1.0muB
, …, i. e., they
are in the membership relationships
x\in A
,
x\in B
, … or,
when accumulated as micro-acts, ‘get belonged to them’. That is to
say, the brace themselves and their formation (accumulation of
statistics for the
\Sigma
-limit) are equivalent to a huge number
of propositional ‘micro-sentences
x\in A

or

x\in B

or
…’. However, again, this is nothing but a logically
formal equivalent of the union operation
\cup
:

(
42
)

A\cup B=\big\{x\mkern 1.0mu\big|\>(x\in A)\mathbin{\mathchar 8799}(x\in B)\big\}\;,

which is already being constantly exploited above.

Remark
6
.

As is well known
[
169
,
100
]
, due to properties of logical
atoms
\in
(
membership
) and
\mathbin{\mathchar 8799}
(
or
), the
properties of sentences such as (
42
) are determined precisely
by rules (
40
) for
\cup
. Technically, we should also take
an idempotence
A\cup A=A
into account, however. At the same time,
the need to have a number requires that the duplicates in ensembles
have to be taken into consideration. Nevertheless, this situation is
easily simulated by the set theory itself. Indeed, look first the
lower row in (
26
) as a strictly abstract set

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime},\ldots\}\subset{\mathfrak{T}}
. Then, instrument

\mathscr{A}
‘asserts’ the distinguishable elements
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}
,

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2},\ldots\}
and those that should be thought of as their
equivalents:

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}^{\prime}\approx{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{1}\approx\cdots=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu,\hskip 17.70859pt{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}^{\prime}\approx{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{2}\approx\cdots=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\mkern 1.0mu,\hskip 17.70859pt\ldots\;.

This equivalence can be characterized, say, by words ‘a detector
click at one and the same place
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}
’. Upon such a
formalization, one obtains the formation

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}^{\prime}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{1}\cdots\}\mkern 1.0mu\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}^{\prime}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{2}\cdots\}

\cdots

\approx

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\cdots\}\mkern 1.0mu\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\cdots\}

\cdots
, i. e.,
the very lower row in (
28
). It is within this context that
we think of the union operation without running into
inconsistencies. Accordingly,
\raise 1.43515pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.43515pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}\cup\raise 1.43515pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.43515pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}\neq\raise 1.43515pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.43515pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
but the standard symbol
\cup

continues to be used for simplicity.

Therefore if we get back to the numeral labels (
37
), but
ignore the ‘inner composition‘ of
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
, i. e., the

M
-paradigm, thus excluding
\cup
and
(
40
) from the reasoning, then all possible

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-objects would turn into the semantically ‘segregated
ideograms’. Micro-transitions, their mass nature, arbitrariness,

\not\approx
-distinguishability, and the ‘quantumness’ of the task
simply disappear. To take an illustration, the obvious statement

the brace
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.90282pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
has an empirical ‘kindred’

with its duplication

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.90282pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.90282pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}

becomes pointless, because the property
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}\cup\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}=\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
is missing. And this is despite
the fact that the creating the transition copies in

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}^{\prime}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
is a primary operation for generating the objects
and reasoning at all. Construction of the theory would then become
possible only with the interpretative introduction of the vanished
concepts anew. Therefore macro-empiricism necessitates that the
relationships (
40
) be operative rules, and with that the
quantumness or classicality of consideration is of no significance.

Remark
7
.

Let us take a closer look at the situation on the
opposite—mathematical—side. The union of sets
\cup
is a
fundamental operation already at the level of the set-theoretic
formalization; e. g., the Zermelo–Fraenkel (
ZF
) axioms
[
100
]
.
This is one of the first ways on how to create sets—the axiom of
union. So if we believe in the set-theoretic mode of
explaining/creating the quantum rudiments, the quantitative
description will inevitably invoke operationality of the
mathematical primitive
\cup
through rules (
40
). This
would be suffice to declare,

•

inasmuch as
we have nothing but
\cup
and

\raise 1.43515pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.43515pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
(taboo
T
),
commutativity/associativity of theory is then postulated from the
outset by (
40
), with the subsequent carrying these
structures over to numerical representations, i. e., to
{\mathbb{R}}
or

{\mathbb{C}}
.

It is preferable, however, to adhere to the sequence order in
ideology more stringently—
\lceil
observation
\rceil

\rightarrowtail

\lceil
mathematics
\rceil
,
\lceil
empiricism
\rceil

\rightarrowtail

\lceil
numerical representation
\rceil
—without substitution it for the
opposite. At least, if we rely upon the comprehension of the
empiricism as a formalization of the zero-principium of
QT
(Remark
2
):

•

our primordial perceptions are formalized only into sets
and set-theoretic
\cup
-abstraction (
42
).

See also
[
113
, Ch. 3]
,
[
63
, p. 178]
,

[
87
, p. 323]
,
[
32
]
,
[
35
, pp. 12, 86,
Ch. 4]
,
[
60
, sect. V.9]
, sect.
11.1
, and

[
22
]
.

Summing up, we detect a kind of a junction point: the physical and
mathematical fundamentality of operation
\cup
for describing the
elementary acts. That is to say, the mathematics of

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-brace (
34
) and of objects (
37
)
may not inherently be exhausted by them as ‘bare’ sets without
structures.

Recalling now pr.
II
, we draw a
conclusion regarding the very construction of the theory.

•

Reconciliation of the
R
-paradigm with
empiricism must transform itself into
rewriting
the primary
ensemble
\cup
-constructions (
28
), (
34
),
(
36
), and relationships between them into the language of
numerical symbols.

More formally, we have the following continuation of
pt.
R
∙
.

\mathsf{R}^{\textsf{+}}

Homomorphism

of the ensemble-brace properties ‘onto
numbers’
: mutual
\cup
-relationships (
40
) between the

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-brace should be carried over to relations between
their numerical
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-representations (
37
).

Thereby we once again fix the maximum that is available for the
building up of quantum mathematics. One may handle only the

\cup
-aggregates of transitions—constructions

(
34
)
, (
37
)—and the minimal modules (
36
).

5.2.
Semigroup

In line with (
39
), let us split the unitary brace
(
36
) into two or combine two brace into one. Delete also the
symbols of primitives
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
and
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
from there. As was
pointed out above, they are not necessary at this stage. Replacing
the notation of upper cardinals (
36
) with pairs

(\infty^{\prime}_{\scriptscriptstyle 1},\infty^{\prime}_{\scriptscriptstyle 2})
and

(\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{\scriptscriptstyle 1},\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{\scriptscriptstyle 2})
, upon the union one obtains

(
43
)

(\infty^{\prime}_{\scriptscriptstyle 1},\infty^{\prime}_{\scriptscriptstyle 2})\cup(\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{\scriptscriptstyle 1},\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{\scriptscriptstyle 2})=(\infty^{\prime}_{\scriptscriptstyle 1}+\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{\scriptscriptstyle 1},\infty^{\prime}_{\scriptscriptstyle 2}+\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{\scriptscriptstyle 2})\;.

Here, addition
+
obviously satisfies the properties
(
40
). If the cardinal ‘
\infty
-coordinates’ are replaced
with the ‘finite percentages’
(\varkappa,\mathfrak{S})
introduced
above, i. e., if one puts

(
44
)

\Big\{\varkappa=\frac{\infty_{\scriptscriptstyle 1}}{\infty_{\scriptscriptstyle 1}+\infty_{\scriptscriptstyle 2}}\mkern 1.0mu,\quad\mathfrak{S}=\infty_{\scriptscriptstyle 1}+\infty_{\scriptscriptstyle 2}\Big\}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\Big\{\infty_{\scriptscriptstyle 1}=\varkappa\mkern 1.0mu\mathfrak{S}\mkern 1.0mu,\quad\infty_{\scriptscriptstyle 2}=(1-\varkappa)\mkern 1.0mu\mathfrak{S}\Big\}

as in (
35
), then rule (
43
) acquires the form of a
number composition:

(
45
)

The finite ensembles are vanishingly small in their contribution
into infinite ones (
\Sigma
-postulate), i. e., elements of the

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}

, …, i. e., the act of
macro-observation.

Let us designate the image of finite ensembles as
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}0\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}

•

5.3.
Measurement

The described above numerical
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-version of the

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-brace ‘
\cup
-phenomenology’ makes it possible now
to preliminarily formalize a concept, the absence of which deprives
the theory of its basis. Namely,
measuring statistics by
observation
\mathscr{A}
over
\mathcal{S}
:

(
50
)

\text{{QM}-measurement:}\qquad\big([\lambda_{1},\mu_{1}],[\lambda_{2},\mu_{2}],\ldots\big)\;\longmapsto\;(\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{1},\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{2},\ldots)\;.

That is, the
[\lambda,\mu]
-collection gets mathematically mapped
into the
\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}
-statistics. This is a maximum of information provided
by observation
\mathscr{A}
. The mapping (
50
) annihilates the
pairs
[\lambda,\mu]
. Therefore the inheritance/homomorphism of
operations
\cup
and
\uplus
onto anything at all is eliminated.
Upon operation (
50
), both the
(\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu},\varkappa)
-sets and

\cup
-unions thereof,
\uplus
-operations, and the semigroup

\mathfrak{G}
per se disappear. As a well-known result, the distinctive
feature subsequently referred to as superposition will also
disappear after measurement. The new numbers
\{\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{s}\}
may be
‘added up’ only as required by the different, i. e., the classical
rule: forming the convex combinations (
47
). We note that the
formalization of measurement does not now depend on how the
mathematical map
[\lambda,\mu]\rightarrowtail(\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu)
would be
further implemented—it is a separate job
[
27
]
—or how the

t
-dynamics would be introduced.

Remark
9
.

Incorporation of
t
-dynamics into the theory is still impossible
due to the absence of mathematics to be applied to instants
t_{1}
,

t_{2}
. Accordingly, no physical
t
-process, a temporal imitation of
the measuring, or its dynamical description may correspond to the
mathematical mapping (
50
). The known ‘conceptual’ problems
with the collapse’ dynamics
[
66
,
183
,
86
,
108
,
126
]
are actually non-existent
[
107
,
109
,
111
]
. More precisely, they stem from the blurring of meaning
that we typically give to the words ‘states’ (what is that?),
‘ensembles’ (what are they comprised of?), and ‘dynamics/collapse’
(of what?). In regard to the latter, the authors of the book

[
113
]
speak out in the most definitive manner—the “fairy
tales”. See also sect.
10.3
further below.

In sect.
2.1
, the fundamental premise of the

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-symbol-based distinguishability
\not\approx
was the
foundation of the entire subsequent language; “two clicks are never
identical”
[
178
, p. 761]
. One then observes that the
measurement or its outcome will essentially remain a vacuous
term—“For microsystems nothing can be directly measured”

[
110
, p. 304]
—up until it invokes the concept of a

QM
-state, i. e., the
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
- and
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-objects. In the
following, we shall see that, as a rough guide, everything that is
observable whatsoever is a function of the state and of the state
space.

Once again it is stressed—the concept of the state must precede
the notion of measurement, rather than the reverse. “[J.] Bell
fulminated against the use of the word ‘measurement’ as a primary
term when discussing quantum foundations”
[
85
, p. 262]
. See
also the entire chapter 23—“Against ‘measurement”’—in

[
20
, pp. 213–231]
.

5.4.
Covariance with respect to observations
(‘the same’)

Up to this point we had had no need for the matching of observation

\mathscr{A}
with observation
\mathscr{B}
, although it is clear that a
description based on a certain specified
\mathscr{A}
will inevitably be
non-invariant with respect to the tool
\{\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B},\ldots\}
—‘observation space’—and unacceptable
(pt.
R
) due to the impermissible
exclusivity of the set
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1},\ldots\}
. At the same time,
we do not have anything but
\{\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B},\ldots\}
and micro-acts
(
12
) (pts.
T
and

M
). In the brace, this fact has already
been present—transitions
\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.90286pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}
are combined into
integrities (
26
). Logically, however, the

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
-,
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}
-objects are
incomparable and isolated from each other as carriers of statistics
of different origin.

On the other hand, ‘
the same
is observed by instrument

\mathscr{B}
, as by instrument
\mathscr{A}
’. Although this context has not
yet been invoked, without it the application of set-theoretic
constructs to physics is devoid of meaning, just like the union of
the speeds of an electron and of the Moon into a set

\{\boldsymbol{v}_{\text{e}},\boldsymbol{v}_{\scriptscriptstyle\text{M}}\}
, with the subsequent
creating a certain physical characteristic of this ‘two-body
system’; say, the mean velocity

\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{v}_{\text{e}}+\boldsymbol{v}_{\scriptscriptstyle\text{M}})
. Indeed, “The
statements of quantum mechanics are meaningful and can be logically
combined
only
if one can imagine a
unique experimental
context
”
[

.

Thus the global structuredness is required in the set of various

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-data according to the context ‘the same, identical’ or its
negation. Apparently, this addition implies such entities as ‘the
same particle’, ‘in the same preparation/state’, ‘under the same
temperature and
\mathbf{M}
-environment (
12
)’, ‘the same

\mathcal{S}

,

\mathbf{M}

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
. All the notions here, including the state, are physical
