2.4
).

The notion ‘with the same initial data’ falls under the same
category, if the intention is to use the term time
t
. Again, the
very creating the
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-brace as a set ‘by the piece’ is from
the outset thought of as (sect.

\mathbf{M}

\mathscr{A}
-statistics

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}

\langle

,

\mathbf{M}

as the

\mathscr{B}

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}

), since the detectors cause an

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}

Notice that the notions “the same” and “distinguishable”
(Remark
2
), while antonymous, mutually exclude each other.
Semantically, one without the other makes no sense, which closely
resembles Bohr’s conception of complementarity
[

]
.

It follows from the above that in order to match
\mathscr{A}
and

\mathscr{B}
, the

]
category

is
required, however, we are only in possession of the ensemble brace

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
and
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}

(pt.

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi^{\prime}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\rightleftarrows\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi^{\prime}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}
,

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\rightleftarrows\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-objects is absolute. (It is clear that the matching of
single micro-events
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.90286pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}
and

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.67369pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{j}
is also futile.) It is
impossible to associate physics with the abstractly segregated

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
-brace. Otherwise, the solitary object

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
, generating nothing more than a statistics
provided by the single instrument
\mathscr{A}
, would yield a
description of everything, which is absurd by
pt.
R
∙∙
. The physical contents (to
come) arise precisely through the above-mentioned matching; see
sect.
6.4
below.

As a result, we adopt a kind of the relativity-principle
analogue—a tenet on the quantum observational covariance.

should

(pts.

\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{+}}}

) by differing instruments

under a common (the same)

\langle

,

\mathbf{M}
,

\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle

.
(

5.4.1.
Semantic closedness and the equal sign
=

We are currently returning once again to sect.
2.1
, falling into
a situation when the case in hand does not just entail fundamental
theory in the form of
\lceil
math
\rceil

\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{+}}}

\lceil
physical
‘bla-bla-bla’
\rceil
, while, continuing on an informal note, the
mathematics of physics—quantum mathematics—is being created
‘from scratch’. When building up this math, it is impossible to
forego the physical conventions
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
, meanwhile, any preliminary and
the formal characterization for the
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
is ruled out.

Indeed, the attempts to mathematically formalize the physical
context of observation, rather than observation itself, will not
logically manage without another—‘more
fundamental’—observation, in this case, of the very
experimental environment. The semantic cycling is apparent here, and
any of its mathematization will lead to a retrogression of
definitions into infinity, which is known as the “von Neumann
catastrophe”
[
43
, pp. 158–…]
) or as the “trying to
swallow itself by the tail”
[
20
, p. 220]
. Which is why, once
again, the ‘box (
6
) method’ prohibitions are required. See
also a paragraph containing the capitalized emphasize “
cannot
in principle
” on p. 418 of the work
[
107
]
. Sooner or later,
it will have to be declared that mathematics will be created for

the
convention
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
, and that this mathematics will be a
mathematical model for this
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
. The analogous
argument—“mathematics is there to serve physics, and not the
other way round”
[
154
, p. 242; L. Hardy]
—has already
long been met in the literature
[
6
,
9
,
64
]
. In
connection with the “general contextual models”, see the books

[
202
,
93
]
(the Växjö-model, “quantum contextuality”)
and bibliography therein.

Remark
10

(semantic)
.

To avoid the just mentioned linguistic closedness—a kind of
mathematical ‘pathology’ of the physical and natural languages—a
description that lays claim to the role of an unambiguous/rigorous
theory requires a careful separation of the object- and
meta-languages. For more detail, see
[
96
, sects. 14–16]
,

[
142
, sect. V.1]
,
[
169
, sect. 3.9]
. For this reason,
the constructs should track the blending of the object
QM
-domain
(syntax) and the meta-domain (semantics) and, more generally, the
penetration of extra-linguistic elements of thinking
[
35
]

into
QM
. The notion of ‘the same
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
’, which is intuitive in the
natural language, should explicitly be indicated as an external and
fundamental category (pr.
III
), and
its circular re-interpretations/re-translations within the theory
should be banned. That is, re-stating ‘the sameness
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
, the
identical
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
’ by way of word or of the equality symbol
=
between
some other entities is forbidden.

•

“The same” may no have a definition in terms of anything
else. It exists prior to theory and has only a meaning (
=
verbal
context), though its natural-language descriptions may be of great
variety and be “presented to us in wildly different ways”

[
120
, p. 2 and the whole of sect. “The awkwardness of
equality”]
.

One could, e. g., accept the typical verbal vehicle “a complex of
conditions, which allows of any number of repetitions” (quotation
from the literature). It is clear that the words “complex … allows … repetitions” here are just another semantic equivalent
to the word “the same
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
”. The physics terminology per se
(sects.
6.4
,
6.5
, and
9.1
) will become
accessible when physical concepts are introduced via the
originating—and obligatorily very
ascetic—quantum-mechanics language. See also the selected
thesis on page
•
‣
11.1
.

It is crucial to immediately note that, in the same manner, the
classical description contains the cited arguments in their
entirety. It is easy to convince that such a description also
implies implicitly that which is designated above as
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
; otherwise,
the physical reasoning would be entirely impossible. “[W]e often
prefer to regard a number of outcomes of distinct physical
operations as registering the same property, … representing the
same measurement. … permitting an unrestricted identification of
outcomes would lead to
"
grammatical chaos
"
”
(Foulis–Randall
[
59
, p. 232]
). More to the point, the
physics and mathematics not merely have been closely interwoven with
each other. Any recursive procedure of definitions will inevitable
result in either a cyclic definition at some level, or a definition
which refers outside not only of the physics but even of the math.
Hence, the hierarchical arrangement of notions/…/definitions—a
property that is frequently uncontrolled and violated in the human
thinking—can only be meaningful if at least one knot in the
definition network is externally defined. In this work, that basic
points are, as a rough guide, the brace

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.90286pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
and the notion of ‘the same

\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
’; motivated in Remarks
2
and
10
, respectively.

Remark
11
.

Here, the situation is similar to the role of the axiom of choice in
the
ZF
-system
[
37
,
100
]
. It has been well known for a
long time that the axiom is often subconsciously implied

[
60
, Chs. II, IV]
; it can also not be either circumvented
or ignored. Another counterexample to ‘infinite retrogression and
circularity’ in logic comes from the very same system. This is a ban
on infinite chain of set memberships
\in
on the left

\boldsymbol{\vbox{\hbox{$|\hskip-1.5937pt|$}}{}}\cdots\in X_{n}\in\cdots\in X_{2}\in X_{1}\in X_{0}

(the regularity axiom
[\forall x\in X,\>x\cap X\neq\varnothing]\Rightarrow[X=\varnothing]
) under the
permissibility of the infinite
(\in)
-continuing to the right:

X_{0}\in X_{1}\in X_{2}\in\cdots\in X_{n}\in\cdots\in\cdots

(not rigorously, the infinity axiom)
[
159
,
100
]
.

The obvious parallels here are the famous Russell paradox

[
60
]
or a chaos in the computer file system when the ‘hard
links’ from a folder to the parent folder are allowed. Thus the
relations
\in
‘downwards’ to the left and necessarily terminates
in something, i. e., in a set
X_{0}
that contains nothing:

\varnothing=X_{0}\in\cdots\in X_{n}\in\cdots
. Therefore one needs to
give ‘meaning’ to the only set—the empty one
\varnothing
.
Incidentally, it is these axioms that guarantee the existence of
infinitely many ordinal numbers (
113
) and uniqueness of
this structure. The ordinals, and numbers at all, have yet to be
dealt with further below in more detail.

All that remains is to add that no theory in physics is feasible
without re-calculations of physical units, of vectors/tensors,
without transformations in the fibre superstructures over manifolds,
etc. Accordingly, the considerations on invariance and on
transformations should be present in the quantum case as well, but
it—which is its principal difference from the classical
case—still lacks the concepts of physical quantities/properties
(see sect.
6.4
). Therefore such argumentation may only be
applied to those objects that we have at our disposal, i. e., to the

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-brace. The renunciation of
pr.
III
would actually be
tantamount to the inability to make the physics theories whatsoever.

Now, pr.
III
and the ‘quantum
diversity of the reference frames’
\{\mathscr{A}
,
\mathscr{B}
, …}
require a kind of factorization of the entire family

\{\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}},\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}},\ldots,\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}^{\prime},\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}^{\prime},\ldots\}
with respect to
the conception
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
, i. e., the introduction of an operation of
equating the results
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
,

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}
that came when observing
\mathcal{S}
. The
immediate putting
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$=$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to7.7778pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle?$}\hfil}}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}
should not be made, since these brace are
simply different sets. That is why, with isolated semigroups

\big\{\underbrace{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\uplus$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.5pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu$}\hfil}\;;\;\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi^{\prime}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\mkern 1.0mu,\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\mkern 1.0mu,\mkern 1.0mu\ldots}_{\mathfrak{G}_{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}\big\}\mkern 1.0mu,\quad\big\{\underbrace{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\uplus$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.5pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}\mkern-1.0mu$}\hfil}\;;\;\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi^{\prime}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}\mkern 1.0mu,\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}\mkern 1.0mu,\mkern 1.0mu\ldots}_{\mathfrak{G}_{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}\big\}\mkern 1.0mu,\quad\ldots

at our disposal, we have to conceive of them as elements of a new
set
H
of objects having a single nature and 1) to carry out
the mapping
\{\mathfrak{G}_{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\mkern 1.0mu,\mathfrak{G}_{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}\mkern 1.0mu,\mkern 1.0mu\ldots\}\mapsto\textsf{{H}}
, assigning new representatives

|\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\rangle\in\textsf{{H}}
to the
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-brace, and 2)
to equip
H
with an equivalence relation

|\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\rangle\approx|\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}\rangle
(the concept
‘the same’ above). Let us implement all of that by the scheme

(
51
)

\begin{array}[]{r@{}l@{}c@{}r@{}l@{}r@{}ccc}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}&{}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\big[\begin{smallmatrix}\mu_{1}\\
\lambda_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\big]\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\uplus$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.5pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu$}\hfil}}\big[\begin{smallmatrix}\mu_{2}\\
\lambda_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\big]\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\uplus$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.5pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu$}\hfil}}\cdots&\quad\rightarrowtail&\big[\begin{smallmatrix}\mu_{1}\\
\lambda_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\big]\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\big[\begin{smallmatrix}\mu_{2}\\
\lambda_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\big]\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\cdots{}=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}&|\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\rangle&{}\in\textsf{{H}}\;,\\[8.61108pt]
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}&{}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\big[\begin{smallmatrix}\mu_{1}\\
\lambda_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\big]\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\uplus$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.5pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}\mkern-1.0mu$}\hfil}}\big[\begin{smallmatrix}\mu_{2}\\
\lambda_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\big]\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\uplus$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.5pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}\mkern-1.0mu$}\hfil}}\cdots&\quad\rightarrowtail&\big[\begin{smallmatrix}\mu_{1}\\
\lambda_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\big]\mkern 1.0mu|\beta_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\big[\begin{smallmatrix}\mu_{2}\\
\lambda_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\big]\mkern 1.0mu|\beta_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\cdots{}=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}&|\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}\rangle&{}\in\textsf{{H}}\;,\\[4.30554pt]
&\mathrel{}\ldots\ldots\ldots&\ldots\ldots&\ldots\ldots\ldots\mathrel{}\end{array}

In this, the new addition
\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}
must of course homomorphically
inherit operations
\uplus

\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu

,

\uplus

\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}\mkern-1.0mu

, …, and extension of this
definition throughout
H
is then made with the aid of the very
equivalence
\approx
:

|\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{\prime}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\rangle\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}|\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}\rangle=\Big||\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}\rangle\approx|\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\scalebox{1.3}[1]{\hbox{$\Rightarrow$}}}}\Big|=|\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{\prime}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\rangle\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}|\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\rangle=|\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{\prime}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}\rangle\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}|\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}\rangle\;.

Negation
\not\approx
of the relation
\approx
, e. g.,

|\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{\prime}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\rangle\not\approx|\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\rangle
, is
exactly the very same distinguishability that was discussed in
sects.
2
–
3
.

For the sake of convenience, we adopt the regular sign
=
for

\approx
, in order not to introduce yet a further homomorphism,
which are already numerous, with more to come. In other words, the
physics
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
is ‘concentrated’ in the sign
=
, turning the
empirical structures (
51
) into the
\mathscr{A}
-,

\mathscr{B}
-implementations of the object

|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle\equiv|\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\rangle=|\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}\rangle

under construction. The adequate term for it—the

Info/Data
-
Source

or
“representative of information” (Č. Brukner
(2014))—corresponds to the preliminary prototype of the concept
of a state, but we will remain within the standard term,
disregarding its variance.

6.
Quantum superposition

How come the quantum?

… No space, no time

— J. Wheeler (1989)

… postulation of something as a Primary

Observable is itself a sort of theoretical

act and may turn out to be wrong

— T. Maudlin

[
119
, p. 142]

6.1.
Representations of states

Let us simplify notation according to the rule

[\begin{smallmatrix}\mu\\[-1.07639pt]
\lambda\end{smallmatrix}]=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}\mathfrak{a}
.
The sought-for relationships between
\mathscr{A}
,
\mathscr{B}
, … then
turn into the key point of further construct—the equalities

(
52
)

\vbox{\hbox{\large$\Big\lceil$}}\mkern-5.0mu\begin{array}[]{c}\text{representations}\\
\text{of $|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle$\penalty-\hskip 0.0ptstate}\end{array}\mkern-5.0mu\vbox{\hbox{\large$\Big\rceil$}}:\qquad\mathfrak{a}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{a}_{2}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\cdots\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to30.5907pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scalebox{2}[1]{$=$}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\text{`the same'}$}}\mathfrak{b}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\beta_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{b}_{2}\mkern 1.0mu|\beta_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\cdots=\cdots\;.

They furnish
representations
|\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\rangle
,

|\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}\rangle
, … of quantum state
|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle
of
system
\mathcal{S}
. By design, this
DataSource
object

|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle
carries data
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
,

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}
and, more generally,
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-data
(
49
) from arrays of
any
observations, including the
imaginary ones. That is what eliminates the initial need for the

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
-brace (
26
) to come from the
observation
\mathscr{A}
, which is reflected in the shortening of the
term “representation of state” to simply “state”
|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle
. It
should be added that the straightforward storing of objects

\{|\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\rangle
,
|\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}\rangle
, …} in a
certain set
H
, but with the independence of operations
{
\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}^{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle(\mathscr{A})}
,
\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}^{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle(\mathscr{B})}
, …}
preserved, would not differ from the tautological substitution of
symbols. Accordingly, the semantic autonomy of

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-brace would also be inherited, whereas
covariance
III
requires an
elimination of precisely this autonomy. What is more, the
set-theoretic original copy for operations
{
\uplus

\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu

,

\uplus

\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}\mkern-1.0mu

, …} and
\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}
is one and the
same—the union
\cup
.

The symbols
|\alpha_{s}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
and
|\beta{}_{s}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
in (
52
) are
no more than symbols. Hence the objects’ property (
52
) of
being identical must be reflected in terms of their coordinate

\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}
-components (pt.
R
∙
).
This means that any aggregate
(\mathfrak{a}_{1}
,
\mathfrak{a}_{2}
, …) is
unambiguously calculated by means of a certain transformation

\widehat{U}
into any other
(\mathfrak{b}_{1}
,
\mathfrak{b}_{2}
, …) when the
two aggregates represent a common
|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle
:

(\mathfrak{a}_{1},\mathfrak{a}_{2},\ldots)=\widehat{U}(\mathfrak{b}_{1},\mathfrak{b}_{2},\ldots)\;.

The
\widehat{U}
then becomes an isomorphism between these
aggregates (a preimage of the future unitary transformation

[
27
, p. 14]
) and, accordingly, their lengths must coincide.
This length—a certain single constant—will be symbolized
as
D
.

6.2.
Representations of devices. Spectra

Naturally, instrument is converted to the
H
-structure
language along with
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-objects. It is a set of symbols

\{|\gamma_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
,
|\gamma_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
, …} in place of the previous

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\gamma\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}
,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\gamma\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}
, …}. As has just been shown,
their number for any
\mathscr{C}
-instrument should be equal to
D
.
However, generally speaking,

|{\mathfrak{T}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}|\neq|{\mathfrak{T}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}|
, since

{\mathfrak{T}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
and
{\mathfrak{T}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}
are assigned in an
arbitrary way (pt.
O
). Therefore if we take
an illustration
\mathscr{A}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}
,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\}
and

\mathscr{B}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}
,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}
,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{3}\}
, then

H
-representation of instrument
\mathscr{A}
should appear at
least as
\{|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
,
|\alpha_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
,
|\alpha_{3}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\}
.
Clearly, the already present distinguishability

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\not\approx{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}
(sect.
2.2
) is
automatically converted into an abstract distinguishability of new
symbols
|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\neq|\alpha_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
, and empirical

\mathscr{A}
-distinguishability is confined exclusively by these two
symbols. In that case, for the purpose of noncontradiction, the
added third symbol
|\alpha_{3}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
, as an adjunction to the
abstract relations
|\alpha_{3}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\neq|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
and

|\alpha_{3}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\neq|\alpha_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
, should be complemented with the
notion of its physical
in
discernibility from
|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}

or
|\alpha_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
. By an extension of this argument, one gets that
every
\mathscr{A}
-instrument should be endowed with the
(non)equivalence relation (
{\Bumpeq}/{\not\Bumpeq}
) in terms of
the
H
-structure by its formal

\{|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}},\ldots\}
-representations. How do we do this?

Let us proceed further from a self-suggested extension of
pt.
R
. Let us declare—and it is more than
natural—that the number representations
\alpha_{s}
are linked
not only to observations but to instruments as well. Each
\alpha_{s}

is the new object of a numerical type: a number or a collection of
numbers. Then, indiscernibility, say

|\alpha_{3}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\bumpeq|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
, is recorded by coincidence of
the numeral labels
\alpha_{3}=\alpha_{1}
attached to the symbols

|\alpha_{3}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
and
|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
respectively. The abstract
(‘old’) distinguishability
|\alpha_{3}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\neq|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
,
meanwhile, remains as it is. From these we have the following
formalization of the relationship between
\bumpeq
and
=
by means
of dropping/adding the brackets
|\ \vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
:

(
53
)

\left.\begin{aligned} |\alpha_{s}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\not\bumpeq|\alpha_{k}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\quad&\mathrel{\hbox{\scalebox{1.3}[1]{\hbox{$\Leftarrow\mkern-13.0mu\Rightarrow$}}}}\quad\alpha_{s}\neq\alpha_{k}\\
|\alpha_{s}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\bumpeq|\alpha_{k}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\quad&\mathrel{\hbox{\scalebox{1.3}[1]{\hbox{$\Leftarrow\mkern-13.0mu\Rightarrow$}}}}\quad\alpha_{s}=\alpha_{k}\end{aligned}\right\}\quad\text{under}\quad|\alpha_{s}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\neq|\alpha_{k}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\;.

Call the quantity
\alpha_{s}
(numerical)
spectral
label/marker
of eigen-element
|\alpha_{s}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
. Then, by the

H
-
representation
\boldsymbol{[}\mathscr{A}\boldsymbol{]}
of instrument

\mathscr{A}
we will mean the set of objects
\big\{|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
,
…,
|\alpha_{\textsf{D}}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\big\}
supplemented with the spectral
structure (
53
):

(
54
)

\boldsymbol{[}\mathscr{A}\boldsymbol{]}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\big\{|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\lfloor\mkern-1.0mu}\alpha_{1}},\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\lfloor\mkern-1.0mu}\alpha_{2}},\mkern 1.0mu\ldots\big\}\;.

It is not difficult to see that if

|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\not\bumpeq|\alpha_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
then either

|\alpha_{3}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\bumpeq|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
or

|\alpha_{3}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\bumpeq|\alpha_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
. Otherwise, spectral markers

\vbox{\hbox{$\lfloor$}}\mkern-1.0mu\alpha_{1}=\vbox{\hbox{$\lfloor$}}\mkern-1.0mu\alpha_{2}
should
coincide, and primary primitives

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\not\approx{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}
lose their empirical
distinguishability in contrast with (
7
). Multiple
coincidence of
\vbox{\hbox{$\lfloor$}}\mkern-1.0mu\alpha_{s}
-markers is admissible.

In the presence of relations (
53
), it is natural to state
that instrument
\mathscr{A}
is coarser (more symmetrical) than
\mathscr{B}

and, terminologically, to declare that the degeneration of the
spectral-label values takes place. In cases of embeddability like

\mathscr{A}_{2}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}
,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}\}\subset\mathscr{A}_{3}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}
,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{2}
,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{3}\}
,
instrument
\mathscr{A}_{2}
can even be called the same as (coinciding
with)
\mathscr{A}_{3}
, but with a more rough scale. Or conversely,

\mathscr{A}_{3}
is a more precise extension of
\mathscr{A}_{2}
. In particular,
the natural notion of a device resolution fits here.

All instruments may then be mathematically imagined as having the
same resolution, but, perhaps, with degeneration of spectra. The
non-coinciding instruments may be interpreted as non-equivalent
reference frames
\mathscr{A}\neq\mathscr{B}
in an observation space.
According to pts.
R
∙∙
and

III
, they are mandatorily present
in the description. The spectral degenerations are also always
present, since element
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}
can always be removed from

{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
, and there are no logical foundations to
prohibit an observational instrument with family

{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}-\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\}
. Hence it follows that
introducing the spectra—instrumental readings—is required
even formally, without physics. It is of course implied here that
spectral (in)discernibility is realized in the same manner as its
statistical counterpart in sects.
2.6
and
4
, i. e., by
numbers. Incidentally, such a property of

\vbox{\hbox{$\lfloor$}}\mkern-1.0mu\alpha_{s}
—i. e., of being a numerical object—is
not at all necessary at the moment. Spectrum

\{\vbox{\hbox{$\lfloor$}}\mkern-1.0mu\alpha_{1},\vbox{\hbox{$\lfloor$}}\mkern-1.0mu\alpha_{2},\ldots\}
may
be thought of as an abstract set of labels attached to the
eigen-elements. As numbers, it is introduced for the subsequent
creation of models to classical/macroscopic dynamic, and they are
numerical.

Returning to
D
, we note that, in any case, the toolkit

\{\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B},\ldots\}=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}\mathcal{O}
in real use has always been
defined, fixed, and is finite. Consequently, the constant

(
55
)

\textsf{D}\geqslant 2

has also been defined and fixed, and it becomes the globally static
observable characteristic—an empirically external parameter.
Meanwhile, the entire scheme internally contains the natural method
of its own extension
\textsf{D}\mapsto\textsf{D}+1
, and the potentially
all-encompassing choice
\textsf{D}=\infty
may be considered the
universally preferable one in
QT
. By freezing the different

\textsf{D}<\infty
, the theory makes it possible to create models, and
they are not only admissible but also well-known. Their efficiency
is examined in experiments. Once again,

•

the
D
-constant, the concept of spectra, and their
degenerations are created by the (
\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B}
)-covariance
requirement, i. e., by
principium
III
.

As a result, the structure of
H
-representations of states and
of instruments are liberated from the arbitrariness in assigning the
subsets
{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
in (
8
). The statistical
unitary pre-images (
36
) and
H
-elements of the form

\mathfrak{c}\mkern 1.0mu|\gamma_{s}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
can be associated with any ‘eigen symbol’

|\gamma_{s}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
. They are always available because every possible
brace (
34
) are known to contain subfamilies when ingoing

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-primitives get to a single one, e. g., to

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\gamma\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}
. Therefore every representation

\mathfrak{a}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\cdots
is always equivalent to a

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}}
-brace for some observation
\mathscr{C}

with a homogeneous outgoing ensemble

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\gamma\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\gamma\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{1}\}
. That is, one may always
write

(
56
)

\mathfrak{a}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{a}_{2}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\cdots=\mathfrak{c}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\gamma_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}0\mkern 1.0mu|\gamma_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\cdots=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}\mathfrak{c}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\gamma_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\;,

while naturally referring to
\mathfrak{c}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\gamma_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
as one of
the
eigen-states of instrument

\mathscr{C}
, with an appropriate
adjusting the similar definition in pt.
O
.
The construction of the representation-state space is far from being
complete, since it is still a ‘bare’ semigroup
H
.

6.3.
Superposition of states

Since writings (
52
) exist for any ensemble
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-brace,
let us consider the following two representations:

(
57
)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{a}_{2}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{b}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\beta_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{b}_{2}\mkern 1.0mu|\beta_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\cdots\;,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}_{2}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{b}_{1}^{\prime}\mkern 1.0mu|\beta_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{b}_{2}^{\prime}\mkern 1.0mu|\beta_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\cdots\;.

Comparison of these equalities tells us that the second one is a
solution of the first one with respect to

\mathfrak{a}_{2}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
. Hence, the semigroup operation
\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}

admits a cancellation of element
\mathfrak{a}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
. This
means that there exists an
H
-element

\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
such that

\displaystyle\big\{\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{a}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\big\}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{a}_{2}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\big\{\mathfrak{b}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\beta_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{b}_{2}\mkern 1.0mu|\beta_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\cdots\big\}

\displaystyle\kern 0.55557pt\mkern 1.0mu\Downarrow

\displaystyle 0\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{a}_{2}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{b}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\beta_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{b}_{2}\mkern 1.0mu|\beta_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\cdots

\displaystyle\kern 0.55557pt\mkern 1.0mu\Downarrow\quad\text{(due to \eqref{eigen})}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}_{2}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{b}_{1}^{\prime}\mkern 1.0mu|\beta_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{b}_{2}^{\prime}\mkern 1.0mu|\beta_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\cdots

\displaystyle\Downarrow\qquad\quad

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\Downarrow

\displaystyle\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$|0\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=0\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}=\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{a}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}$}\mkern 1.0mu,\quad

\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{b}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\beta_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\cdots=\mathfrak{b}_{1}^{\prime}\mkern 1.0mu|\beta_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\cdots\;,

where
|0\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
stands for a zero in semigroup
H
(image

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}0\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
of the finite-length brace
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
) and 0 in

0\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
is a symbol of its
[\lambda,\mu]
-coordinates.
By canceling out
\mathfrak{a}_{s}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{s}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
, one by one, if
necessary, one deduces that any element of
H
does have an
inversion. That is,
H
is actually a group. We re-denote
inverse elements
\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{s}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{s}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
by

(-\mathfrak{a}_{s})\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{s}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
and inversions of sums are formed from

(\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}})
-sums thereof. Moreover, all the
[\lambda,\mu]
-pairs
turn into a set
\{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\ldots\}
equipped with the above
mentioned composition
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}
, which follows from an obvious
property of unitary brace:

(
58
)

\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{b}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}=(\mathfrak{a}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}\mathfrak{b})\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}

(inheritance of clossedness under the
\cup
-operation). This
composition is also a
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}
-operation of a group and of a
commutative one:

(
59
)

\mathfrak{a}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}\mathfrak{b}=\mathfrak{b}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad(\mathfrak{a}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}\mathfrak{b})\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}\mathfrak{c}=\mathfrak{a}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}(\mathfrak{b}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}\mathfrak{c})\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\mathfrak{a}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}0=\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\mathfrak{a}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}(-\mathfrak{a})=0\;.

Therefore the group nature of semigroup
H
and the group
(
59
) come from the scheme

\boxed{\begin{array}[]{c}\displaystyle\bigg\lceil\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\begin{array}[]{c}\text{single observations}\\
\text{$\mathscr{A}$, $\mathscr{B}$, \ldots}\end{array}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\bigg\rceil\quad\mathrel{\hbox{\scalebox{1.3}[1]{\hbox{$\Rightarrow$}}}}\quad\bigg\lceil\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\begin{array}[]{c}\text{semigroups}\\
\text{$\mathfrak{G}_{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}$,
$\mathfrak{G}_{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}$, \ldots}\end{array}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\bigg\rceil\quad\rightarrowtail\\
\\
\hskip 30.00005pt\displaystyle\rightarrowtail\quad\bigg\lceil\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\begin{array}[]{c}(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\text{-covariance},\\
\text{{\hbox to0.0pt{$\langle$}\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}$,}\thinspace\hbox{$\mathbf{M}$,}\thinspace\hbox{\ldots\hskip-1.0pt}\hbox to0.0pt{$\rangle$}\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu\rangle$}}\ and principium\penalty\kern 2.77802pt\ignorespaces\penalty\hskip 0.0pt\hyperlink{III}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{III}}}}\end{array}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\bigg\rceil\quad\mathrel{\hbox{\scalebox{1.3}[1]{\hbox{$\Rightarrow$}}}}\quad\big\lceil\text{\kern 0.55557ptgroup {{H}}\kern 0.55557pt\kern 0.55557pt}\big\rceil\end{array}}

and, technically, from equatings/identifyings (
52
), i. e., from
conception ‘the same’ (sect.
5.4
). For its part, it is this
very structure of algebraic operations—the 2-, 3-term (and
nothing else) axioms of commutativity/associativity, i. e., the
group—that comes from properties (
41
). All of this provides
an answer to the key question: where do the (semi)group and the
minus sign come from and why?

Thus handling the
|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle
-objects breaks free from its ties to the
notion of observation, and the objects admit the formal writings

\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{b}\mkern 1.0mu|\Phi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\cdots
. Call them

superpositions
. However, as
soon as they or the state are associated in meaning with the word
‘readings’ (this is discussed at greater length in sects.
6.4

and
6.5
), this term should be replaced with a non-truncated
one—representation of the state with respect to a certain
observation. Specifically, the statistical weights
\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{j}
are
extracted from such expressions only after their conversion into a
sum over eigen-states of the form (
52
); a task of the
subsequent mathematical tool.

No superposition

\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{b}\mkern 1.0mu|\Phi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\cdots
, including
(
56
), has any physical sense in and of itself

[
160
, p. 137]
,
[
50
]
nor is it preferable to any
other one. It merely mirrors the closedness of states with respect
to operation
\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}
, since any
|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle
is re-recorded as a sum
of various {
\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
,
\mathfrak{b}\mkern 1.0mu|\Phi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
, …} in a
countless number of ways and is linked to any other such sum.
Without a system of
|\alpha_{s}\mkern-1.0mu\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
-symbols for instrument

\mathscr{A}
, nothing observable is extractable out of the aggregate of
coefficients
\{\pm\mathfrak{a}
,
\pm\mathfrak{b}
, …} (and, of course, of
the
|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
-letters themselves) in any imaginable way.
Accordingly, it is incorrect to speak of—a widespread
misconception—the destruction of the superposition or of the
“relative-phase information”
[
152
, p. 253]
, associating the
word destruction with the physical/observational meanings or
processes.

As a result,
even without having a numerical theory yet
and
without recourse to the concept of a physical
quantity—superposition may not address whatever physical
concepts, we arrive at the paramount property, which characterizes
the most general type of micro-observation’s
ensembles (
17
).

•

Superposition principle

A
(\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}})
-composition of quantum states
\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
and

\mathfrak{b}\mkern 1.0mu|\Phi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
, which are admissible for system
\mathcal{S}
, is an
admissible state

(
60
)

\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{b}\mkern 1.0mu|\Phi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}=\mathfrak{c}\mkern 1.0mu|\Xi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}

and, with that, the set
\big\{\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}},\mathfrak{b}\mkern 1.0mu|\Phi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}},\mathfrak{c}|\Xi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}},\ldots\big\}=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}\textsf{{H}}
forms a commutative group
with respect to operation
\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}
. The family

\{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\ldots\}
of coordinate
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
-representatives of
states (
52
) is also equipped with the same group structure
under the
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}
-operation (
59
) and with the rule of
carrying the operation
\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}
over to
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}
:

(
61
)

\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{b}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}=(\mathfrak{a}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}\mathfrak{b})\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\;.

Let us clarify the transferring (
58
) to (
61
). The union
of the state prototypes
\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
,

\mathfrak{b}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\in\textsf{{H}}
is known to belong to
\mathfrak{G}
. So
the composition
\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{b}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
should be
identical to a certain element
\mathfrak{c}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\in\textsf{{H}}
. It is
clear that
\mathfrak{c}
depends on
\mathfrak{a}
,
\mathfrak{b}
and, hence,

\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{b}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}=\mathfrak{c}(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b})\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
. The exhaustive properties of
dependence
\mathfrak{c}(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b})
are given by formulas
(
59
) and (
61
) under notation

\mathfrak{c}(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b})=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}(\mathfrak{a}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}\mathfrak{b})
.

6.3.1.
‘Physics’ of superposition

Besides the essentially unphysical nature of the
(
\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}
)-superpositions—“we cannot recognize them”

[
50
, p. 13]
, the primary and salient property of quantum
addition is in the fact that, due to the group subtraction, it is
possible the experimental obtainment of a ‘quantum zero’ in
statistics from ‘non-zeroes’. With that, these ‘seem to be’
positive, but there are ‘negative non-zeroes’—negative numbers
(sect.
9.2
). Subtraction manifests by the typical obscurations
in interference pictures. S. Aaronson adds to this: “We’ve got minus
signs, and so we’ve got interference”
[
2
, p. 220]
. No
classical composition

(
62
)

w\mkern 1.0mu\varrho_{1}+(1-w)\mkern 1.0mu\varrho_{2}

of non-zero statistics
\varrho_{1}
,
\varrho_{2}
can provide a
zero value, since the zero will never be obtained via the

\cup
-unions. The same is true for the pre-superposition in
isolated brace
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
, i. e., when one instrument is
in question.

Remark
12
.

One cannot but mention yet another counterexample to the
superposition’s ‘physicality’—the (in)famous ‘quantum cat’. Any
combination of the dead and living animal is meaningless as a
statement about new/nonclassical entity such as a ‘(half-)dead/alive
cat’ or such statements about particles as ‘their being neither here
nor there but everywhere’; especially with the stress on ‘at the
same point in time’ (see pr.
I
). It
makes absolutely no sense to add (allegedly in accord with the
character
+
) to each other the nature’s phenomena and notions that
have not yet been created and are dynamical (‘alive’) at that.

•

What’s being added is states, not their
denominations

or verbal descriptions of envisioned

(‘fantasized’, “fantastic phantoms”
[
50
, p. 15]
)

physical properties
such as spin up/down or dead/alive.
Cf.
[
119
, pp. 134(!), 135]
.

The ‘cat-box open’ is a click, not state; without a notion of “a
cat”. Accordingly, the word combination “the quantum objects exist
in “superpositions” of different possibilities” (a representative
excerpt from the literature) is at most an interpretative allegory
(sect.
10
) without physical and mathematical content.
That is to say, strictly speaking,

•

no quantum (micro)system has ever been/dwelled in any
state, much less in a superposition one, and much less at an
instant
t
. Ludwig, on pp. 16 and 78 of the book
[
113
]
,
insists that it is a “myth” and “a fairy tale, … the very
widespread idea that each microsystem has a real state … represented by a vector in a Hilbert space”, and M. Nielsen remarks
in
[
133
]
that “Saying
0.6\mkern 1.0mu|\boldsymbol{}0\rangle+0.8\mkern 1.0mu|\boldsymbol{}1\rangle
is
simultaneously
0
and
1
makes about as much sense as Lewis
Carroll’s nonsense poem
Jabberwocky
: …”. K. Svozil does
also underscore that “‘coherent superpositions’ just correspond to
improper, misleading representations of non-existing aspects of
physical reality. They are delusive because they confuse ontology
with epistemology”
[
172
, p. 26]
.

The meaning of the word ‘add’ is still being created, including an
implementation at objects to be thought of as the ‘atomic
irreducible’ entities—the numbers (sect.
7
).

T. Maudlin notes on p. 133 of the work
[
119
]
: “Our job … is to invent mathematical representations …, rather than merely
linguistic terms such as “
z
-up.” … we are in some danger of

confusing physical items with mathematical items
” (italics
supplied). Here is an example of confusion. If we are going to
measure the
z
-spin in one of the
(\rightleftarrows_{x})
-beams in a
Stern–Gerlach device, then why and when does this
‘
observable
’ certainty—say

|\boldsymbol{}{\rightarrow}_{x}\rangle
—get turned into a

(\uparrow\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\downarrow_{z})
-uncertainty
|\boldsymbol{}{\uparrow}\rangle+|\boldsymbol{}{\downarrow}\rangle
? (see
[
14
, p. 232]
). But what if
we are about not to do this? We come up against the question:

•

What does one mean by an equal-sign
=
in the orthodox
notation
|\boldsymbol{}{\rightarrow}\rangle=|\boldsymbol{}{\uparrow}\rangle+|\boldsymbol{}{\downarrow}\rangle
?

Which state does the system ‘intend’ to fall into: the

z
-uncertainty or the
x
-determinacy? Which of the states is it
in, after all? Examples to the ‘physicality of states’ may be
continued endlessly
[
18
]
.

A statement about
QM
-superposition (without
{\mathbb{C}}
-numbers) as a
non-independent axiom can be found in the book
[
89
, p. 108]

but arguments given there are circular:
\lceil
Hilbert space
\rceil

\rightarrowtail

\lceil
quantum logic of propositions
\rceil

\rightarrowtail

\lceil
superposition principle
\rceil
. Similarly, in
the works
[
137
]
and
[
21
, p. 164]
, all of that
is ‘derived’ from modular lattices
[
24
]
. However, the
lattices are known to enter
QM
from the Hilbert space structure
and, on the other hand, the purging quantum rudiments of such a
space’ axiomatics constitutes Birkhoff’s 110-th problem

[
24
, p.  286]
. Note also that, in connection with the formal
logic approaches to the theory construction
[
21
,
59
,
89
,
103
,
137
,
179
,
192
]
, the issue of
vindicating the
matters
that this logic deals
with

should not be neglected. What me mean here is the questions: logic
of what?
[
113
,
111
,
163
]
of propositions?

[
96
,

]
of relations? of (math-logic) classes/sets?

[

]
of phenomena/properties? (which ones?) of
quantum/classical events? …? “For example, would one have to
develop a quantum set theory?”
[
46
, p. 17]
. “If by
“logic” we mean something like “correct reasoning,” then it would
make no sense to think of logic as “just another theory.””

[

, p. 258]
. The more so as the abstract micro-events and

[
