.

6.3.2.
What is non-commutativity? Whence this structure?

Yet another fact that results from the above constructs is that the
availability of a superposition math-structure (
60
) reflects
the presence of at least
two

\mathscr{A}
,
\mathscr{B}
with

non-coinciding
families of eigen-primitives

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}\}
,
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{k}\}
. This consequence of
pt.
R
∙∙
should be particularly
emphasized, since it will manifest in the
non-commutativity

of operators
{}\kern 2.16684pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.24329pt{}\mkern 17.0mu}\mkern-17.0mu}{}$}\kern-2.16684pt{}\mathscr{A}
and
{}\kern 5.58345pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.24329pt{}\mkern 6.0mu}\mkern-6.0mu}{}$}\kern-5.58345pt{}\mathscr{B}
in the future. Although the present
work does not get to operators as a mathematical structure, it is
clear that the emergent eigen-states and spectra have a direct
bearing on them. In this context, the ‘commuting instruments’

\{|\alpha_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}},|\alpha_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}},\ldots\}=\{|\beta_{1}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}},|\beta_{2}\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}},\ldots\}
can be treated, roughly
speaking, as coinciding, because this fact is independent of the
specific spectrums
\{\vbox{\hbox{$\lfloor$}}\mkern-1.0mu\alpha_{1},\vbox{\hbox{$\lfloor$}}\mkern-1.0mu\alpha_{2},\ldots\}
,
\{\vbox{\hbox{$\lfloor$}}\mkern-1.0mu\beta_{1},\vbox{\hbox{$\lfloor$}}\mkern-1.0mu\beta_{2},\ldots\}
assigned to them. If they differ,
this is merely a different (numerical) graduation of the spectrum
scale. It is the same for all instruments, and its length is the
parameter
D
.

Notice that definition of an
\mathscr{A}
-observation is not different
from the formal assignment of the family
{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}

(pt.
O
and (
8
)), which is why the
non-coinciding sets
{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
,
{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}
do
always exist. This provides a kind of abstractly deductive
existence’s proof for the non-commutativity,

QM
-interference—see sect.
6.5
further below—and for
the utmost low-level finality of
QM
altogether
[
111
,
66
,
9
]
. The whys and wherefores of theory do not
require invoking the physical conceptions; cf.
[
104
, p. 2]
.

Of no small importance is that this point entails an independence of
the (existence/presence of) classical physics or of its formal
deformation, which are yet to be created from the quantum one (cf. a
selected thesis on page
•
‣
2.6
). In particular, no use
is required of the notion of a certain pretty small—again the
classical/physical term—quantity, i. e., the Plank
constant
\hbar

[
25
, sect. 6.5]
. (Parenthetically, no
the numerical value of this constant matters here; the more so as it
is not dimensionless and its zero limit is not meaningful.) What is
more, the quantum paradigm (
17
)–(
19
) tells us
that the classical description begins—i. e., we do
create/introduce—with the notions of a micro-event’s average and
of time, whereas these conceptions are still absent at the moment
and in the present work. Similarly for the notions of locality,
causality, the classical event, and the classical object.

6.4.
Physical properties

Now, the ‘general physics’
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
is mathematized into representations
(
52
) of states
|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle
of system
\mathcal{S}
. There is,
however, an ambiguity, the source of which is the fact that the
natural/classical language also lays claims to a similar
formulation. This refers to the belief in the existence of
mathematics (‘bad habit’
[
123
]
; see also
[
71
]
,

[
113
, p. 122]
, and
[
122
]
) that describes
\mathcal{S}

as an individual object with properties regardless of observation;
an observation that is not a
functioning
attribute of the
mathematics itself. In classical description, it is specified by
definitions: point
\mathcal{P}
of a phase space,

(q,p)
-coordinatization of the point (manifold), and statistical
distribution
\varrho(q,p)
.

On the other hand, quantum empiricism provides nothing more to us
besides the ensemble brace and
|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle
-states
(pt.
T
). Preordained definienda with
physical contents are unacceptable, i. e.,
\mathcal{S}
should not be
conceived as ‘something with
physical
properties’ or as an
‘
individual
object’
[
134
, p. 645]
,
[
111
,
112
]
. However, since the observational data (in the broadest
sense of the word) may not originate from anywhere but a certain

|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle
-object, there should subsequently create

1)

the very concept of physical objects and properties

[
182
, pp. 211–230]
and

2)

their numerical values/characteristics, i. e., the “physical
attributes of objects”
[
149
, p. 238; N. Bohr]
.

This is habitually referred to as elements/images of reality

[
70
, pp. 194]
,
[
6
, sect. 10.2]
,

[
112
, sect. XIII.4.8]
—Bell’s “beables”
[
20
]
—or
what we have been calling attributes of a physical system.

•

“The very notion of ’phenomenon’ or of ’the appearance of things,’
… is a cognitive and perceptual act of abstraction”

.
M. Wartofsky
[
182
, p. 220]

That is to say, the physical phenomena per se do not exist

[
30
]
,
[
110
, p. 310]
.

Indeed, the primary ideology of sects.
1.3
and
2.1

tells us that an invasion of physically self-apparent images into
the theory should be avoided
[
109
, p. 69]
, because “quantum
theory not only does not use—it does not even dare to
mention—the notion of a “real physical situation””

[
70
, p. 198; E. Jaynes]
. Continuing a quotation from
R. Haag on page
•
‣
1.3
, one requires “the renunciation of
the absolute significance of conventional physical attributes of
objects”
[
149
, p. 238; N. Bohr]
and of concomitant and
accustomed logic in reasoning. In fact, we are led to (re)build
the language of the classical description. Therefore everything,
with no exceptions, should be created mathematically: coordinates,
momenta, energies, optical spectra, device readings,
lengths/distances and time, extension and lifetime of objects, the
language of particles, their number/numeration (Fock space),
(in)discernibility/individuality (bosons/fermions), the notions of
a subsystem of system
\mathcal{S}
(see (
3.1
)), and even a notion
of the physical rigor (in reasoning), etc.

Degrees of freedom, the concepts of the
field/body/mass/inertia/interaction, Newtonian mechanics with its
equations and the concepts of the force, interaction, and the
causality of classical events, thermodynamics, the very term ‘the
classical state’, the numerical labels of the space-time continuum
and numerical forms of what is known as the classical reference
frames—coordinates on manifolds—need to also be created.
Once more to underscore, the numerical forms of the classical space
coordinates and the time (e. g., the metric tensor

g_{\alpha\beta}(x)\mkern 1.0mudx^{\alpha}dx^{\beta}
) have a quantum empirical
origin. The latter fact is required for carefully posing the
questions of quantum gravity, and it should be noted in passing that
the simultaneity is an ill-defined term not only in the (general)
relativity theory; in
QT
it is even worse. In common with the
simultaneous measurability, this term appears to have come from the
classical framework, which is why it is illegal as a
quantum-theoretical primitive (pr.
I

and
[
109
]
).

6.4.1.
Waves/particles?

The concept of a (non-elementary) particle, which is conceptually
close to the notion of a subsystem/part, is also a physical
convention and can only arise from the
|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle
or its models:
Bose-condensates, deformation excitations in crystal lattices,
quasi-particles in a superfluid phase, quantum theories of various
fields (relativistic or non), and more. By a particle is meant here
the classical kinematic conception. “What do we detect? The presence
of a particle? Or the occurrence of a microscopic event?”, wondered
R. Haag (2013). H. Zeh and G. Ludwig do answer: “There are no
particles in reality”
[
188
]
, “we must abandon the notion of a
microscopic “object”, one to which we have been accustomed”

[
109
, p. 69]
.

Clearly, the
QFT
s is a subclass of
QM
rather than its
extension; not that we have yet given a definition of
QM
. In
particular, it is common knowledge in
QFT
that there is no logical
way to distinguish a particle from a certain state—normally, a
vacuum excitation. One word should therefore be used for both. To
this extent, the familiar “dualism of … the
particle
picture
and the
wave picture
”
[
139
, p. 28]
,

[
87
, sect. 7.2]
,
[
88
]
simply disappears.
K. Popper is rather emphatic concerning this ‘problem’ and puts it,
in his “thirteen theses”
[
139
]
, quite rightly in the
following terms: “
the great quantum muddle
”, “alleged
“duality” or “complementarity”, …
this
kind of
“understanding” is of little value”, “has not the slightest bearing
on either physics, …”, “fashionable among quantum theorists, … a vicious doctrine”, and the like. As a matter of fact, both the
particles and waves are the classical terms
[
84
]
and, in
quantum language, they turn into the derivatives of the concepts of
state and mixture (
3.1
).

•

Like waves,
the particle is already an appearance
—an
observable one (phenomenology, derivative)—rather than a logical
primitive or a fundamental substance, which is why it may not exist

[
188
]
prior to theory’s principles
[
178
, p. 762(!)]
.
Paraphrasing Heisenberg, Haag remarks, in the context of his “event
theory”, that “Particles are the roof of the theory, not its
foundation”
[
74
, p. 300]
.

Both these notions should be superseded by a mathematics of clicks.

The
\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}
-statistics also falls under observable quantities, and
constant
D
, if declared finite, is an example of an already
created characteristic; the dimension of a state space to come. A
tensorial structure of this space—compound systems—also
pertains to the physical properties but we do not touch upon this
point here. As an aside, this compositional structure will provide
the means of distinguishing the aforementioned models under

\textsf{D}=\infty
.

In other words, the logic of the above constructs prohibits not only
endowing the phraseology ‘internal state of an individual object

\mathcal{S}
’ and ‘the system is in a (definite) state’

[
111
]
–
[
113
]
,
[
129
]
with a meaning, but
also indirectly using its numerical forms. That would work in the
circumvention of empiricism, assuming the a priori availability of
mathematical structures that do not rest on the state space.
L. Ballentine remarks in this regard: “the habit of considering an
individual particle to have its own wave function is hard to break”

[
15
, p. 238]
; cf. “To speak of a single possible
initial apparatus state is pure fantasy”
[
43
, pp. 241–242;
N. Graham]
.

6.5.
Interference

Let us go on with comments as to involving the

physics
-related argumentation to explicate of the quantal
behavior. We have already mentioned above that for this purpose
there is simply no language of physics (sects.
2.1
,
6.4
)
and of mathematics yet (sects.
2.3
,
5
). That
is why analogies of this sort are not only deceptive, but must be
prohibited for exactly the same reasons that accompanied boxes
(
5
). The typical examples in this connection are the
simultaneous measurability mentioned above and the 2-slit
interference
[
160
,
3
]
.

First and foremost, the two cases—whether one or two slits are
open—are the utterly “different experimental arrangements”

[
14
, p. 236]
,
[
202
, p. 58]
:

{\hbox to0.0pt{$\langle$}\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}$,}\thinspace\hbox{$\mathbf{M}$,}\thinspace\hbox{\ldots\hskip-1.0pt}\hbox to0.0pt{$\rangle$}\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu\rangle$}}\mkern-1.0mu^{\prime}\neq{\hbox to0.0pt{$\langle$}\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}$,}\thinspace\hbox{$\mathbf{M}$,}\thinspace\hbox{\ldots\hskip-1.0pt}\hbox to0.0pt{$\rangle$}\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu\rangle$}}\mkern-1.0mu^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\;.

There is nowhere to seek a means of their comparison or the
transference of one into another
[
14
, p. 236]
.
Nonetheless, the classical approach, when opening another slit

\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
\mkern-1.0mu{}_{2}^{\prime}
together with the first one
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
\mkern-1.0mu{}_{1}^{\prime}
, does literally
envision properties for
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}
(see sect.
6.4
). In doing
so, the transference method itself—‘addition of the two 1-slit

\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime}
-physicae’ by the rule of arithmetical addition of
statistics (
62
)—is meanwhile considered self-apparent.
Thus, natural questions arise, such as ‘why/where are the zeroes
coming from, they shouldn’t be there’. In accordance with the
aforesaid, everything here is erroneous, including the ‘natural’
questions. There are no rules at the outset—(non)classical and
even quantum, just as there is no addition per se. An a priori
assumption that stem from the obvious images for
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
\mkern-1.0mu{}_{1}^{\prime}
and

\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
\mkern-1.0mu{}_{2}^{\prime}
is actually a declaration of the physical properties for

\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
\mkern-1.0mu{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}
, but they do not follow from anywhere
[
202
, p. 55]
,

[
173
]
,
[
3
]
. The (illegal) assumption of the
’negligible effect of the which-slit detectors’ were mentioned on
p.
5.4
is identical with a declaration of a physical
property; as well as a solenoid’s switch-on/off in the
Aharonov–Bohm effect.

Taken alone, the
\varrho
-distributions—separate for

\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
\mkern-1.0mu{}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
\mkern-1.0mu{}_{2}^{\prime}
—are entirely correct observational
pictures, but introducing the rule (
62
) is indistinguishable
from ‘invention’ of physics; a logically prohibited operation. Or,
as Slavnov had put it, “to invent the physical exegesis of a … mathematical scheme”
[
199
, p. 304]
. “Our custom of seeing
classical mechanics as a no-nonsense description of ’reality as it
is’ does not seem to be justified. This custom is actually based on
a confusion of categories …” (W. de Muynck
[
129
, p. 89]
).
In other words, the mere fact of non-adherence to this rule means
that the grammatical conjunction of the verbs ‘to understand/deduce’
with the noun ‘micro-phenomena’ is unacceptable even linguistically.
It is the point
T
that prohibits predefined
(classical) semantics, and this was faithfully summarized by
C. Fuchs: “badly calibrated linguistics is the predominant reason
for quantum foundations continuing to exist as a field of research”

[
63
, p. xxxix]
. To figure out or deduce (from mathematics)
that quantal phenomena are unfeasible
[
160
, p. 111]
and
are “
absolutely
impossible, to explain in any classical way”
(quotation by Feynman). Just as with the elucidation of the nature
of the quantum state on p.
4.2
, any (circum-)classical
justification or even motivation are guaranteed to fail here, since
they are based upon significant and implicit assumptions.

The classical theory is a theory of
observational
objects
with
observational
properties expressed by

observational
numbers. We possess none of the three items
required to create the quantum (
=
correct) description
(sect.
2
). The adjective ‘observational’ itself is a
linguistic notion of the classical vocabulary (sect.
2.2
).
Accordingly, the description can only be changed into the ‘to
describe in newly created terms’. A. Leggett notes
[
105
]

that which is understood as common-sense should also be changed (see
also
[
50
, p. 10]
). The reason is clear.

•

Common-sense operates—and that is perfectly normal—with
observational categories rather than with structureless
‘microscopy’ (
9
) and
\cup
-abstractions of
sect.
5.1
;

cf. Bohr’s correspondence principle
[
87
]
. In effect, we
have dealt with a ‘fundamental chasm’ between the right
description—“what is really going on?”

[
50
, p. 12]
—and our ability to give a (naturally
speaking) explanation in terms of these categories:

“All our intuition, all our sense of what constitutes concreteness
are based upon our everyday experience, and the terms used to
describe a phenomenon concretely are necessarily drawn from that
experience. There is no indication that such a language could be
used without contradictions for phenomena which are as far removed
from it as those of microscopic physics” (A. Messiah.
Quantum
mechanics
).

The total dismissal of this very concrete has to be at the heart of
quantum reconstructing.

6.5.1.
Detector micro-events

For similar reasons, we may not think or envision that a particle in
an interferometer ‘flies through the slit’, ‘has (not) arrived’, “is
located somewhere in the region of space”
[
44
, p. 7]
, ‘here,
not there’, ‘now/later’, that ‘the choice of a detector has been
delayed’
[
70
, Wheeler]
,
[
186
]
, or that a
“photon … interferes … with itself”
[
44
, p. 9]
, and
that, generally, ‘something is flying along a trajectory’, and
‘something’ is a particle at an intuitive understanding. Cf. Dirac’s
description of “the translational states of a photon” in sect. 3 of

[
44
]
.

•

“Photons are just clicks in photon detectors; nothing real is
traveling from the source to the detector” (ascribed to
A. Zeilinger),

and this point is supported by all the known varieties of
interferometers. There has to be an amendment here.

The clicks themselves are the clicks not
of
photons/particles
, just ‘merely clicks’. “[T]he click is no … produced by a particle. … nothing takes place in the source that
could be a cause of the click …, the genuinely fortuitous click
comes without a cause and has no precursor”
[
178
, pp. 758,
765]
. Nothing really interferes inside interferometers, nor
is anything superposed/reinforced. For example, that the path of
‘photons’ is not represented by trajectories was impressively
demonstrated with the nested Mach–Zehnder experimental setup in the
work
[
39
]
. Asking “where the photons have been”
[
39
]

is also the matter of a certain
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-distinguishability.
An interferometer—the entire installation—should be
perceived as nothing more than a black box
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
—the box
(
5
)—outside of space and time. This is a kind of
irreducible element that produces the only
entity—distinguishable
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-events, and no other. The
box contains no ‘flying particles’. Exempli gratia, none of the
words in the typical sentence ‘photon propagates a definite path’
are well-defined. Any assessment of the screen flashes observed
within the interferometer—‘is zero statistics possible in any
spot?’—lacks meaning until the theory’s numerical apparatus is
presented.

Remark
13
.

Thus Young’s interference of the light beams (1803) is

inherently
the quantum, not the classical effect: a
micro-events’ accumulation is usually termed as the light intensity.
The classical electromagnetism and optics, in an exact sense, do not
explain, only describe, the phenomenon quantitatively with the use
of the numerical concepts of the positive and, which is important,
the negative values of observable strength-fields
\vec{\boldsymbol{E}}
and

\vec{\boldsymbol{H}}
. (The negative numbers are specifically discussed
further in sect.
9.2
.) Accordingly, operations of their
addition/subtraction ‘rephrase’ the effect in words “superposing,
suppressing, waves, intensities”, and we call this ‘the
explanation’. In quantum way of looking at it, all of these concepts
are not yet available, and the phenomenon per se is no more than
statistics of the ‘positively accumulative’ quantal clicks;

•

there are no particles, waves, and subtractions there.

The same—visible with the naked eye and ‘explainable by
waves’—macroscopic effect would take place, if we had a ‘laser’
of, say, mono-energetic very slow electrons (a proposal for
experimentalists). To put it more precisely: a gun or emitter of
something we envision as the ‘tiny bodily formations’ the electrons,
molecules, microbes, and the like. It is self-evident that we would have
seen the wave-like manifestation even from a single slit.

Criticism of the typical (a common event-space) examination of the
2-slit experiment
[
55
]
is already abundant in the
literature. See, for example, the works
[
173
]
(!),

[
202
, pp. 55–58]
,
[
94
, Ch. 2]
,
[
3
, sects. V.1,
VI.1–2]
,
[
135
, p. 93]
,
[
56
]
.

By way of a continuation to the last sentence in Remark
3
, we
add the following. To force an electron-click to happen each time at
the same (or predictable) place is no different from “completely
describing
everything
that we have”, i. e., from the precise
setting of ‘the same’ and of macro-context
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
. It is amply evident
that this is a manifest absurdity. Hence it immediately follows that

the unpredictability of microscopic events must exist in
principle
and macro-determinism may be only an idealization through
a (math) model: the model description of the
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
itself.

Summing up, it is not the quantum interference that requires
interpretative comprehension but its classic ‘roughening’. In other
words, a scheme that latently presumes the rule (
62
) of
extrapolation of what is observed in macro and micro
[
182
, (!) last
sentence on p. 101]
. It is this scheme and not the quantum
approach that contradicts the logic and experience. Pauli
characterizes this as habits “known as ‘ontology’ or ‘realism”’.
More than that, the chief component of
constructs—
\lceil
observation
\rightarrowtail
state
′
\rceil
—is
cast out and replaced with (
19
) under such a
transformation. The
DataSource
object
(p.
5.4.1
) begins to be identified with

observational
and
numerical
characteristics (see a
paragraph preceding Remark
4
), while the logic of the
micro-world requires precisely distancing these two concepts, with
no need for the characteristics themselves.

Thus we should not be deriving the physics of one phenomenon from
another
[
113
, p. 92]
and making (super)generalizations,
as soon as the incorrectness of the previous derivation method was
established.

•

Quantum

mathematics is not a physical theory—and
that is its distinguishing feature—but rather a single
syntactical
(meta)
principle of forming the
mathematical models being subsequently turned into
(the physical)

theories
. This principle is not subject to any physical
validation.

Scott Aaronson was likely the first to advance the line of thought
about non-physicality. On page 110 of the book
[
2
]
, he
writes that “it’s
not
a physical theory in the same sense as
electromagnetism or general relativity … quantum mechanics sits
at a level
between
math and physics …
is the operating
system
…”. Fuchs–Peres provoke: “quantum theory does
not

describe physical reality”
[
65
, p. 70]
.

To create the models, we already have a good deal of

latitude
: the toolkit
\mathcal{O}=\{\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B},\ldots\}
, the
parameter
D
, the families
\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}
,

{\mathfrak{T}}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}
, …}, numbers
\{\varrho_{s}\}
of mixtures
(
3.1
), and—thanks to the notion of
covariance
III
—spectra, a
structure of a group, and the concept of (different) representations
of a mathematical structure. This liberty will be subsequently
augmented with the key notions of a mean and of time
t
, and also
with the composite systems, the classical Lagrangians/Hamiltonians,
their symmetries, gauge fields, and phenomenological constants. This
is what is currently termed the quantum phenomenology or a

quantization procedure
of the classical models: the
path-integrals,
S
-matrix, et similia.

All that remains is to examine the numerical constituent of quantum
mathematics. The further strategy
(sects.
7
–
9
) lies in the fact that the
numbers need to be created at first as a theoretical
concept—arithmetic—and then as the ‘numerical values for
observable quantities’—the observations numbers. The sections

9.1
–
9.2
contain some more explanations along these
lines.

7.
Numeri

By number we understand not so much a multitude of

unities, as the abstracted ratio of any quantity to another

quantity of the same kind, which we take for unity

— I. Newton
(1707)

7.1.
Replications of ensembles

In connection with the emergence of a group, the numerical
representation of brace also undergoes a change, since the
‘doubling’ of a semigroup into a group through adjoining the
inversions deprives coordinate
\mathfrak{a}
of its distinction in
comparison with the inversion
-\mathfrak{a}
. Given the involution

(
63
)

-(-\mathfrak{a})=\mathfrak{a}\;,

it makes no difference what to call an element and what to call its
inversion in the pair
\{\mathfrak{a},-\mathfrak{a}\}
. This doubling is
formally known as a symmetrization of the commutative associative
law (monoid)
[
26
]
. Curiously, under commutativity and
associativity
[
34
, sect. 1.10]
, solution to the problem
of embedding is unique
[
26
, pp. 15–17]
, and otherwise, no
solution, in general, exists. There exist the classes (Mal’cev
(1936)), which are not axiomatized by finitely many

\forall
-formulas
[
118
, pp. 216–217]
.

The aforesaid is best demonstrated by another way of ‘numeralizing’
the empiricism, which is realized as the infinite replication of
finite ensembles

(
64
)

\big\{\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\mathsf{n}}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\mathsf{n}}\cdots\big\}=\big\{\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\mathsf{n}}\big\}_{\infty}=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\mathsf{n}\infty}\;.

That is, empirically, any infinite ensemble is thought of as created
by repetitions (copies) of the finite objects

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\mathsf{n}}
. It is in this sense, and in this sense
alone, that one should read the writing

\Sigma\rightsquigarrow\infty
for the infinity postulate
(
14
), because at the moment we possess neither the mathematics
nor the topological concepts, such as a passage to the limit

lim

\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\Sigma\to\infty
. For example,
expression
\Sigma\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}\infty
can be viewed as a conjunction of
the actual and potential infinity
[
200
,
96
]
. Simply put,
the case in point is not an
axiomatic
act—an imposition of
the
math
-existence condition for numbers
\{\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{j}\}
in
(
14
). The latter has been typically criticized as an idea of
the stable limiting frequencies in
QM

[
148
, pp. 15, 183, 211,
…]
,
[
161
, pp. 97–99]
,
[
181
,
64
]
.
Rather, we claim that the only way to consistently incorporate the
language notions of infinity and of the finite (observational)
numbers in theory—“to cross an abyss” (Poincaré)—is the above
semantics and correspondence between symbols

\{n_{j},\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{j};\Sigma,\rightsquigarrow,\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}},\infty\}
. See also
subsection “
StatLength
and infinity” in the work
[
27
]
.

In turn, the above mentioned copies
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\mathsf{n}}
are
replications of the atomic primitive
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{1}
. Replication
is thus an operation of the same significance as
\cup
and

\uplus
. With this point, the
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-brace is characterized by
the ‘numerical’ combination

\eqref{set}\quad\rightarrowtail\quad\big\{[\mathsf{n}_{1}\infty,\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{m}_{1}\infty],\;[\mathsf{n}_{2}\infty,\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{m}_{2}\infty],\;\ldots\big\}\;\rightleftarrows\;\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}

(indices label the
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}
-primitives), which has been
created from the unitary brace by the scheme

(
65
)

\eqref{min}=\vbox{\hbox{${\left\lgroup\vbox to22.81938pt{}\right.}$}}\mkern-11.0mu\begin{array}[]{c}\big\{\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\infty^{\prime}}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\infty^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}}\big\}\\
\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\;\;\;\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\;\;\;\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\;\;\;\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\\
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\infty$}}\end{array}\mkern-8.0mu\vbox{\hbox{${\left\rgroup\vbox to22.81938pt{}\right.}$}}\quad\rightarrowtail\quad\vbox{\hbox{${\left\lgroup\vbox to22.81938pt{}\right.}$}}\mkern-11.0mu\begin{array}[]{c}\big\{\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\mathsf{n}\infty}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\mathsf{m}\infty}\big\}\\
\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\;\;\;\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\;\;\;\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\;\;\;\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\\
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle(\mathsf{n}+\mathsf{m})\infty$}}\end{array}\hskip 13.00005pt\vbox{\hbox{${\left\rgroup\vbox to22.81938pt{}\right.}$}}\quad\rightarrowtail\quad[\mathsf{n}\infty,\mathsf{m}\infty]\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\;.

The semigroup union
\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi^{\prime}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}\uplus\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
is then conformed
with the writing

(
66
)

\big\{[\mathsf{n}_{1}^{\prime}\infty,\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{m}_{1}^{\prime}\infty],\;[\mathsf{n}_{2}^{\prime}\infty,\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{m}_{2}^{\prime}\infty],\;\ldots\big\}\uplus\big\{[\mathsf{n}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{1}\infty,\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{m}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{1}\infty],\;[\mathsf{n}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{2}\infty,\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{m}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{2}\infty],\;\ldots\big\}=\\
=\big\{[(\mathsf{n}_{1}^{\prime}+\mathsf{n}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{1})\mkern 1.0mu\infty,\mkern 1.0mu(\mathsf{m}_{1}^{\prime}+\mathsf{m}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{1})\mkern 1.0mu\infty],\;[(\mathsf{n}_{2}^{\prime}+\mathsf{n}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{2})\mkern 1.0mu\infty,\mkern 1.0mu(\mathsf{m}_{2}^{\prime}+\mathsf{m}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}_{2})\mkern 1.0mu\infty],\;\ldots\big\}\;.

Moreover, the
\mathsf{n}
-,
\mathsf{m}
-quantities may be freely thought
of as real ones due to the
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
-continual infinity of ensembles
proven above (sect.
4
). The empirical rationale of this is
apparent; namely, fractions of the arbitrarily large ensembles

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu\mkern 1.0mu\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots\}
.

This way of matching the infinity with
\Sigma
-postulate
automatically inherits translation of associativity/commutativity,
because the ‘percentages’, such as
s
and
w
, just as the rules
(
46
)–(
47
) themselves, do not even emerge. There, these
numbers were originating from
\Sigma
-postulate, but it, in turn,
was
demolishing the pair
(\varkappa,\mathfrak{S})
itself
in
(
45
):
\mathfrak{S}\to\infty
. It is clear that, according to
(
66
), the semigroup structure
\mathfrak{G}
is also inherited,
turning into the addition of the numerical pairs

(
67
)

(\mathsf{n}^{\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime})\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}(\mathsf{n}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime})=(\mathsf{n}^{\prime}+\mathsf{n}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}+\mathsf{m}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime})\;.

Returning to the group, we observe that the ‘negative symbols’

(-\mathsf{n},-\mathsf{m})
might be initially taken as the semigroup

\mathfrak{G}
being duplicated, with equal success and with the same
arithmetical addition
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}
, while the positive
(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})

could be thought of as inversions thereof.

Summing up, let us specify the rules of passing to the numerical
representations

(
68
)

\eqref{min}\quad\Leftarrow\mkern-11.0mu=\mkern-11.0mu\Rightarrow\quad\{\pm{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{p}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}},\pm{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{q}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}}\}\mkern 1.0mu{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad(\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}

and, to avoid ambiguity, replace the binary-composition symbols

\{\uplus,\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\}
with a new symbol
\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to9.58336pt{\hfil\hbox{$+$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\smallfrown$}}
for
objects (
68
):

\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{p}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{q}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\}\mkern 1.0mu{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to9.58336pt{\hfil\hbox{$+$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\smallfrown$}}\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\mkern 1.0mu{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}}\;.

The previously dropped primitives
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
have been
restored here, since they will be further needed for theory’s
covariance (sects.
7.4
,
7.5
), although they are still
unnecessary at the moment.

It is not accidental that we spoke of “numeral labeling” the brace
(p.
4.2
), since the question of arithmetic on them
had not yet arisen. Although
\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}
-statistics—the real

{\mathbb{R}}
-numbers—are already involved, their use was based on an
accustomed perception of the number. In accordance with
pr.
II
, the numerical formalization
of ensemble empiricism should be considered in greater detail.

7.2.
The number as an operator

Let us take up the ‘process of manufacturing’ the numbers
(pr.
II
). We begin with the
classical simplification

(
69
)

\mathfrak{A}=\big\{\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\},\mkern 1.0mu\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\},\mkern 1.0mu\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\},\mkern 1.0mu\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\},\;\ldots\big\}\;,

and the notion of the number does not yet appear in any form.

The mathematical abstracting the observation micro-acts is an
employment of the operation
\cup
and of its closedness (see
sect.
5.1
). For example,

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}\cup\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}=\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}
. All the symbols in
(
69
), as well as the character
\cup
, is of course
merely a convention, and they may be changed. By writing
(
69
) in symbols like
\{a\mkern 1.0mu,b\mkern 1.0mu,c\mkern 1.0mu,d\mkern 1.0mu,\ldots\}
and
+
, this
set should be supplemented with identities as
a+b=c
,
b+b=d
, …,
i. e., with a binary construction
+
. Then (semi)group and
commutative superpositions arise. Though note that the introducing
the numbers at this point—even if only as symbols—is not
necessary. It would reduce to re-notating the set’s elements, to be
precise. But the empirical description calls for their unification,
as manifested in the numerical notation like

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}1\mkern 1.0mu\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}
,
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}2\mkern 1.0mu\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}
, …. It is precisely this pattern that was
implicitly kept in mind in procedures (
36
)–(
37
) and
(
64
)–(
65
), i. e., when introducing the numbers

\mathsf{n}
by means of replication of finite or infinite ensembles:

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\mathsf{n}}\;\mathrel{\hbox{\scalebox{1.3}[1]{\hbox{$\Leftarrow\mkern-13.0mu\Rightarrow$}}}}\;\mathsf{n}\mkern 1.0mu\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\big\{\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\infty}\cdots\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}_{\infty}\big\}_{\mathsf{n}}\;\mathrel{\hbox{\scalebox{1.3}[1]{\hbox{$\Leftarrow\mkern-13.0mu\Rightarrow$}}}}\;\mathsf{n}\mkern 1.0mu\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}\;.

The symbol
\mathrel{\hbox{\scalebox{1.3}[1]{\hbox{$\Leftarrow\mkern-13.0mu\Rightarrow$}}}}
should read here as ‘the same thing as’.
Clearly, the very idea of conjunction of the two
entities—empirical brace (
36
) and the concept of a
(quantitative and ordinal) number
(sects.
5.1
,
5.2
)—is not otherwise implementable.
That is to say,

•

we have no any means of translating the aggregates of micro-acts

\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.90286pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}
(i. e.,
macro-observations
M
) into the numerical
language, other than through the counting of things
[
99
]
,
i. e., through the natural-language notion of the ‘quantity of
something’:

(
70
)

\begin{array}[]{r@{}c@{}l}&\makebox[0.0pt][c]{{\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}$\cdots$\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\;\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}}\;
\text{$\mathscr{A}$\penalty-\hskip 0.0pttransitions}\;
{\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}\;\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}$\cdots$\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}$}}}&\\
&\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\Downarrow\qquad\Downarrow$}\\
\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\lceil$quantity of$\rceil$}&&\;\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\lceil$something$\rceil$\quad(replication)}\\
&\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\Downarrow\qquad\Downarrow$}\\
\lceil\text{numbers}\rceil&&\;\text{$\lceil{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$\penalty-\hskip 0.0ptprimitives, ensembles$\rceil$}\\
\searrow&&\quad\swarrow\\
&\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\mathsf{n}\mkern 1.0mu\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}$}\end{array}\;.

Heisenberg stresses an obligatory relationship with “the natural
language because it is only there that we can be certain to touch
reality”
[
82
, pp. 201–202]
. Otherwise, the
quantitative theory would have nowhere to originate even at the
level of calculating the natural entities by the
\mathbb{N}
-number
tokens. It may be added that arising the numbers is a permanently
present (innate) process of creating the thought objects by an
abstracting in the human brain: the mental suppressing/neglecting of
the inessential and identifying the distinguishable
entities—perceptual objects—irrespective of their nature

[
102
, “forming collections, … putting objects together”; pp. 99,
251]
. It is something that humans do all the time without
even realizing they are doing it. This process, say,

\displaystyle\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptlanguage, words\penalty$\rceil$}\cdots\rightarrowtail\text{\lx@text@lbrace{sheep, ${\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$, verb, ${\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$, theory, \ldots}}\}\rightarrowtail

\displaystyle\{\textsf{a sheep, a ${\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$, a verb, \ldots}\}\cdots\rightarrowtail\lceil\text{something/thing/\ldots/St\"{u}cke}\rceil\cdots\rightarrowtail

\displaystyle\{\bullet\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{St\"{u}ck},\bullet\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{St\"{u}ck},\bullet\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{St\"{u}ck},\bullet\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{St\"{u}ck},\bullet\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{St\"{u}ck},\ldots\}\rightarrowtail\{\bullet,\bullet,\bullet,\bullet,\bullet;\textsf{St\"{u}cke}\}\rightarrowtail

\displaystyle\{1,1,1,1,1;\textsf{St\"{u}cke}\}\rightarrowtail 5\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{St\"{u}ck}\rightarrowtail 5\;\textsf{\sout{St\"{u}ck}}\rightarrowtail 5\rightarrowtail\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptabstraction
{5}\penalty$\rceil$}\;,

is akin to Cantor’s introducing the concept of a Menge
[
169
, Ch. 1,
sect. 1.1]
and has
no
the mathematical (math-logic)
nature. Rather, the math of numbers does originate from it

[
102
, Ch. 3]
; see also

[
68
, sect. 2.4.5.1
arithmetic
]
.

Incidentally, the ‘inessential and identifying’ just mentioned have
the nature just like the ‘the same’ in sect.
5.4
. It is with
these notions—a key feature of the natural/physical language and
of speech—that any abstracting begins: the ‘abstracting from …’.

On the other hand, the numerical tokens are ‘affixed’ not only to
the ‘atom’
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}
, but also to other objects,
any
at
that; for more details, see Remark
16
further below.
Therein lies the primary meaning of this still proto-mathematical
concept
[
147
, “Psychologie du nombre”]
. One might even
say, a definition according to which this notion has been conceived
(‘20 Stück’, ‘half an hour’, …) and is being used universally.
Here are a few examples:

(
71
)

\begin{array}[]{c}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}\equiv 3\mkern 1.0mu\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}\equiv 2\mkern 1.0mu\big(\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}\cup\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}\big)\\[4.30554pt]
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\rightarrowtail$}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox to11.11115pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}\hfil}}\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad a\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\rightarrowtail$}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox to11.11115pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle 3$}\hfil}}3\mkern 1.0mua\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad c\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\rightarrowtail$}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox to11.11115pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\hfil}}1\mkern 1.0muc\end{array}\;.

Accordingly, in between the elements, there arise identities such as

2\mkern 1.0mub\equiv 4\mkern 1.0mua
,
3\mkern 1.0mua\equiv c
,
1\mkern 1.0muc\equiv c
. In other words, as we
complete simplification (
69
),

•

While abstracting the empirical contents of the number entities
into math-symbols, they should be defined as unary operations
{
{}\kern 7.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.83109pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.33339pt{}1
,
{}\kern 7.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.83109pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.33339pt{}2
,
\ldots
,

\widehat{\raisebox{1.72218pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\hbox{$/$}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}
,
\ldots
,
{}\kern 7.0869pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.0869pt{}\pi
, …} that
take action at
\mathfrak{A}
-set (
69
) as automorphisms:

\{{}\kern 7.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.83109pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.33339pt{}2\mkern 1.0mub={}\kern 6.00008pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.83109pt{}\mkern 1.0mu}\mkern-1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.00008pt{}4\mkern 1.0mua,\mkern 1.0mu{}\kern 7.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.83109pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.33339pt{}1\mkern 1.0muc=c\mkern 1.0mu,\ldots\}
.

That said, replication is formalized as an operator
{}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n

with its numerical symbol
\mathsf{n}
:

(
72
)

\psi\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.00003pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mapsto$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{n\mkern 1.0mu}\mkern-1.0mu}{}$}}\mathsf{n}\mkern 1.0mu\psi\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\psi,\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{n}\mkern 1.0mu\psi\in\mathfrak{A}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\mathsf{n}\in{\mathbb{R}}\;,

where
\psi
is understood to be any (sub)ensemble/(sub)set. In
the language of the
ZF
-theory,
\mathsf{n}\mkern 1.0mu\psi
would be formally
organized as an ordered pair
(\mathsf{n},\psi)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\{\{\mathsf{n}\},\{\mathsf{n},\psi\}\}

[
100
]
, where
\mathsf{n}
is a cardinality of
a set consisting of copies of the object/set
\psi
. We will refer
to these facts as the implementation of a replication operator by
numbers.

Attention is drawn to the fact that the case in point at the moment

is not
a math-logical
definitio/formalization
of the
concept of a number, such as (
113
), but is an introducing
of what is understood by number in the empirical/physical theory
(
II
). For example, Chomsky says with
regard to this point: “When multiplying numbers in our heads, we
depend on many factors beyond our intrinsic knowledge of arithmetic”

[
36
, p. 3]
.

7.3.
QM
and arithmetica

We immediately observe the following properties.

The operators are applicable to each other. Being a family

\{{}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n\mkern 1.0mu,{}\kern 8.93483pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.93483pt{}m,\mkern 1.0mu{}\kern 6.35214pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern 0.0mu}\mkern 0.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.35214pt{}p\mkern 1.0mu,\ldots\}
, they are closed with
respect to their composition

{}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n\mkern 1.0mu({}\kern 8.93483pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.93483pt{}m\mkern 1.0mu\psi)=({}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}{}\kern 8.93483pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.93483pt{}m)\mkern 1.0mu\psi={}\kern 6.35214pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern 0.0mu}\mkern 0.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.35214pt{}p\mkern 1.0mu\psi
, and among them, there is an
identical operator
{}\kern 7.66672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.83109pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.66672pt{}\mathds{1}\mkern 1.0mu\psi=\psi
. The empirical
meaning of the concept—a fractional portion of ensemble
(see (
64
))—requires that for each
{}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n
there
exists its inversion
{}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n^{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}1}
. Hence, the composition of
replications
{}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}{}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n^{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}1}
must return the
former ‘quantity’:

({}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}{}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n^{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}1})\mkern 1.0mu\psi={}\kern 7.66672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.83109pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.66672pt{}\mathds{1}\mkern 1.0mu\psi
.
Therein lies “the actual meaning of division. … this [operator]
construction really corresponds to division”
[
11
, p. 37]
. By
virtue of the fact that family

\{{}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n\mkern 1.0mu,{}\kern 8.93483pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.93483pt{}m\mkern 1.0mu,{}\kern 6.35214pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern 0.0mu}\mkern 0.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.35214pt{}p\mkern 1.0mu,\ldots\}
provides automorphisms of
the
\mathfrak{A}
-set, these operators entail the associative identities

(({}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}{}\kern 8.93483pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.93483pt{}m)\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}{}\kern 6.35214pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern 0.0mu}\mkern 0.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.35214pt{}p)\mkern 1.0mu\psi=({}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}({}\kern 8.93483pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.93483pt{}m\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}{}\kern 6.35214pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern 0.0mu}\mkern 0.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.35214pt{}p))\mkern 1.0mu\psi
. This point is a property, and it
has a proof
[
101
, sect. I.1.2]
. The common nature of the
replication and of the
\cup
-union also signifies that there are
relations in place that mix the actions of the unary
{}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n
’s
and the binary union of ensembles. At a minimum, suffice it to
define the action of the replicator on a ‘
\cup
-sum’ of
replications. Clearly, the case in point is a distributive
coordination of
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}
and
\cup
:

{}\kern 6.35214pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern 0.0mu}\mkern 0.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.35214pt{}p\mkern 1.0mu({}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n\mkern 1.0mu\psi\cup{}\kern 8.93483pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.93483pt{}m\mkern 1.0mu\psi)=({}\kern 6.35214pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern 0.0mu}\mkern 0.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.35214pt{}p\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}{}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n)\mkern 1.0mu\psi\cup({}\kern 6.35214pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern 0.0mu}\mkern 0.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.35214pt{}p\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}{}\kern 8.93483pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.93483pt{}m)\mkern 1.0mu\psi\;.

We now observe that the indication of
\psi
everywhere in the
identities above loses the necessity, and the
\psi
-label becomes a
semblance of a dummy index or the unit symbol [
kg
], which can
be changed. As we omit it, the theory is freed of
\psi
as a
‘calculation unit’. Then the last relation, as an example, acquires
the form of a property between the operator
\mathsf{n}
-symbols
(
72
), if
\{\cup,\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}\}
are replaced with the symbols of
binary operations
\{+,\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}\}
:

(
73
)

\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}(\mathsf{n}+\mathsf{m})=(\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}\mathsf{n})+(\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}\mathsf{m})\;.

Supplementing this relation with other empirically determining
properties, one infers that the
unary
operationality of

{}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n
-replications (
72
) is
indistinguishable

from the
binary
operationality on their
\mathsf{n}
-symbols. The
latter, in turn, acquires the multiplicative structure of a
commutative group

(
74
)

\mathsf{n}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}\mathsf{m}=\mathsf{m}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}\mathsf{n}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad(\mathsf{n}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}\mathsf{m})\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}\mathsf{p}=\mathsf{n}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}(\mathsf{m}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}\mathsf{p})\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\mathsf{n}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}1=\mathsf{n}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\mathsf{n}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}\mathsf{n}^{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}1}=1\;,

and, as for the addition
+
, it is already binary and commutative
due to properties of
\cup
(sect.
5.1
):

(
75
)

\mathsf{n}+\mathsf{m}=\mathsf{m}+\mathsf{n}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad(\mathsf{n}+\mathsf{m})+\mathsf{p}=\mathsf{n}+(\mathsf{m}+\mathsf{p})\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\mathsf{n}+0=\mathsf{n}\;.

Incidentally, the 3-term multiplicative associativity relation in
(
74
) has the same operatorial nature and origin as
operations
\{\cup,\boldsymbol{\cup}\}
do in (
41
). We have already
commented on the additive analog to this situation—a
determinative structure of the binary operation—after formula
(
59
).

It is also clear that rules (
73
)–(
75
) must be
supplemented with the concept of a negative number

(
76
)

\mathsf{n}+(-\mathsf{n})=0\;,

for such numbers have been fully justified in the superposition
principle.

After having acquired properties (
73
)–(
76
)—call
them
arithmetica
, symbols
\{\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m},\ldots\}
turn into
abstract numbers, although their operator genesis does not go away
and is yet to be involved. This is where a full list of requirements
for the concept of a real number should be added, and which have to
do with ordering
<
, completeness/continuality,
and
their
relations with algebraic rules (
73
)–(
76
). We will
take that this is conducted axiomatically
[
193
, pp. 35–38]
,
although the algebraic constituent of this ‘axiomatics’, as we have
seen, is not axiomatical but deducible from empiricism.
Multiplication
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}
, and also the subsequent

\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}
-multiplication of
{\mathbb{C}}
-numbers (
84
), is a most
nontrivial part in deriving the structure ‘the arithmetic’.

As an outcome, we reveal an essential asymmetry in the genesis of
the standard binary structures
+
and
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}

(cf.
[
102
, p. 60]
), and thereby a greater primacy of

QM
-consideration even over the (seemingly
self-evident)

arithmetic. Indeed, binarity may come
only from operation
\cup
, which is primordially unique and,
thereby, is inherited only to the one natural
prototype—addition.

•

Multiplication is not featured in the superposition principle, nor
does it arise directly as a binary structure. The absence of a
multiplication symbol in (
60
)–(
61
) is no accident.

The multiplication originates in the closedness of replications

{}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}{}\kern 8.93483pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.93483pt{}m
, and they are required according to the

M
-paradigm (
12
). In effect, any
non-operatorial way of introducing the
\mathsf{n}
-numbers is not
self-evidence for empiricism. An operator nature of the number is
precisely that which gives rise to the second binary operation.
Moreover, without such a comprehension of the number the ‘linear
nature’ of
QM
(sect.
8.1
) will remain axiomatic at all times
and, as will be seen below, quantum foundations will be doomed to
never-ending interpreting the mathematical symbols. However, the
pure axiomatic declaration of arithmetic (
73
)–(
76
)
will, in one way or another, require a (reciprocal to
(
70
)) treatment of the number in a context of ‘the
quantity of what?’, while its empirical pre-image always appears in
the pair
\lceil
the quantity of
\rceil

\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{+}}}

\lceil
something
\rceil
.
Another way to put it is that,

•

in the foundations of theory, there arises a predecessor/analog to
the notion of a physical unit,

though the ultimate description is a description in terms of binary
structures (
73
)–(
76
). It is carried out by
dropping/attaching the symbols such as
\psi
, which is a quantum
generalization to the independence of a physical theory from the
measurement units.

Certainly, when formalized, the
{}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n
-replication and its
binary
\mathsf{n}
-twin become universally abstract. For example, the

{}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n
-operator (
72
) may be applied to the quantum
case in which the object
\psi
has already an internal structure
associated with the presence of
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-primitives.
This changes no the essence of the matter

. Another
example is when numbers
\mathsf{n}
give birth to the really observable
quantities. See also sect.
5.3
, Remark
16
, and
additional discussion in sect.
9
. Let us now proceed from
the fact that the comprehension/relation of the number and its
operator has been formalized as described above. This is the set of
‘axioms’ (
73
)–(
76
).

As concerns the philosophical literature, the issue of numbers was
likely discussed
[
144
,
158
,
98
]
(see also

[
99
]
and non-philosophical book
[
147
]
), and it
would be appropriate to quote T. Maudlin: “… numbers: they can be
added to one another,
perhaps
multiplied by one another, ….
But it is typically obscure what sort of
physical
relation
these mathematical operations could possibly represent”

[
119
, p. 138; first emphasis ours, second in original]
.
Cf. Einstein’s remarks regarding the “concepts and propositions” and
“the series of integers” on p. 287 in
[
48
]
.

7.4.
‘2-dimensional’ numbers

A number in and of itself, as a replication operator, may be applied
to any ensemble and to anything at all. However, in quantum case,
the ‘upper’ primitives are attached to every ‘lower’

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-event. These primitives, as was noted above, have to
be discarded. At the same time, the minimal structure associated
with the homogeneous array
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}\cdots{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}\}

as a whole is a unitary brace
\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}
containing two
‘upper’ primitives
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
. Their order, however,
is arbitrary there. That is to say, given

(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
there are two quite equal objects

\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
and

\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
that are subjected
to a replication. Each of them should be in a relationship (see
sect.
5.1
) to any other brace (
65
), which is already
apparent in the example of ‘
1
-dimensional’ versions

(\mathsf{n},0)\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
and
(\mathsf{n}^{\prime},0)\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
. We mean
that for each pair

\{(\mathsf{n},0)\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},(\mathsf{n}^{\prime},0)\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}
, there always
exists the number
\mathsf{m}
such that
{}\kern 8.6015pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern 0.0mu}\mkern 0.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.6015pt{}m\mkern 1.0mu(\mathsf{n},0)\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}=(\mathsf{n}^{\prime},0)\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
, i. e.,

\mathsf{m}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}\mathsf{n}=\mathsf{n}^{\prime}
.

As in the classical case (
71
), the sought-for
generalizations of replicators are the transitive automorphisms on

unitary

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-brace (
68
), but they

are not
abstract and
not
arbitrary. They are strictly
bound to the declared meaning of the number:
{}\kern 8.80841pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.24329pt{}\mkern 0.0mu}\mkern 0.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.80841pt{}N
-operation of
creating the copies. Therefore, by virtue of the equal rights of

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
and
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
, it is imperative to bring the two
1-fold copying acts
{}\kern 8.80841pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.24329pt{}\mkern 0.0mu}\mkern 0.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.80841pt{}N\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\mkern 1.0mu,\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
and

{}\kern 9.80843pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.24329pt{}\mkern 0.0mu}\mkern 0.0mu}{}$}\kern-9.80843pt{}M\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\mkern 1.0mu,\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
into play, which
differ in the permutation of primitives

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\rightleftarrows{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
. This point will determine a
quantum extension of the replication.

As a result, since we have nothing but the copying
{}\kern 8.80841pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.24329pt{}\mkern 0.0mu}\mkern 0.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.80841pt{}N
and
‘union’
\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to9.58336pt{\hfil\hbox{$+$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\smallfrown$}}
, the most general transformation of the brace

\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
into (any) brace

\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\vbox to6.45831pt{}\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}{}^{\prime},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\vbox to6.45831pt{}\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}{}^{\prime}\}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
, that has been in a quantum-replication relation with
it, is determined by the rule

(
77
)

\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\mkern 1.0mu,\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\quad\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\scalebox{2.5}[1]{$\rightarrowtail$}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to28.88898pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle(\widehat{N\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu,M})$}\hfil}\quad\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\vbox to5.59721pt{}\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise to5.59721pt{}\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern to5.59721pt{}\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern to7.24329pt{}\mkern 0.0mu}\mkern 0.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.80841pt{}N\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern to9.58336pt{\hfil\hbox{$+$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\smallfrown$}}\;{}\kern 9.80843pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.24329pt{}\mkern 0.0mu}\mkern 0.0mu}{}$}\kern-9.80843pt{}M\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\;.

This is the quantum version of operators
(
71
)–(
72
), and the foregoing ideology of

{}\kern 8.80841pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.24329pt{}\mkern 0.0mu}\mkern 0.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.80841pt{}N
-operators and of liberation from the

-symbols

(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})
-representation of itself, is also

The aforesaid means that the numerical form

(

)

{}\kern 8.80841pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.24329pt{}\mkern 0.0mu}\mkern 0.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.80841pt{}N\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}=\{\mathsf{M}{\mkern 1.0mu\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathstrut\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathstrut\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.3194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern Here, a positivity/negativity of symbols
(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})
in
(

) should also be taken into account. Having regard to

)–(

)

(\mathsf{N},\mathsf{M})\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})=(\pm\mathsf{N}\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{n}\pm\mathsf{M}\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{m},\;\pm\mathsf{N}\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{m}\pm\mathsf{M}\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{n})\;,

wherein all four signs
\pm
are independent of each other, and the

\vbox{\hbox{$($}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}}\vbox{\hbox{$)$}}
-multiplication of 1-dimensional numbers
in (

) have been re-denoted by the habitual
standard
\mathsf{N}\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{m}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathsf{N}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}\mathsf{m}
. What should the
