) be?

As was the case previously, the just emerged binarity for
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}

should inherit—due to its operator origin—associativity,
existence of unity
\mathds{1}
, and of inversions. Namely, if the

(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})
-pairs are identified with the notation (
59
)
according to the convention

(
80
)

(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}\mathfrak{a}\;,

then the following properties should be declared:

(
81
)

(\mathfrak{a}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}\mathfrak{b})\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}\mathfrak{c}=\mathfrak{a}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}(\mathfrak{b}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}\mathfrak{c})\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\mathfrak{a}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}\mathds{1}=\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\mathfrak{a}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}{\mathfrak{a}}^{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}1}=\mathds{1}\;.

From (
77
)–(
78
) it is not difficult to see that the
combining (
81
) with (
59
) leads to a distributive
coordination of operations
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}
and
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}
:

(
82
)

\mathfrak{c}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}(\mathfrak{a}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}\mathfrak{b})=(\mathfrak{c}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}\mathfrak{a})\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}(\mathfrak{c}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}\mathfrak{b})\;.

However, the direct examination of this property shows that Ansatz
(
79
) satisfies it automatically. More than that, we can
consider this Ansatz even with parameters

\{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\}
instead of
(\pm)
-signs:

\mathfrak{a}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}\mathfrak{b}=(\mathsf{N},\mathsf{M})\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})=(\alpha\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{N}\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{n}+\beta\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{M}\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{m},\;\gamma\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{N}\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{m}+\delta\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{M}\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{n})\;.

Then the straightforward calculation shows that distributivity
(
82
) holds under the arbitrary

\{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\}
.

In turn, the examination of associativity—the first equality in
(
81
)—under the same meaning for

\{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\}
yields
\alpha=\gamma=\delta
and
free
\beta
. Returning to the
(\pm)
-values of these parameters,
this associativity particularizes (
79
) into the expression

(\mathsf{N},\mathsf{M})\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})=\pm(\mathsf{N}\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{n}\pm\mathsf{M}\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{m},\mathsf{N}\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{m}+\mathsf{M}\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{n})\mkern 1.0mu;

now, with two independent signs
\pm
. Moreover, in passing we
reveal the commutativity

(
83
)

\mathfrak{a}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}\mathfrak{b}=\mathfrak{b}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}\mathfrak{a}\;,

though it was not presumed prior to that.

The search for unity
\mathds{1}
and subsequent finding an inversion
of the element
(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})
yield:

\mathds{1}=(\pm 1,0)\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})^{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}1}=\vbox{\hbox{\large$\Big($}}\frac{\mathsf{n}}{\mathsf{\Delta}},-\frac{\mathsf{m}}{\mathsf{\Delta}}\vbox{\hbox{\large$\Big)$}}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\mathsf{\Delta}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathsf{n}^{2}\pm\mathsf{m}^{2}\;.

Both the
(\pm)
-symbols continue to be independent here. The choice

\mathsf{\Delta}=\mathsf{n}^{2}-\mathsf{m}^{2}
results in the absence of inversions

(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{n})^{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}1}
. This is in conflict with the group
property (
81
) and also causes the unmotivated exclusivity of
the unitary brace
\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
. There remains the
case
\mathsf{\Delta}=\mathsf{n}^{2}+\mathsf{m}^{2}
, and it reduces the scheme to
the form

\mathds{1}=\pm(1,0)\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad(\mathsf{N},\mathsf{M})\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})=\pm(\mathsf{N\mkern 1.0mun}-\mathsf{M\mkern 1.0mum},\mathsf{N\mkern 1.0mum}+\mathsf{M\mkern 1.0mun})

with a single symbol
\pm
. It is a simple matter to see that the
choice of sign
+
or
-
leads to the models that are isomorphic in
regard to which of representatives
(+1,0)
or
(-1,0)
should be
assigned for the identical replication
{}\kern 6.50005pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.24329pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.50005pt{}\mathds{I}
. By
virtue of (
63
) it does not matter, and we declare

(
84
)

\boxed{\mathds{1}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=(1,0)\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad(\mathsf{N},\mathsf{M})\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})=(\mathsf{N\mkern 1.0mun}-\mathsf{M\mkern 1.0mum},\mathsf{N\mkern 1.0mum}+\mathsf{M\mkern 1.0mun})}\;.

This is nothing more nor less than the canonical multiplication of
complex numbers
\mathsf{n}+{\mathrm{i}}\cdot\mathsf{m}=\mathfrak{a}\in{\mathbb{C}}
, if the
following identifications are performed:

(
85
)

(1,0)\rightleftarrows\mathds{1}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad(0,1)\rightleftarrows{\mathrm{i}}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\{\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}},\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}\}\rightleftarrows\{+,\cdot\}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})\rightleftarrows(\mathsf{n}+{\mathrm{i}}\cdot\mathsf{m})\;.

Notice that the known fully matrix (over
{\mathbb{R}}
) equivalent to
(
84
)

(\mathsf{n}+{\mathrm{i}}\cdot\mathsf{m})\mapsto(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})\mapsto{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\Big($}}}\mkern-6.0mu\begin{array}[]{c}\mathsf{n}\\
\mathsf{m}\end{array}\mkern-6.0mu{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\Big)$}}}\mapsto{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\Big($}}}\mkern-6.0mu\begin{array}[]{r@{}r}\mathsf{n}&\;-\mathsf{m}\\
\mathsf{m}&\mathsf{n}\end{array}\mkern-6.0mu{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\Big)$}}}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\Big($}}}\mkern-6.0mu\begin{array}[]{r@{}r}\mathsf{n}^{\prime}&\;-\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\\
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}&\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\end{array}\mkern-6.0mu{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\Big)$}}}={\vbox{\hbox{\large$\Big($}}}\mkern-6.0mu\begin{array}[]{r@{}r}\mathsf{N}&\;-\mathsf{M}\\
\mathsf{M}&\mathsf{N}\end{array}\mkern-6.0mu{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\Big)$}}}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\Big($}}}\mkern-6.0mu\begin{array}[]{r@{}r}\mathsf{n}&\;-\mathsf{m}\\
\mathsf{m}&\mathsf{n}\end{array}\mkern-6.0mu{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\Big)$}}}

does directly reflect the above ascertained operator essence

(\widehat{n^{\prime}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu,m^{\prime}})=(\widehat{N\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu,M})\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}(\widehat{\mkern 1.0mun,m\mkern 1.0mu})

of both the number multiplication
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}
and the
{\mathbb{C}}
-number
itself.

In view of the paramount importance of the
{\mathbb{C}}
-number field in

QT

[

,

,

]
, let us provide additional

),
(

)–(

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\rightleftarrows{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
used above fit more general
reasoning.

7.5.
Involutions and
\tilde{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
-algebra

Apart from a freedom in ordering the primitives

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\rightleftarrows{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
in brace

\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
, there is one more
arbitrariness: reappointing them
(
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\rightarrowtail{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
, …) as elements of the set

{\mathfrak{T}}
. However, no physics predetermines any of these degrees of
freedom. For, if other ingoing
{\mathfrak{T}}
-elements
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
,

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Omega\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
were present in (
34
) instead of

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
, then the theory of semigroup
\mathfrak{G}
,
strictly, should be declared the segregated theories

\mathfrak{G}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}}
,

\mathfrak{G}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\Omega\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}}
, etc. It is
clear that the labeling the theories, or a family thereof, is a
manifest absurdity, and they should be thus factorized with respect
to all kinds of ways to label them by
{\mathfrak{T}}
-primitives. The
liberation from the
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-icons and reconciliation
of the result with pt.
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathsf{R}^{\textsf{+}}

(p.

R
+

) are then performed by the scheme
\lceil
primitive
has changed
\rceil

\rightarrowtail

\lceil
a number character is
changing
\rceil
.

Inasmuch as declaring the
\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},\ldots\}
to be ingoing primitives in (
34
) is a replacement
of one to another, any such an appointment boils down to
permutations of no more than
pairs
, with two types
(inner/outer):

(
86
)

{}\kern 6.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.3022pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\gimel}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi\mkern-1.0mu\Phi}\mkern 1.0mu\mkern 1.0mu{:}\quad({\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{})\>\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\scalebox{2.3}[1]{$\rightleftarrows$}$}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox to27.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu\leftrightarrow\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\>({\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{})\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\qquad{}\kern 8.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.36108pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\Phi\mkern-1.0mu\Theta}\mkern 1.0mu\mkern 1.0mu{:}\quad({\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{})\>\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\scalebox{2.3}[1]{$\rightleftarrows$}$}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox to27.77785pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu\leftrightarrow\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\>({\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{})\;.

However, it is immediately obvious that these reappointments change
nothing in the
\cup
-relationships between (
34
) and are
defined by the structural relations

{}\kern 6.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.3022pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\gimel}_{\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi\mkern-1.0mu\Phi}}^{\smash{2}}={}\kern 6.50005pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.24329pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.50005pt{}\mathds{I}
,
{}\kern 8.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.36108pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph}_{\smash{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\Phi\mkern-1.0mu\Theta}}^{\smash{2}}={}\kern 6.50005pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.24329pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.50005pt{}\mathds{I}
. Then the need to indicate the primitives
themselves, as required, is eliminated, and their symbols may be
thrown away, if semigroup
\mathfrak{G}
is properly furnished with the
two abstract involutions
{}\kern 6.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.3022pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\gimel}
and
{}\kern 8.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.36108pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph}
. The

\mathfrak{G}
itself, of course, possesses also involution (
63
)
that turns it into the group
H
, but this involution has
already had a numerical representation (
68
) by signs
\pm
.
To be precise, it suffices to identify here the term “numerical”
with the group arithmetic of the
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}
-addition (
59
) coming
from the superposition principle realized on pairs
(
67
)–(
68
). Therefore the operators’ actions
(
86
) should be carried over onto objects defined in
precisely this manner; nothing more needs to be assumed.

Operator
{}\kern 6.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.3022pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\gimel}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi\mkern-1.0mu\Phi}
is immediately
translated into a numerical form independently of the property that
the objects
\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
form a
(semi)group. Indeed, since the swap

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\rightleftarrows{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
in the unordered pair

{}\kern 6.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.3022pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\gimel}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi\mkern-1.0mu\Phi}:\qquad\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\rightarrowtail\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}=\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\}\qquad\cdots

(the
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-label is dropped here as superfluous) is
indistinguishable from the permutation of numbers

\mathsf{n}\rightleftarrows\mathsf{m}
, the symbols
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
and

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
may be thrown away, organizing the numbers themselves
into the ordered pairs

\cdots\quad\mathrel{\hbox{\scalebox{1.3}[1]{\hbox{$\Rightarrow$}}}}\quad(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\rightarrowtail$}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox to11.11115pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}\kern 4.64005pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to3.57806pt{}\mkern 0.0mu}\mkern 0.0mu}{}$}\kern-4.64005pt{}\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\gimel}$}\hfil}}(\mathsf{m},\mathsf{n})\;.

When required, the
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-symbol returns hereinafter.

Let us now proceed to the outer involution

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\rightleftarrows{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
in (
86
):

{}\kern 8.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.36108pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi\mkern-1.0mu\Theta}:\qquad\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\rightarrowtail\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\;.

It is indifferent to the (first)
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-element of the pair,
and, extracting it by the rule

\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}=\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$0$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to9.58336pt{\hfil\hbox{$+$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\smallfrown$}}\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$0$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\;,

the question boils down to finding a representation to the
transformations

\big(\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$0$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to9.58336pt{\hfil\hbox{$+$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\smallfrown$}}\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$0$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\big)\;\rightarrowtail\;\big(\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 5.59721pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\smallfrown$}}\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$0$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern to5.16667pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle?$}\hfil}{\kern-1.2pt\radical 0{{}^{\prime}}{}},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle?$}\hfil}{\kern-1.2pt\radical 0{{}^{\prime}}{}})\;.

The component
\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$0$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\}
must go into itself, since the
symbol
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
attached to it has not changed. It means that

\mathsf{n}^{\prime}=\mathsf{n}
, and one is left with the task

\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$0$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\;\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\rightleftarrows$}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle?$}\hfil}}\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$0$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}{\kern-1.2pt\radical 0{{}^{\prime}}{}}\}\;.

However, operation
{}\kern 8.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.36108pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi\mkern-1.0mu\Theta}
recognizes only the
primitive’s symbols rather than their numbers. That is, replications

{}\kern 8.93483pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.93483pt{}m\mkern 1.0mu\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$0$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\pm\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$1$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\}=\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$0$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\pm\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}
do formally commute with

{}\kern 8.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.36108pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi\mkern-1.0mu\Theta}
. Hence, by omitting the letters

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}
, it will suffice to look for
the representation of
{}\kern 8.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.36108pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph}
by numerical pairs
(0,\pm\mathsf{m})

factorized with respect to replications
{}\kern 8.93483pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.93483pt{}m
, i. e., by the
set
\{(0,1),(0,-1)\}
. It, for its part, remains to be transformed
into itself, and the replication operators
{}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n
,

{}\kern 8.93483pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.93483pt{}m
will recreate the generic case. The identical
transformation
(0,\pm 1)\rightarrowtail(0,\pm 1)
is ruled out since

{}\kern 8.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.36108pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi\mkern-1.0mu\Theta}\neq{}\kern 6.50005pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.24329pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.50005pt{}\mathds{I}
; therefore,

(0,\pm 1)\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\rightarrowtail$}\hss}\raise 4.73611pt\hbox to11.11115pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\aleph}}$}\hfil}}(0,\mp 1)
. Restoring all the symbols that were dropped, the effect
of
{}\kern 8.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.36108pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph}
reduces to the sign change for the 2
-
nd element of the
coordinate pair:

(
87
)

(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\rightarrowtail$}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox to11.11115pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\aleph}}$}\hfil}}(\mathsf{n},-\mathsf{m})\;.

There is no need to change sign for the 1
-
st element, as this change
is the operator
-{}\kern 6.50005pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.24329pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.50005pt{}\mathds{I}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}{}\kern 8.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.36108pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph}
. Furthermore, one
observes that the already existing group inversion

-{}\kern 6.50005pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.24329pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.50005pt{}\mathds{I}
coincides with composition

(
88
)

({}\kern 8.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.36108pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}{}\kern 6.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.3022pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\gimel})^{2}=-{}\kern 6.50005pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.24329pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.50005pt{}\mathds{I}\;,

and we may even ‘forget’ about (the ‘old’) subtraction, leaving the
equipment

(
89
)

\{\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}},{}\kern 6.50005pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.24329pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.50005pt{}\mathds{I},{}\kern 8.93483pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.93483pt{}m,{}\kern 8.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.36108pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph},{}\kern 6.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.3022pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\gimel}\}

of semigroup
\mathfrak{G}
as an irreducible set of mathematical
structures over it.

In this connection, yet another—more formal—motivation of the
passage
\lceil
semigroup
\rightarrowtail
group
\rceil
, and thus of the
superposition principle, does arise. Indeed, the derivation of

{}\kern 8.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.36108pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph}
above engaged the inversion (
63
), but reappointment
of primitives
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\rightleftarrows{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Theta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
in
(
86
) is a fully independent act. Therefore if we forget
about ‘
(-)
-copies of the positive pairs’
(0,\mathsf{m})
, then the
involutory nature of automorphism
{}\kern 8.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.36108pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi\mkern-1.0mu\Theta}
would
still reproduce the semigroup
\mathfrak{G}
in numbers by ‘duplication’

\mathsf{m}\rightarrowtail\pm\mathsf{m}
, i. e., create the negative pairs

(0,-\mathsf{m})
, thus turning
\mathfrak{G}
into a group
H
. An
analogous reasoning on the symbol ‘
-
’ could be cited even earlier,
when the
{\mathbb{C}}
-field was being derived.

Now, remembering the above-described move to the binarity of

\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}
-multiplication on the
(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})
-pairs, we arrive at
the problem of matching it with structures (
89
). Clearly,
one needs only to ascertain the functionality of operators

{}\kern 6.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.3022pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\gimel}
and
{}\kern 8.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.36108pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph}
that were not available yet.

Relation (
88
) immediately gives us the correspondence

{}\kern 8.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.36108pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}{}\kern 6.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.3022pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\gimel}\rightleftarrows{\mathrm{i}}
, since

{\mathrm{i}}^{2}=-1
. Hence, one of these operators, say

{}\kern 6.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.3022pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\gimel}
, manifests itself in the imaginary unit

{\mathrm{i}}
. The origin of this operator—permutation

{}\kern 6.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.3022pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\gimel}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi\mkern-1.0mu\Phi}
in (
86
)—is the
very same permutation
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\rightleftarrows{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
that
generated the
{\mathrm{i}}
-object in algebra (
84
)–(
85
). The
second operator, i. e., (
87
), as is directly seen, is also
not related to the binary
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}
and
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}
but determines the
change
{\mathrm{i}}\rightarrowtail-{\mathrm{i}}
. This means that the

QM
-consideration does not just give birth to the field
{\mathbb{C}}
but
to a division
\tilde{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
-algebra, which is equipped with two

non-binary
operations

\mathfrak{a}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\rightarrowtail$}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox to11.11115pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\aleph}}$}\hfil}}\mathfrak{a}^{*}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\mathfrak{a}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\rightarrowtail$}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox to11.11115pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}\kern 4.64005pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to3.57806pt{}\mkern 0.0mu}\mkern 0.0mu}{}$}\kern-4.64005pt{}\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\gimel}$}\hfil}}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}\;.

Informally, it defines all the basic actions on ‘complex quantities’
and thereby determines a
QM
-extension/generalization to the
intuitive and habitual arithmetical manipulations
(
70
)–(
76
) with real things. Consequently, the four
binary arithmetical operations—addition/subtraction/multiplication/division—should be supplemented with the two
unary ones: conjugation
{}\kern 8.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.36108pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph}
and swap
{}\kern 6.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.3022pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\gimel}
.

Remark
14
.

A curious observation for the formal complex-number mathematics is
appropriate here. None of these operators boil down to involution

-{}\kern 6.07993pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.03607pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.07993pt{}\mathds{I}
. We mean that each of the pairs

({}\kern 7.69106pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.13423pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.69106pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph},-{}\kern 6.07993pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.03607pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.07993pt{}\mathds{I})
or

({}\kern 5.9723pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.08516pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-5.9723pt{}\boldsymbol{\gimel},-{}\kern 6.07993pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.03607pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.07993pt{}\mathds{I})
is expressible
through
({}\kern 5.9723pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.08516pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-5.9723pt{}\boldsymbol{\gimel},{}\kern 7.69106pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.13423pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.69106pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph})
, and not the reverse; see
(
88
). To put it plainly, the self-suggested going from the
natural sign-change (i. e.,
-{}\kern 6.54521pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to5.69258pt{}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.54521pt{}1
over
{\mathbb{R}}
) to the
inversion of the 2-dimensional
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}
-addition (i. e.,

-{}\kern 6.07993pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.03607pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.07993pt{}\mathds{I}
over
{\mathbb{C}}
) deprives the involution

-{}\kern 6.07993pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.03607pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.07993pt{}\mathds{I}
of its primary character, as it has taken in
the 1-dimensional domain
{\mathbb{R}}
. Furthermore, the 2
-
nd operation

{}\kern 5.9723pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.08516pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-5.9723pt{}\boldsymbol{\gimel}
is, in a sense, more ‘primitive’ even than
the complex conjugation
{}\kern 7.69106pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.13423pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.69106pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph}
, as this operation has had to do
with a formal pair
(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})
—merely transposes it—and
does not invoke an arithmetic action, as does
{}\kern 7.69106pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.13423pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.69106pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph}
when changing
the sign
\mathsf{m}\mapsto-\mathsf{m}
in (
87
).

Relationship between the operators is by the binary multiplication:

\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}={\mathrm{i}}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}\mathfrak{a}^{*}
. By virtue of this relation it
makes no odds which one of these unary operators is left for

{\mathbb{C}}
-algebra.

We note—and this is important
[
27
]
—that the observational
statistics
\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{j}
are unchanged upon both operations

{}\kern 6.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.3022pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\gimel}
and
{}\kern 8.33339pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.36108pt{}\mkern-4.0mu}\mkern 4.0mu}{}$}\kern-8.33339pt{}\boldsymbol{\aleph}
.

7.6.
Naturalness of
{\mathbb{C}}
-numbers

Thus the
{\mathfrak{T}}
-set primitives have been entirely banished from the
theory, with the exception of the eigen-state

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}
-markers, which are needed only for
distinguishability (sect.
2.1
) in
\mathscr{A}
-observations. These
markers may be interchanged, but permutability

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{j}\rightleftarrows{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{k}
is already reflected
by the superposition’s commutativity. Taking now into account the
fact that reassigning the
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-labels does not touch on
the concept of the number, one infers: the covariance attained above
is exhaustive. As a result, we draw the following conclusion.

•

The coordinate representatives
\{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c},\ldots\}
of states and superpositions thereof (
60
) form the
complex number field
\tilde{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
equipped with the structures of
conjugation and of swap:

(
90
)

(\mathsf{n}+{\mathrm{i}}\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{m})\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.66664pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mapsto$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\displaystyle*$}}(\mathsf{n}-{\mathrm{i}}\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{m})\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad(\mathsf{n}+{\mathrm{i}}\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{m})\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mapsto$}\hss}\raise 1.29167pt\hbox to0.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\displaystyle\mathchar 869$}\hfil}}(\mathsf{m}+{\mathrm{i}}\mkern 1.0mu\mathsf{n})\;.

Statistical weights
\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{j}
in object (
37
) are invariant
with respect to both the involutions

\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{j}(\mathfrak{a}^{*})=\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{j}(\mathfrak{a})=\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{j}(\tilde{\mathfrak{a}})
for each
component
\mathfrak{a}_{s}
independently.

What is more, the commentary on the primacy of
QM
over the
abstract arithmetic (see p.
7.3
) has a logical
continuation.

•

Quantum-theoretic description invokes no
{\mathbb{C}}
-numbers; nor does
it introduce them. It
does create
them together with the

\tilde{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
-algebra. The
{\mathbb{C}}
-numbers are in and of themselves
the
quantum
numbers.

This fact is remarkable in its own right because the ‘2-dimensional’
numbers arise at the lowest empirical level, not from the need for
solving any mathematical problems. Mathematics is still lacking.
Therefore, pt.
\displaystyle\mathsf{R}^{\textsf{+}}
(p.

R
+

)
could have even been weakened by replacing
\lceil
homomorphism onto
numbers
\rceil
, roughly, with the
\lceil
homomorphism onto continuum
\rceil
.
Our minimal points of departure are replications and the
ingoing/outgoing structure of brace (
34
). The imaginary
part of the complex number—as a supplement to the real
one—comes, as a rough guide, from the left hand side of the
conception
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
. The theory does not depend
on the meanings that will be later attached to the physical
concepts—observables, measurement, spectra, means, etc—to
their interpretations or rigorous definitions. At the same time, the
interferential ‘effects of subtraction and of zeroes’ are
intrinsically present within the construct’s foundation itself.

Let us add, in conclusion, two more formal vindications of rigidity
of emerging the
{\mathbb{C}}
-structure. In doing so, one assumes that we
have already had the
{\mathbb{R}}
-numbers.

Unitary brace contain pairs of the form

\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$0$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 7.74998pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\}
. The binary operations

\{\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}},\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}\}
on their numerical representatives
(\mathsf{n},0)

are closed and, as easily seen, form a commutative field, which is
isomorphic to
{\mathbb{R}}
. It is a subset of the generic pair set

(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})
. From the operator nature of
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}
, it follows
that these pairs form a certain distributive ring with general-group
properties (
81
)–(
82
). The presence of the field
{\mathbb{R}}

contained in it tells us that these pairs can be realized by the
elements
\mathsf{n}+\mathsf{m}\mkern 1.0mux
of, at most, associative algebra
A
over

{\mathbb{R}}
. Here,
\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
x
is a generator of any
ring’s element beyond
{\mathbb{R}}
, and the habitual
+
replaces the sign

\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}
. Multiplication of two such elements

\displaystyle(\mathsf{n+m}\mkern 1.0mux)\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}(\mathsf{n^{\prime}+m^{\prime}}\mkern 1.0mux)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{n\mkern 1.0mun^{\prime}+(m\mkern 1.0mun^{\prime}+n\mkern 1.0mum^{\prime})}\mkern 1.0mux+\mathsf{m\mkern 1.0mum^{\prime}}\mkern 1.0mux^{2}=\cdots

immediately shows that result does not depend on order of
factors, i. e.,

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{n^{\prime}+m^{\prime}}\mkern 1.0mux)\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}(\mathsf{n+m}\mkern 1.0mux)\;,

due to permutability of
\{\mathsf{n,m,n^{\prime},m^{\prime}}\}
between each other and
of any
x
with itself. This is a direct consequence of
2-dimensionality of the algebra
A
; it must be commutative.
Invoking now the well-known Frobenius theorem on associative and
commutative structures containing the field
{\mathbb{R}}

[
180
]
, we
arrive once again at a multiplication of the form (
84
).
Körner puts this point as follows: “The complex numbers constitute
the largest system of objects that most people are content to call
numbers”
[
99
, p. 230]
.

7.6.1.
Topologies on numbers

Yet another reasoning about exclusivity of
{\mathbb{C}}
-numbers follows
from matching the topological and algebraic properties of the
general number systems
[
138
, sect. 27]
. The case in hand is
the uniqueness and non-arbitrariness in the emergence of the
topological field
{\mathbb{C}}
; Pontryagin (1932). In our case, we have
two continuums—numerical symbols
\mathsf{n}
and
\mathsf{m}
, each
of which, by the very method of constructing the

\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}
-objects (
36
), is equipped only with the
natural ordering
<
. Since we do not have any more math-structures
yet, the topology, continuity, and limits on each of the continuums
can already be introduced with respect to this relation. For one
example, there is no need to introduce the topology a priori by
creating the arithmetical operation of multiplication/divisibility
of rationals (and a concept of the prime integer), as is conducted
in the
p
-adic approaches to
QM

[
201
,
196
,
94
]
.
The ‘non-naturalness’ of multiplication as compared with addition
was already noted above. Moreover, in the
p
-adic versions for a
numerical domain, the topologically and physically required matching
between the natural ordering, connection, and continuity

[
138
, Ch. 4]
is destroyed, and the approaches themselves
stipulate the existence of the
observations
numbers with a
comprehensive arithmetic. At the same time, questions about the
‘structure’ of the physical
x
-space at Planck’s scale and about
measurements by rationals (see motivation in
[
196
,
201
]
) have not yet emerged, because we are not relying on physical
conceptions and are not yet introducing these notions as numerical.
The
x
-space itself is as of yet absent, and D. Mermin

[
123
]
overtly claims along these lines that “when I hear
that spacetime becomes a foam at the Planck scale, I don’t reach for
my gun”. From the low-level empiricism standpoint, any objects,
apart from the
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
-continuality and frequencies
\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}
, call for
independent axioms. In turn, the primary nature of the

{\mathbb{R}}
-continuality itself follows directly from the boolean

2^{\mathfrak{T}}
(p.
4.1
) and
\Sigma
-postulate of infinity.

8.
State space

Quantum states … cannot be ‘found out’

— W. Zurek
[
148
, p. 428]

… quantum theory refuses to offer any picture

of what is actually going on out there

— D. Mermin (1988)

8.1.
Linear vector space

Once replication
(\widehat{N\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu,M})
of brace

\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
has acquired a
binary character

(
91
)

(\widehat{N\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu,M})\big(\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu\big)\quad\mathrel{\hbox{\scalebox{1.3}[1]{\hbox{$\Leftarrow\mkern-13.0mu\Rightarrow$}}}}\quad\big((\mathsf{N},\mathsf{M})\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}(\mathsf{n},\mathsf{m})\big)|\alpha\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}=\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\;,

the difference between ‘what is replicated’ and ‘how many times’
disappears. A symbol
|\alpha\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
of the eigen-state has been
attached to the abstract
{\mathbb{C}}
-number
\mathfrak{a}
. Construing this
point as a quantum analog of re-choosing (liberation of) the
measurement units (p.
7.3
), we obtain that the two
formal states
\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
and
\mathfrak{b}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
are
always connected by a certain number operator
{}\kern 7.66673pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.66673pt{}\mathfrak{p}
:

\mathfrak{b}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}={}\kern 7.66673pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.66673pt{}\mathfrak{p}\mkern 1.0mu\big(\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\big)\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad{}\kern 7.66673pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.66673pt{}\mathfrak{p}\rightleftarrows\mathfrak{b}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}\mathfrak{a}^{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}1}\;.

Manipulating the numbers becomes independent of symbols

|\alpha\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
. The way to formalize this is to think of generic
states
\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\in\textsf{{H}}
as the ‘solid characters’

(
92
)

\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\rightarrowtail|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle\in{\mathbb{H}}\;,

i. e., as the
|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle
-elements of a new set
{\mathbb{H}}
, which is
equipped with the
{}\kern 7.66673pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.66673pt{}\mathfrak{p}
-replication images
represented by the
\mathfrak{p}
-family (
\mathfrak{p}\in{\mathbb{C}}
) of maps

(
93
)

{\mathbb{C}}\mathbin{\mathchar 514}{\mathbb{H}}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to2.77779pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mapsto$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\cdot}$}}{\mathbb{H}}:\qquad\mathfrak{p}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle=|\boldsymbol{\Phi}\rangle\in{\mathbb{H}}\;,

and which is obliged to inherit the structure (
91
). This
inheritance says that the coordination of
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}
-multiplication in
(
91
) with the replication’s
\mathfrak{p}
-realization is
performed by a new operation
\boldsymbol{\cdot}
of the unary kind on
{\mathbb{H}}
,
i. e., (
93
), which should be subordinated to the rule

(
94
)

\mathfrak{p}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\big(\mathfrak{a}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\big)=(\mathfrak{p}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}\mathfrak{a})\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\mkern 1.0mu\qquad(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{a}\in{\mathbb{C}}\mkern 1.0mu,\quad|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\in{\mathbb{H}})\;.

Due to this connection between operations
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}
and
\boldsymbol{\cdot}
, the
latter is usually referred to as ‘multiplication’ as well, however
such an intuition with dropping the word “unary” may have an
implication
[
28
, sect. 6.2]
. An analogous rule had already
occurred in the relationship (
61
) between the
\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}
-number

{\mathbb{C}}
-structure and the
(\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}})
-group superposition, i. e., when
the multiplicative structures
\{\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}},\boldsymbol{\cdot}\}
were not available
yet.

Among replication operators
{}\kern 7.66673pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.66673pt{}\mathfrak{p}
, there exists an
identical transformation

{}\kern 7.66673pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.66673pt{}\mathfrak{p}={}\kern 6.50005pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to7.24329pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.50005pt{}\mathds{I}:\qquad\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\rightarrowtail$}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox to11.11115pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\mathds{I}\mkern 1.0mu}\mkern-1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}}\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\;,

to which a symbol of the numerical unity
\mathfrak{p}=\mathds{1}

corresponds. From this, in accordance with
(
92
)–(
93
), there follows the rule

\mathds{1}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle=|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\forall\mkern 1.0mu\mkern 1.0mu|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle\in{\mathbb{H}}\;.

It is clear that the
\vbox{\hbox{$($}}{\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\vbox{\hbox{$)$}}
-multiplication needs
to be agreed with the
\uplus
-union. Let us make use of the fact
that an object of (quantum) replication may be not only the unitary
brace
\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
, which is
equivalent to the eigen-element
\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
, but a

(\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to9.58336pt{\hfil\hbox{$+$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\smallfrown$}})
-sum of the like objects and, in general, any constituents
of quantum ensembles (see p.
70
). Therefore the

{}\kern 7.66673pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.66673pt{}\mathfrak{p}
-replication

(
95
)

{}\kern 7.66673pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.66673pt{}\mathfrak{p}\mkern 1.0mu\big(\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{b}\mkern 1.0mu|\beta\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\big)=\cdots

is known to have its twin-sum

(
96
)

\cdots=\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}\mkern 1.0mu|\beta\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}=\cdots

with certain coefficients
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}
,
\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}
.

Let us, for the moment, give back (
95
) to the initial
language of operators/brace according to the scheme

(
97
)

(\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\underbrace{\widehat{N,M}}$}\hss}\lower 12.91663pt\hbox to7.5pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\textstyle\mathfrak{p}$}\hfil})\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\underbrace{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-1.2pt\radical 0{{}^{\prime}}{},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\kern-1.2pt\radical 0{{}^{\prime}}{}}$}\hss}\lower 12.91663pt\hbox to7.5pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\textstyle\mathfrak{a}$}\hfil}\}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to9.58336pt{\hfil\hbox{$+$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\smallfrown$}}\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\underbrace{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-1.2pt\radical 0{{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}}{},\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}\kern-1.2pt\radical 0{{}^{\prime\mkern-1.5mu\prime}}{}}$}\hss}\lower 12.91663pt\hbox to7.5pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\textstyle\mathfrak{b}$}\hfil}\}\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\;.

Take also into account a pre-image of operation
\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to9.58336pt{\hfil\hbox{$+$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\smallfrown$}}
on objects
(
95
), i. e., the
\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}
. Then, (

) and content of
sects.

–

certainly shows that the expression

) must be of the form

\cdots=(\mathfrak{p}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}\mathfrak{a})\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}(\mathfrak{p}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}\mathfrak{b})\mkern 1.0mu|\beta\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}=\cdots\;.

Reconverting, by (
94
), expressions like

(\mathfrak{p}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}\mathfrak{a})\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
into the operatorial

{}\kern 7.66673pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.66673pt{}\mathfrak{p}(\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}})
, we complete the ellipsis

\cdots={}\kern 7.66673pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.66673pt{}\mathfrak{p}\mkern 1.0mu\big(\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\big)\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}{}\kern 7.66673pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.66673pt{}\mathfrak{p}\mkern 1.0mu\big(\mathfrak{b}\mkern 1.0mu|\beta\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\big)\;.

Passing now to the
\mathfrak{p}
-number and to the
|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle
-objects
(
92
), i. e., replacing

\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\alpha\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\rightarrowtail|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle
and

\mathfrak{b}\mkern 1.0mu|\beta\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\rightarrowtail|\boldsymbol{\Phi}\rangle
, one derives an additivity
of operation
\boldsymbol{\cdot}
when acting on a sum:

\mathfrak{p}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\big(|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}|\boldsymbol{\Phi}\rangle\big)=\mathfrak{p}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}\mathfrak{p}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Phi}\rangle\;.

Here, the
H
-addition
\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}
has been carried over to the
group
{\mathbb{H}}
as a new symbol
\mathbin{\hat{+}}
. This is nothing but a
distributive coordination of the

\vbox{\hbox{$($}}{\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\vbox{\hbox{$)$}}
-multiplication with the group
addition
\mathbin{\hat{+}}
.

In a similar way, through a number operator, one establishes yet
another relation

\mathfrak{a}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}\mathfrak{b}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle=(\mathfrak{a}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}\mathfrak{b})\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle

between
\boldsymbol{\cdot}
and
\mathbin{\hat{+}}
. Its origin is equivalent to (
61
).
From the constructs above, it is not difficult to see that we have
examined all the possibilities of
{\mathbb{C}}
-replicating the
superpositions (
60
) or their constituents, which is why we
have exhausted all the compatibility rules that stem from the two
fundamental operations—replication and union (
\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}
).

Thus, having considered the passage
(
92
)–(
93
) as a final homomorphism of the

H
-group elements
\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}
onto the objects

|\boldsymbol{\Xi}\rangle\in{\mathbb{H}}
, i. e., adjusting the previous concept of a state and
of
DataSource
(p.
51
), we infer the following.

•

The minimal and mathematically invariant bearer of the
observation’s empiricism is an abstract space
{\mathbb{H}}
of states

|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle
of the system
\mathcal{S}
. The structural properties

(
98
)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\big\{|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\mkern 1.0mu,|\boldsymbol{\Phi}\rangle\mkern 1.0mu,\ldots\big\}\mkern 1.0mu\quad

\displaystyle\lceil\text{commutative group under operation $\mathbin{\hat{+}}$}\rceil\;,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\{\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu,\mathfrak{b}\mkern 1.0mu,\ldots\}\mkern 1.0mu\quad

\displaystyle\lceil\text{field of complex
numbers \eqref{g+},
\eqref{nma}--\eqref{riC}}\rceil\;,

\displaystyle{}\kern 7.00006pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.00006pt{}\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathrel{\mathop{:}}\mathfrak{a}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\in{\mathbb{H}}\mkern 1.0mu\quad

\displaystyle\begin{array}[t]{@{}l}\lceil\text{closedness under operation $\boldsymbol{\cdot}$}\\
\text{\phantom{(}$\;\mathrel{\hbox{\scalebox{1.3}[1]{\hbox{$\Leftarrow\mkern-13.0mu\Rightarrow$}}}}$ operator automorphism
${}\kern 7.00006pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.00006pt{}\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle$}\rceil\;,\end{array}

(
101
)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathfrak{a}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\big(\mathfrak{b}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\big)&=(\mathfrak{a}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}\mathfrak{b})\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\mkern 1.0mu,\quad&\mathfrak{a}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}\mathfrak{b}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle&=(\mathfrak{a}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}}\mathfrak{b})\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\;,\\
\mathds{1}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle&=|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\mkern 1.0mu,&\mathfrak{a}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\big(|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}|\boldsymbol{\Phi}\rangle\big)&=\mathfrak{a}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}\mathfrak{a}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Phi}\rangle\end{aligned}

of the space coincide with the axioms of a linear vector space
(
LVS
) over the field
{\mathbb{C}}
.

Attention is drawn to the fact that this is the first place in our
construct where the word ‘
linear
’ has appeared, and even the
superposition principle, page
•
‣
6.3
, was formulated
without using this term. Commutativity is necessary for linearity
but is not confined to this. The ‘axiom list’
(
98
)–(
101
) should also be complemented with a
declaration of the global
D
-number value (
55
) established
above.

In a nutshell, the nature of the quantum state space is twofold:
group superposition (
60
) and operator nature of the
‘
\mathfrak{a}
-numbering’ the elements of the group. It admits the

{\mathbb{C}}
-field scalars as operators. Relations (
101
) describe
rules of ‘interplay’ between all the objects. It is known that such
formations, while being implemented by a binary algebra of numbers,
turn into the vector spaces and modules
[
101
, Ch. 5]
,

[
180
, sects.
I
(7.1–2),
II
(13.4)]
. Concerning
the consistency of these rules—say, of numerical distributivity
(
82
)—with relations (
101
), see the
work
[
145
]
.

Remark
15
.

A certain oddity is in place.
QM
-empiricism is such that the
standard definition of
LVS
by the all-too-familiar axioms
(
98
)–(
101
) is more ‘
non
physical’ by its
nature than the ‘generalistic’ abstraction of a group with operator
automorphisms of the group
H
-structure itself

[
26
, sect.
I
(4.2)]
,
[
101
]
. A point like this
might be expected, though. This is because, as noted in
sect.
1.2
, meaning all of tokens in (
1
) and their
origin are entirely unknown, and linearity of
QM
is radically
different from other ‘linearities’ in physics.

All told, the appearance sequence of the mathematical structures is
as follows:

\displaystyle\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptsets, union $\cup$, \ldots\penalty$\rceil$}\;\rightarrowtail\;\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptsemigroup (sect.\penalty\kern 2.45973pt\ignorespaces\penalty\hskip 0.0pt\ref{semi})\penalty$\rceil$}

\displaystyle\rightarrowtail\;\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptgroup {{H}} (sect.\penalty\kern 2.45973pt\ignorespaces\penalty\hskip 0.0pt\ref{ssuper})\penalty$\rceil$}\;\rightarrowtail\;\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptnumbers \& arithmetics (sect.\penalty\kern 2.45973pt\ignorespaces\penalty\hskip 0.0pt\ref{Numbers})\penalty$\rceil$}

\displaystyle\rightarrowtail\;\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptcompatibility of the
group and numbers (sect.\penalty\kern 2.45973pt\ignorespaces\penalty\hskip 0.0pt\ref{lvs})\penalty$\rceil$}

\displaystyle\rightarrowtail\;\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptthe abstract {LVS}\ and its
bases\penalty$\rceil$}\;.

This sequence is rigid, such as the box-diagram in
sect.
1.3
, so the structure of
LVS
cannot be weakened
because we have the two fundamental principia
(
II
and

III
) in between the semigroup, the
group, and the vector space.

8.2.
Bases, countability, and infinities

From a ban on transitions
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}\mathrel{\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{n}
under

s\neq n
, it follows that unitary
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}
-brace (
36
)
correspond to vectors
\mathfrak{a}_{s}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{s}\rangle
that are
linearly inexpressible through each other. Aside from the general
ensemble brace (
34
), no other elements exist, and all of
them are in one-to-one correspondence with the vector
representations
\mathfrak{a}_{1}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}\mathfrak{a}_{2}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{2}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}\cdots
. Each such vector has a statistical
pre-image (
34
), and vice versa; there are no gaps. This
means that the system of vectors

\{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}\rangle,|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{2}\rangle,\ldots\}
forms a basis of
{\mathbb{H}}
as
the basis of
LVS
—
basis of an observable
\mathscr{A}
or

\mathscr{A}
-basis
—and the number of symbols
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{s}\rangle
is its
dimension:
\dim{\mathbb{H}}=\textsf{D}
. The
\textsf{D}=\infty
case, just like
anything associated with infinity, cannot be formalized without
topology, and its presence is presumed, but this discussion is
dropped. We just remark that even earlier, when arising the
2-dimensional continuum, we have silently assumed the

({\mathbb{R}}\mathbin{\mathchar 514}{\mathbb{R}})
-product topology on it. This supposition is
natural, inasmuch as it does not involve additional
constructions/requirements. Thus if properties
(
98
)–(
101
) are directly accepted as empirical,
then the mathematical rigors augment them axiomatically on the
outside, because one constructs the mathematical theory.

The micro-transition
\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to8.33339pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}
in sect.
2.1
is a
solitary entity. This means that the number of eigen

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{s}
-primitives for an actual instrument may be either
finite or discretely unbounded. We base this on the fact that
continual formations is a product of mathematics rather than
empiricism; see also
[
113
, p. 35]
. The
{\mathfrak{T}}
-set, as an
example, is also non-continual, but that premise may even be given
up, because only a discrete portion of this set is present at
arguments (transitions
\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.90286pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}
). Notice incidentally
that continuum, along with the number, does not feature in the

ZF
-axioms
[
100
]
but is also created, just as “an infinity is
actually not given to us at all, but is … extrapolated through an
intellectual process.”
[
96
, p. 55; Hilbert–Bernays]
; see
also the book
[
102
]
for the conceptualization of infinity.
One obtains a countability of the
\mathscr{A}
-basis. Hence it follows a
completeness of
{\mathbb{H}}
and countability of dimension (
55
), as
of the number
LVS
-invariant:

(
102
)

\textsf{D}=2,3,\ldots,\aleph_{\textsf{o}}\qquad(=\dim{\mathbb{H}})\;.

Finally, let us mention the following. The basis is a term that in
no way is present in the abstract axiomatics
(
98
)–(
101
), and
LVS
on its own account does
not contain a motive for introducing that concept. But empirically,
the
{\mathbb{H}}
-space is arising entirely and ab initio in all possible
linear combinations over
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}\rangle
, i. e., through

\mathscr{A}
-bases. Because of this, in order for an abstract
LVS
to
become the quantum state-space the
LVS
should be considered as
being accompanied by the concepts bases and changes thereof.
Conforming to such a requirement and the formal existence of a basis
is given by a nontrivial math theorem invoking the axiom of choice

[
26
, sect.
II
(7.1)]
.

8.3.
The theorem

The states
|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle
and sums thereof, at the moment, form a formal
family of different elements. Recall that symbols

\{\approx,\not\approx\}
in pt.
R
, as from
the end of sect.
5.4
, have been replaced with the standard ones

\{=,\neq\}
. The physical aspects of
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
were being left aside so
far, and, for example,
|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle
and
\mathfrak{c}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle
were the
different vectors of the
{\mathbb{H}}
-space. However,

•

empiricism (deals with and) yields originally
not states

and superpositions thereof
but

|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\rangle
-representations
.

It is these representations (alone) that carry information about
statistics
(\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{1},\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{2},\ldots)
through coefficients
\mathfrak{a}_{j}
. The
replicative character of
\mathfrak{c}
-multipliers and

\Sigma
-postulate entail however that the two vectors

\mathds{1}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle
and
\mathfrak{c}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle
should correspond
to the one and the same statistics

\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle(\mathscr{D})}=(1,0,\ldots)={\tilde{\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}\mkern-1.0mu}{}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle(\mathscr{D})}

under an observation
\mathscr{D}
with the eigen collection

\{\mathds{1}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}},\ldots\}
.

Let us write the equalities

(
103
)

\begin{array}[]{rcccl}\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle(\mathscr{D})}&\twoheadleftarrow\quad\underbrace{\mathds{1}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle}&=&\underbrace{\mathfrak{a}_{1}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}\mathfrak{a}_{2}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{2}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}\cdots}&\twoheadrightarrow\quad\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle(\mathscr{A})}\;,\\
&\scriptstyle\text{observation $\mathscr{D}$}&&\scriptstyle\text{observation $\mathscr{A}$}\\
{\tilde{\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}\mkern-1.0mu}{}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle(\mathscr{D})}&\twoheadleftarrow\quad\overbrace{\mathfrak{c}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle}&=&\overbrace{\mathfrak{c}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\big(\mathfrak{a}_{1}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}\mathfrak{a}_{2}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{2}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}\cdots\big)}&\twoheadrightarrow\quad{\tilde{\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}\mkern-1.0mu}{}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle(\mathscr{A})}\end{array}

and look at them in the following order: the first line from right
to left, and the second in the reverse direction. Their right hand
sides are the carriers of some statistics
\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle(\mathscr{A})}
and

{\tilde{\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}\mkern-1.0mu}{}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle(\mathscr{A})}

,

{}\kern 7.26814pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to4.56386pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.26814pt{}n\mkern 1.0mu(\mathfrak{a}|\alpha\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}})

Remark
16
.

As in sect.
7.3
, we draw again attention to a hidden and
(logically) unremovable extension of the physical units’ concept.

•

“…
units
. Despite the rudimentary nature of
units, they are probably the most inconsistently understood concept
in all of physics … where do units come from?”.

.
S. Gryb & F. Mercati
[
72
, p. 91]

Surprisingly, the naïve and straightforward conjunction of this
concept with an abstract number seems to contravene the
multiplication arithmetic; but not the addition one. The typical
example illustrates the point:

(2\mkern 1.0mu\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{kg})\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}(3\mkern 1.0mu\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{kg})\neq 6\mkern 1.0mu\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{kg}\hskip 17.70859pt(3\mkern 1.0mu\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{sheep}+5\mkern 1.0mu\mkern 1.0mu\psi\neq 8\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{St\"{u}ck})

(see also
[
99
, items (4) and (5) on p. 16]
). On the other
hand,
2\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}(3\mkern 1.0mu\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{kg})=6\mkern 1.0mu\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{kg}
and

(2\mkern 1.0mu\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{kg})+(3\mkern 1.0mu\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{kg})=5\mkern 1.0mu\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{kg}
, and the
kg
may be
replaced here with any other entity: the classical mètres, the
abstract ‘Quanten Stücke
\psi
’, and the like. They have no any
operational significance, but one cannot get by without them.

The numeral characters acquire their usual abstract-numerical
meaning—mathematization
[
113
]
—only when we throw
(sect.
7.3
) the ‘units’
\lceil\textsf{St\"{u}ck}
,
{}^{\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}}
C,

sheep
,
\psi
,
\ldots\rceil
out of data like

\lceil 5\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{St\"{u}ck}
,

C, 5
sheep
,
5\mkern 1.0mu\psi
,

\ldots\rceil
. The symbol ‘

as in

\lceil\text{the abstraction $\mathsf{5}$}\rceil\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{{}\kern 6.26744pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to5.69258pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.26744pt{}5^{\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}}\text{C}}{{}\kern 6.26744pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to5.69258pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.26744pt{}1^{\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 8718$}}$}}}\text{C}}=\frac{{}\kern 6.26744pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to5.69258pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.26744pt{}5\>{}|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle}{{}\kern 6.26744pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to5.69258pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.26744pt{}1\>|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle}=\cdots=\frac{{}\kern 6.26744pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to5.69258pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.26744pt{}5\>\textsf{unit}}{{}\kern 6.26744pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to5.69258pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.26744pt{}1\>\textsf{unit}}\;.

It may be even said that creating the number
\mathsf{n}
(from its
operator
{}\kern 6.83458pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to3.8032pt{}\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\kern-6.83458pt{}n

Incidentally, within this physical and quantum context,

•

the
LVS
itself should be regarded as no less a primary
math-structure than the numbers themselves. Empiricism gives birth
to both these structures together. Neither of them is more/less
abstract/necessary than the other. Behind them is certainly a
commutative group with operator automorphisms over it, and
“numbers” is just a shortened term for that operators. Therein lies
their nature (sect.
7.2
).

The habitual physics’ construct

\lceil
number
\rceil
{}\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}{}
\lceil
physical unit
\rceil
exemplifies in
effect the simplest (1-dimensional)

. However, the structure
“the
LVS
”, in contrast to the ‘bare’ arithmetic, simply ‘does not
forget and keeps’ an operator nature (unary multiplication
\boldsymbol{\cdot}
)
of the structure “the number” and its empirical inseparability from
the notion of the unit:

\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\text{${}\kern 7.00006pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.83109pt{}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.00006pt{}2\>({}\kern 7.00006pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vbox to6.83109pt{}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}{}$}\kern-7.00006pt{}3\>\textsf{unit})\;\mathrel{\hbox{\scalebox{1.3}[1]{\hbox{$\Leftarrow\mkern-13.0mu\Rightarrow$}}}}\;2\boldsymbol{\cdot}(3\boldsymbol{\cdot}\textsf{unit})$}$\crcr\kern 2.0pt\cr\tiny$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.4mu\bracelu\mkern-0.4mu\leaders{\vrule height=0.59pt}{\hfill}\leaders{\vrule height=0.59pt}{\hfill}\braceru$\cr}}$}\hss}\lower 12.91663pt\hbox to130.44452pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\text{vector space}$}\hfil}\quad\rightarrowtail\cdots\text{\ abstracting\ }\cdots\rightarrowtail\quad\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to38.38687pt{\hfil\hbox{$\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\text{$(2\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 514$}}$}}3)\>\textsf{\sout{unit}}$}$\crcr\kern 2.0pt\cr\tiny$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.4mu\bracelu\mkern-0.4mu\leaders{\vrule height=0.59pt}{\hfill}\leaders{\vrule height=0.59pt}{\hfill}\braceru$\cr}}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 12.91663pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\text{arithmetic}$}\;.

A direct corollary of this point is the fact that
principium
II
can in no way be given
up or disregarded. This would be tantamount to impossibility to

[

, sect. 6]
.

At the moment, it is worthwhile to summarize where we stand. As we
have seen, nothing above and beyond what was used in constructing

)–(
