{\boldsymbol{\mathbin{\mathchar 8712}}}(\mathfrak{a}_{1},|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}\rangle;\mathfrak{a}_{2},|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{2}\rangle;\ldots)=\mathfrak{a}_{1}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}\mathfrak{a}_{2}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{2}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}\cdots\;.

In the first place, one establishes
a genesis of the quantal
discreteness. Discriminating is an isolated act in the very nature
of the perception process
: ‘one thing is distinct from another’,
‘the controlling the minimal begins with a distincting of something
the two’, and the like (sect.
2.2
). Accordingly,
“indivisibility, or “individuality”, characterizing the elementary

[

, p. 203; N. Bohr]

.

The theorization does also bear on preparation

We also clarify the formalization of measurement/preparation and of

\mathfrak{a}\mapsto\lceil\text{statistics }\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{j}\rceil,\quad|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}\rangle\mapsto\lceil\text{spectra }\alpha_{j}\rceil\\[4.30554pt]
\text{tensorial structure of the ${\mathbb{H}}$\penalty-\hskip 0.0ptspace}\end{array}

\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.4mu\bracelu\mkern-0.4mu\leaders{\vrule height=0.59pt}{\hfill}\leaders{\vrule height=0.59pt}{\hfill}\braceru

.

In other words, if a concept is a numerical one already in
empiricism—frequencies, spectra, etc—then its meaningful
formalization by means of a mathematical definitio can only resort
to mathematics that we have at our disposal:
LVS
and algebra of
numbers.

Thus numerical quantities in the entire theory are initially divided
up by their emergence mechanism
(
II
): the intrinsic abstracta and
reifications (
9.1
). Without such a division, the

circular logic is inevitable
, and the above-mentioned ‘unit’
treatment of numbers would still be supplemented with the task of
their observational interpretation complicated by
two-dimensionality. This task would be present in formalism not
merely as a problem but as an inherently intractable challenge.
Actually,
any entity can be identified with numbers
, and this
is why, the quantum empiricism and
principium
II
—paradigm of the
very number in the physical theory—insist on the need to pay the
closest possible attention to all these things.

Remark
17
.

In this regard, the situation has a parallel with the familiar
history of electrodynamics of moving bodies; as was pointed out just
before principium
III
. Lorentz’s
contraction theory is inconsistent, if the space-time tags to events
are not linked up to the empirically precise and operationally
defined concepts in different reference frames: clocks,
simultaneity, rigid rods, distances, and the like.

In quantum case, the chief subject of empirical definition is a
concept of the number and of the ‘numerical value of …’.
Otherwise, the meaning given to the conception of a quantitative
theory itself has been blurring. The ‘quantum numbers’
{\mathbb{C}}
are
built up from the reals, and the latter have an operator nature
(sect.
7.2
). But the complexes
{\mathbb{C}}
, being also operators
and unlike the reals, never act (operationally) on the reified
quantities. They do act on the abstract
|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle
-elements of the
abstract commutative group
{\mathbb{H}}
. Recall that this group and
superposition principle were arising before the numbers.

We have seen now that “it is quite wrong to try founding a theory on
observable magnitudes[/categories] alone”
[
148
, p. 504; Einstein, in
a talk (1926) to Heisenberg]
, and resorting to the
physical notions—the camouflaged

M
-observations—is prohibited; see also
Remark
2
. The attempts to use statistics at the very
beginning of the theory are known
[
127
,
78
,
12
,
15
,
51
,
109
,
111
]
, and rightly so; they were
initiated by H. Margenau (1936)
[
9
, Ch. 15]
. However, the
scheme just given is rigid. To obviate the premature appearance of
the very need for an interpretation, the scheme must not be varied.
Being a sequence of steps, it provides in essence an answer to the
principium
II
.

9.2.
Operations on numbers

The last step in this scheme contains, in particular, the map

\mathfrak{a}\mapsto\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}
, i. e., measurement (
50
). Its form should
be established in its own right—Born’s rule
[
27
]
. To
illustrate, the naïve transformation of negative numbers

\mathsf{p}
into the actually perceived quantities by a ‘seemingly
natural’ rule such as
|{\pm}\mathsf{p}|
is not correct and does not
follow from anywhere. For the built algebra
(
98
)–(
101
), the operation
\pm\mathsf{p}\mapsto|{\pm}\mathsf{p}|
is extrinsic and illegal, it is nowhere to be had.
According to ideology of sect.
1.3
, not only
objects—numbers, vectors, quantities, characteristics,
etc—but also all the math operations should be created because
one without the other is meaningless. The more so as the numerical
object of the theory—the complex pair

(\pm\mathsf{p},\pm\mathsf{q})
—is as yet single, it contains a
principally ‘non-materializable’ ingredient (sect.
7.4
) and
behaves as a whole. With regards to empiricism, the negative and the

{\mathbb{C}}
-numbers are equally ‘nonexistent, fictitious entities’, since
the state’s mathematics (
98
)–(
101
) has not been
supplemented with the doctrine of ‘empirically perceived’ quantities
(
9.1
). As a matter of fact, the step-by-step transformation
of the binary operation
\cup
to symbols
\uplus
,
\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}
,

\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to9.58336pt{\hfil\hbox{$+$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\smallfrown$}}
and, finally, to operations
\{\mathbin{\hat{+}},\boldsymbol{\cdot},\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8712$}}$}},\mathbin{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchar 8716$}}$}}\}

does not terminate at states. Algebra (
98
)–(
101
)
will be further required to create, now, the mathematically correct
calculation rules of the proper observational quantities.

The foregoing is amplified by the fact that
pr.
II
has been involved in the
classical description and in vindication/refutation of, say, the
hidden variable theories. Here, numbers are identified with the
reified quantities, and subtraction is taken for granted from the
outset. However, the negative quantities are also being created
here, and they are constructed in the same manner as the ‘quantum
zero’ for the
H
-group in sect.
6.3
.

Indeed,

•

the instrument indications and physical quantities are not
numbers, nor the (“pointer”) states;

“detector … does not measure a field or an
S
-matrix-element”
(R. Haag (2010)). They are no more than notches, and ‘negative
notches’ are introduced prior to mathematics of symbols according to
the following subconsciously intended scheme. The self-apparent
physical conventionality, which has been calling ‘an addition’ of
two such notches, must produce, in accord with the
supra-mathematical requirements of physics, what is named ‘nought,
zero’. Two waves at a point, for instance, compensate each other.
The result is asserted to be identical with a zero the mathematical,
and that is the subtraction.

The classical ‘explanations’ are the ones with use of
compensations/subtractions (see Remark
13
), whereas
the
minus
we have been accustomed
is a fairly abstract
construction in its own right
. J. Baez and J. Dolan best reflected
the situation, observing on page 37 of
[
11
]
that “half an
apple is easier to understand than a negative apple!”; on the same
page, a good discussion of division is given. In this respect, one
might state that the very classical physics needs an
interpretation—in terms of strictly positive ‘the number of
Stücke’. Mathematization of empiricism into numbers is not a
distinctive feature of quantum description. However, comprehending
‘abstracting the minus sign’ is not confined even to this. A word of
explanation is necessary with regard to the situation.

Mathematics formalizes
[
100
]
the positive/negative

\pm\mathsf{p}
into the pairs’ classes
(m,n)
being equivalent with
respect to an ‘adding’ of the class
(\ell,\ell)
(the ‘zero’):

\displaystyle(+\mathsf{m})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=(m,0)\approx(m+\ell,0+\ell)\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad(-\mathsf{n})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=(0,n)\approx(0+\ell,n+\ell)\;,

(
111
)

\displaystyle\pm\mathsf{p}\quad\mathrel{\hbox{\scalebox{1.3}[1]{\hbox{$\Leftarrow\mkern-13.0mu\Rightarrow$}}}}\quad(\mathsf{m}-\mathsf{n})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=(m,n)\approx(m+\ell,n+\ell)\;,

where
m,n,\ell
are to be seen as ‘something strictly positive’.
This ‘adding’ is yet another tacitly assumed and much more abstract
action: addition of objects of some other kind—‘positive couples’

(m,n)
. Technically, at an appropriate place of
sect.
7
, we had to introduce such classes and to assign
their own algebraic operations for them. The result might be called
the ‘genuine’ arithmetic (of ‘the positives’) and could be enlarged
to the ‘complete arithmetic’ with multiplication and division.

In consequence, the single-token object
(+\mathsf{m})
or
(-\mathsf{n})
,
which we perceive as self-evident
(cf. pr.
II
), is a highly unobvious
construction—the generic equivalence-class of two-token

(m,n)
-abstractions (
111
). Essence of the symbol of a
negative number
(-\mathsf{n})
is revealed only when contrasting the
two positive ones. Exactly the same situation has occurred when
deriving the superposition principle in (
51
)–(
52
) and
(
57
).

It is clear that once all the
\pm\mathsf{p}
-numbers, and the ‘normal’
positive
+\mathsf{p}
’s among, have been formalized into the
equivalences (
111
), the fact that they possess any ‘natural
meanings’, such as the ‘operation of the quantity

\mathsf{p}\mapsto|\mathsf{p}|
’ invented above, becomes more than
unnatural; the abstract class operations that appear out of nowhere.
Similarly with
{\mathbb{Q}}
-numbers and their
{\mathbb{R}}
-extension: classes of
equivalent pairs
(\mathsf{n}/\mathsf{m})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=(n,m)\approx(n\mkern 1.0mu\ell,m\mkern 1.0mu\ell)
.

9.3.
Naturalness of abstracta

We thus infer that rejection or disregard of the similar ‘naturally
abstract’ set-theoretic models would be tantamount to the rejection
of the minus sign even in the elementary physics. This is an absurd,
but its root is a need for abstracting. On the other hand, the
motivated deduction of these models cannot be replaced with (hidden)
axiomatic assumptions or with ready-made math-structures. Such an
ambivalence is, in our view, one reason why the problem with
‘decrypting’ quantum postulates is so difficult; it touches on the
metamathematical and metaphysical aspects of the very thinking

[
102
,
96
,
35
,
200
,
98
]
. The stream of
subconsciously abstractive homomorphisms

(
112
)

\lceil\text{pt.\penalty\kern 2.77802pt\ignorespaces\penalty\hskip 0.0pt}\hyperlink{S}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\textbf{{S}}}\mkern 1.0mu,\mkern 1.0mu{\mathfrak{T}}\mkern 1.0mu\rceil\rightarrowtail\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu,{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\}\rightarrowtail\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu,\>\cup\}\rightarrowtail\cdots\rightarrowtail\big\{\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}\Xi\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}_{\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\mkern 1.0mu,\>\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\uplus$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.5pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu$}\hfil}\mkern 1.0mu;\>\ldots\big\}\rightarrowtail\cdots\rightarrowtail\\
\vbox{\hbox{\large$\big\{$}}\big[\begin{smallmatrix}\mu\\
\lambda\end{smallmatrix}\big]\mkern 1.0mu{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu,\>\uplus\vbox{\hbox{\large$\big\}$}}\rightarrowtail\cdots\rightarrowtail\{\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu|\Psi\vbox{\hbox{\small$\scriptstyle\mkern-2.0mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}}\mkern 1.0mu,\>\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to10.00002pt{\hfil\hbox{$\raise 0.6458pt\hbox{$+$}$}\hfil}\hss}\lower 2.79857pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\smash{\smallsmile}$}}\}\rightarrowtail\big\{\{{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathsf{m}$}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox to7.94446pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}\hfil}},{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to6.1111pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathsf{n}$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Phi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$}}\}\mkern 1.0mu{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}}\mkern 1.0mu,\>\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to9.58336pt{\hfil\hbox{$+$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\smallfrown$}}\big\}\rightarrowtail\{\mathfrak{a}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\rangle\mkern 1.0mu,\>\mathbin{\hat{+}}\}\rightarrowtail\\
\rightarrowtail\big\{\mathfrak{a}\mkern 1.0mu,|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle;\>\mathbin{\hat{+}},\boldsymbol{\cdot}\big\}\;\mathrel{\hbox{\scalebox{1.3}[1]{\hbox{$\Rightarrow$}}}}\;{\mathbb{H}}

is considerable and is always larger than it seems. In
sects.
3
–
9
, we have described not all of them.
Each such homomorphism is a mapping into a representation by a
model, and for a philosophical discussion of these representations
and the origin of models, see pages 1–230 in
[
182
]
. For
another comment concerning the abstracting/realism, we refer to the
first half of a letter from A. Einstein to H. Samuel in

[
146
, pp. 157–160]
; see also
[

]
.

Thus ‘difficulties’ with complex numbers, stricto sensu, should
already be attributed to the level of the usual negative ones.
Bearing in mind that the minus comes from the equal sign
=

[
120
]
and the equality comes from the scheme

\eqref{o+}\rightarrowtail\eqref{g1}
, both the principia

II
and

III
are very important (and
functioning) also in the classical case. In quantum case, they are
just fundamentally unavoidable for the very creation of the theory.
The nature of
QM
theory, of arithmetic, of complex numbers, and of
their algebras is one and the same.

Transferring the reasoning above to the natural numbers
\mathbb{N}
, the
degrees of classical and quantum abstraction become even
indistinguishable. Empirical motivation leads, in one way or
another, to the standard von Neumann’s representation for ordinals

(
113
)

0\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\varnothing\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad 1\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\{\varnothing\}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad 2\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\{\varnothing,\{\varnothing\}\}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad 3\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\{\varnothing,\{\varnothing\},\{\varnothing,\{\varnothing\}\}\}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\ldots\;,

i. e., to using the
ZF
-axiom of union:
n+1\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\{n\}\cup n

[
100
]
. Therefore, less obvious is the
\mathbb{N}
-numbers
themselves, to be followed by the ordering
<
, topologies,
extensions, generalizations, etc. The formal characterization of
all of the experimental reduces then to the successive creating from
the set-theoretic atoms—unions of sets—some direct products
thereof and mappings into other constructions of the same kind.
Hence, both the physical images ‘being under a ban above’ and
auxiliary structures—dimensionalities/orders, etc—should
equally become homomorphisms onto certain formal constructions
regardless of the description’s classicality/quantumness. The
presence of, say, non-binary operations (
90
) does not stand
out, because their nature does not differ from the one of habitual
subtraction and of division. All of these are involutory structures
that have been mathematically inherited from the empirical
meta-requirements: repetitions
(
M
-paradigm), experimental context
\langle
\mkern 2.0mu\langle\mkern 1.0mu\mathcal{S}
,

\mathbf{M}
,

…
\rangle
\mkern 2.0mu\rangle
, and
covariance
III
(sect.
5.4
).

To close the section we add that the distancing the concepts of
state/
DataSource
and of a physical property is the
continuation of a more primary idea—detaching the proper
macro-perceptions from what is being
represented

theoretically
[
182
,
109
,
111
]
and from conceptualizing
the notions
[
102
]
. As B. Mazur has noted in

[
120
, p. 2]
, “This issue has been with us … forever: the
general question of
abstraction
, as separating what we want
from what we are presented with”, i. e., the separating the ‘bare’
empiricism from mathematics with
\Sigma
-limit and the number.

The atomic constituent of perception—sensory experience—is
an elementary quantum event
[
111
,
77
,
178
,
25
]
,
and it begins and terminates in

(\not\approx_{\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-1.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}})
-distinguishability of

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-clicks (sect.
2.1
). Any continual is a
‘speculative theory’ (act of abstracting), not the underlying
empiricism. Therefore all the further matters—numbers,
arithmetic, cause/effect, (non)inertial reference frames, the
notions of an observer, of a classical event in the Minkowski space,
the spacetime concept itself and coordinates in the relativity
theory (a quantum view of the equivalence principle), device
read-out, tensors, composite systems, symbols
\mathbin{\mathchar 8714}
,
and the like—self-evident as it may seem, are the math add-ons, which
could originate only in the ‘
\cup
-theory’ of sect.
5
.
Following von Weizsäcker, it might be coined the name ‘Ur-theory’.
There are no contradictions in observations themselves, whether we
call them macro- or micro-scopic. Contradictions do arise in the
‘mathematicae being constructed’.

10.
About interpretations

It is … not … a question of
a re-interpretation … quantum

mechanics would have to be objectively false, in order that

another description … than the statistical one be possible

— J. von Neumann

[
131
, p. 325]

… one begins to suspect that all the deep questions

about the meaning of measurement are really empty

— S. Weinberg

“At this point in time it appears that a stalemate has been reached
with regard to the interpretation of quantum mechanics” (E. Tammaro

[
175
, p. 1]
). A “stalemate in which each side refuses to cede
territory but is unable to produce a defining argument that would
change the hearts and minds of the opponents” (M. Schlosshauer

[
154
, p. 227]
).

10.1.
Click, again

The source of the “foundational skirmishes”
[
154
, p. 227]

and the numerous treatments of
QM

[
85
, Ch. 10]
,

[
103
, Ch. 10]
,
[
9
,
150
,
148
,
156
]
—‘the Copenhagen’ among—is the fact that the

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-event and intuitive sense of the

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-primitive (pt.
S
) are a priori
endowed with physical properties, observational/determinative
characteristics of the
DataSource
, and operationality of
the canonical
QM
-concept of the ket-vector
|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle
. A
representative example in this regard is one of the first sentences
from Everett’s PhD: “The state function
\psi
is thought of as
objectively characterizing the physical system … at all times
… independently of our state of knowledge of it”

[
52
, p. 3]
,
[
19
, p. 73]
; and also, on p. 48,
“The general validity of pure wave mechanics,
without any
statistical assertions
, is assumed for
all
physical systems,
including observers and measuring apparata”. And, again the
Everett’s: “The physical ‘reality’ is assumed to be the wave
function of the whole universe itself”
[
61
, p. 100]
,

[
19
, p. 70]
. However, none of these initially exist. The
primitive
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-events’ abstractions

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.90286pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
are all there is.

An important point is that the eigen
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-click (of a
photon/electron in the
epr
-experiment, say) should not be
identified with an
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\rangle
-state. The latter is
re-developable with respect to eigen-states associated with other
click-sets of any other instruments:

|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}\rangle=\mathfrak{b}_{1}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}\mathfrak{b}_{2}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{2}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}\cdots=\mathfrak{c}_{1}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}\mathfrak{c}_{2}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}\cdots=\cdots\;,

which is why it is logically meaningless to attribute one

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-click to that which carries the statistics of other
clicks
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\beta\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{k}
,
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\gamma\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{k}
, … and has nothing in
common with
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
. All the more so the click may not be
related with the physical texts—physically descriptive
collocations such as ‘the measuring act on Bob’s electron reveals
the spin-up state’. As in the ‘cat case’ (sect.
6.3.1
), the
spin-up here is a click-up rather than a state
|\boldsymbol{}{\uparrow}\rangle
.
Similarly, a click (allegedly of a photon) with Alice/Bob has
nothing to do with distance between photons (the locality
‘problem’), with speed of light, nor with a kinematic
‘understanding’ of the photon.

The quantum-detection micro-event is not a classical one as we have
been understanding it, say, in the special relativity. The “click
does not establish the presence of something”

[
178
, p. 761]
, it “is an elementary act of “fact
creation.””
[
64
, Wheeler]
. The facts and phenomena are
made up of clicks. That is to say, the distinguished

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-clicks are not the events spaced at some distant from
each other or at different points in time. These are just clicks
without accounting to them such descriptive notions as distance,
coordinates, point of time
t
, or the picturesque words like
‘dead/alive/…/cat’; of course, the click itself has no
size/duration. Exempli gratia, the particles at accelerators and
their physical properties are observed not as “material bodies in
the proper sense of the word”
[
81
, p. 62]
—this is
impossible—but rather through the abstract detector-snaps.
Neither the electron in interferometer nor the Higgs boson at a
collider are observed in a detector as objects that are finite in
extent; they are not observable entities. ‘A Higgs’ is just a
frequency
5\mkern 1.0mu\sigma
-histogram at LHC.

Likewise, the math-properties of the eigen
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\rangle
and of the
abstract
|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle
can in no way be ‘syncretized’ with

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-events when they are still being accumulated. The
screen scintillation is not a photon and photon is not a
scintillation . Similarly, “the arrival of an electron”

[
55
, p. 3]
at the screen does not mean ‘what is here at
this point in time with a given coordinate is the materialized
particle-electron’. Accompanying the
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\rangle
-states and

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-clicks with the phenomenological and dramatic words
‘up/down/…/alive/dead’ has nothing to do with physics, which is
yet to be created. The click should not be an element of the
language in which
|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle
-terminology, numbers, and physical
properties have been employed whatsoever.

10.2.
Abstraction the state

Then something subsequently referred to as a state (the abstract)
and a measurement (the concrete) is created. However, as already
stressed in sect.
2.4
, process of abstracting is a rather
multistage one (sects.
3
–
9
), and a reduction of
the long sequence (
112
) ‘for physical reasons’ does always
contain phenomenological axioms a priori. Clearly, in the reverse
direction, we confront hard-to-disentangle assumptions and the
well-known axiomatic cycle. The physical considerations and
phenomenology should not be present in fundamentals of quantum
mathematics.

To avoid paradoxes with ‘quantum cats’—“state vector does not
describe … a single cat”
[
13
, p. 37]
) and “One
cannot think about it as in a superposition”
[
154
, p. 134;
D. Greenberger]
, with ‘the presence of a particle here and
there’, or with ‘quantum bomb-testing’ (Elitzur–Vaidman)

[
9
,
70
]
,

•

it is imperative to keep a severe conceptual differentiation

[
123
, 1
-
st column]
between term ‘the state’ and ‘physically
sounding’ adjectives/verbs and the spatiotemporal or cause-effect
images.

Similarly T. Maudlin: “we need to keep the distinction between
mathematical and physical entities sharp. Unfortunately, the usual
terminology makes this difficult”
[
119
, p. 129]
. Even
indirect usage of the terminology borrowed from the classical
description can be a source of confusing. For example, a so-called
exchange interaction as a ‘cause of correlation’ between identical
parts of system.

It seems preferable to radicalize the non-connectivity of these
categories, i. e., to proclaim it a postulate. For instance, boldface
italics in Remark
12
or selected thesis on
page
•
‣
6.5.1
. At least, the differentiation between them
should not be neglected in reasoning, inasmuch as it seems
unrealistic to change the deeply ingrained
[
177
, p. 7]
and
ill-defined terminological locutions such as “photon is in a certain

state of polarization
…
one
photon being in a
particular place”
[
44
, p. 5, 9]
, “an observable has/acquires
a (numerical) value when being measured”
[

, p. 310;
criticism]
, “outcome of a measurement”
[

,
85
,
103
]
, “quantum parallelism”
[
85
, p. 282]
, or ‘simultaneous
measurability’; see sect.
2.1
and
pr.
I
. (As if we’ve had some
micro-physics prior to math; there is nothing a priori.) With this
mixing, the circular logic pointed out in Remark

will be
present at all times. See, for instance, pages 29–30 in the work

[

]
and notably an emphasized warning by D. Foulis about
“
a mistake, and a serious one
!”, including criticism
addressed to von Neumann on p. 29. This

•

trap of the ‘

ting the

‘

|\boldsymbol{\psi}\rangle
and of
+
’ in (

).

Again (see p.

and sect.

), even the

‘reconcile’ its non-classicality with any
observational
prototypes
are hopeless. “The wave function is in the head and not

function is the very information
DataSource
around which
all sorts of words on physics—readings, frequencies, objects,
phenomena, particles, events, and other entities—are only slated
to create.

•

“We cannot … manage to make do with such old, familiar, and
seemingly indispensible terms” (Schrödinger (1933)) as the
““ physikalische Realität ” …. “ Realität der
Aussenwelt ”, “ Real-Zustand eines Systems ””

[

, p. 34]
in the way we are doing this in classical

)
[

,

, criticism]
should be dismissed from
QT
-fundamentals, because the
microscopy of quantum
{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-clicks shows that this
colloquial habit is an unmeaning collocation.

This term may only be a theoretical conventionality in the follow-up

physical
theory. See also the first sentence in
[
84
]

and selected theses on page
•
‣
4.2
and at the beginning of
sect.
2.4
.

The principled abstractness of the
|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle
-object

[
66
, pp. 27–28]
is a core attribute of quantum theory
as contrasted to the classical one. This abstraction cannot be
‘struggled’, it is not an idealization of something
phenomenological. It is absolute. An interpretative comprehension
like
|\boldsymbol{}\text{ dead}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}|\boldsymbol{}\text{ alive}\rangle
, even if it
is permissible, may issue only from the
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\rangle
-representations

\mathfrak{a}_{1}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}\mathfrak{a}_{2}\boldsymbol{\cdot}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{2}\rangle\mathbin{\hat{+}}\cdots
, i. e., from a treatment of the

(\mathbin{\hat{+}})
-addition (of quantum amplitudes) as an accumulation of

{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}
-microevents; many ‘cat boxes’. In other words,

•

the
interpretation of the quantum state
is its very
definiendum
(
98
)–(
101
). Even with the physical
terminology created, there may be only one paraphrase for the
meaning to the state: an abstract element of the
abstract

⊚
⊚\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\circledcirc}}

⊚
⊚\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\circledcirc}}

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\circledcirc}}

The point
4
) in
Theorem

determines a supplement—the number add-on over the utterly
abstract
LVS
.
linear (not Hilbert
[
28
]
) vector space over

{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
.

The “not Hilbert” here is because the norm and inner-product are the
extra—nonessential—math add-ons over
{\mathbb{H}}

[
28
]
,
which come from the follow-up introducing the Born statistics

[
27
]
. In and of itself, the state needs none and knows nothing
of them. These concepts, similarly to the descriptive physical
notions and a measurement, will be required further, not now, for
calculation of observable quantities: math-calculus of statistics

\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{k}
and of means.

We may not blend the fundamentally abstract part of quantum
mathematics—pre-physics and the structural properties of

{\mathbb{H}}
—with those in charge of its observational/physical
constituent; i. e., we may not ascribe the ontological status

[
106
,
105
]
to everything. In the strict sense, the
ontology of/and physics, the classical one included, cannot arise
before the statistical processing of quantum micro-events.
(Parenthetically, the 6
-
th Hilbert problem on “Mathematical
Treatment of the Axioms of Physics”
[
4
]
becomes thus an
ill-posed problem
[
28
, sect. 8]
.) The processing itself begins
with the Born rule
[
27
]
.

Continuing scheme (
70
), a certain parallel takes place
between the following couples:

\begin{array}[]{rr@{}c@{}l}\text{\footnotesize observations' language:}&\lceil\text{${\mathbb{R}}$\penalty-\hskip 0.0ptnumbers}\rceil&\;\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{+}}}&\lceil\text{physical quantities/\hskip 0.0pt\ldots}\rceil\\[4.30554pt]
&\downarrow\;\uparrow&&\qquad\downarrow\;\uparrow\\[4.30554pt]
\text{\footnotesize quantum language:}&\lceil\text{${\mathbb{C}}$-,\penalty\kern 2.77802pt\ignorespaces\penalty\hskip 0.0pt$|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle$\penalty-\hskip 0.0ptobjects}\rceil&\;\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{+}}}&\lceil\text{physical properties/\hskip 0.0ptdata/\hskip 0.0pt\ldots}\rceil\end{array}\;.

Just as we are not rasing the question about abstractness/treatment
of the
\lceil
{\mathbb{R}}

\lceil

-,

from the $-symbol in
\$5
, the number 5 in and of itself may carry
neither the financial nor any other (‘bank/(non)commuting/…’)
treatment; nor does it contain some hidden ‘microeconomic’ content.
The number has no a ‘retrograde memory’.

The first ‘summands’ in the aforementioned

\vbox{\hbox{$($}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{+}}}}\vbox{\hbox{$)$}}
-conjunctions are the abstracta of
principle. They may exist as the ‘math-things-in-themselves’ and we
know that they really do just as we are comprehending the existence
of the
\mathbb{N}
-arithmetic that has been constructed in
sect.
7
. The second ‘summands’ are the interpretative
supplementations in their own rights. If a theory does not spell out
a nature of accounting the second to the first
(pr.
II
), then it is impossible to
find-out/guess the ‘true’ interpretation or nonexistent physical
‘protosource’ of the abstracta
\mathsf{n},\mathfrak{a}
, and
|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle

‘ab intra’ their algebras (
73
)–(

), (

),
(

)–(

), (

),
(

)–(

) or from the Hilbert-space
mathematics. See again Remarks
2
,
16
and warnings
by Ludwig of “a mistake. … false notion that “mathematical
objects” must be pictures of physical objects”

[

, p. 228]
and of a “reality [of the] word “state,” a
reality in which one must not believe!”
[
113
, p. 78]
.
A. Peres also makes special note of the analogous: “… physicists
have been tempted to elevate the state vector
\psi
to the status
of substitute of reality”
[

, p. 645]
; and D. Mermin puts
this as “a regretable atavistic tendency to reify the quantum state”

[

, p. 144]
.

10.3.
Measurement ‘problem’

The most representative example is the (in)famous problem of
measurement
[
131
, sect. V.4 and Ch. VI]
—“tyranny of
thinking of von Neumann measurements”
[
64
, p. 534]
with the
collapse postulate. This is the subject of an “endless stream of
publications suggesting new theories … unending discussions … symposia”
[
88
, p. 519]
and of “the mountains of
literature”
[
112
, p. 118]
containing opposing opinions

[
88
, Ch. 11]
,
[
122
,
91
,
85
,
103
,
108
,
126
,
33
]
. It is, indeed, the source of questions around
locality in
QM
. As we have seen, this problem “is simply not a
problem at all!”
[
62
, p. 1013]
. It is a
nonexistent—“the alleged … does not exist as a problem of
quantum theory”
[
50
, p. 15]
—as well as a pseudo problem
and a non-issue
[
111
, p. 79(!)]
,

[
112
, p. 118(!)]

[
46
]
, because

•

in measurements, nothing either propagates (much less at
superluminal speeds) nor interacts, nor is anything collapsed

[
110
, sect. XVII.4.3]
,
[
103
, p. 328]
,

[
13
,
113
]
, nullified, localized, there are no
such things as quantum jumps
[
188
]
, and no “pieces” of the
wave function are “cut out”
[
197
, p. 57, 158]
.

It is no exaggeration to say that the need to projective
postulate—“a fruit of realist thinking”

[
129
, p. 172]
—is much the same as the necessity for the
world ether supporting the electromagnetic waves. All the more so
because such a view of the theme has been present in the literature
for quite a while
[
52
,
78
,
162
,
107
,
97
,
66
,
111
,
112
,
129
]
even as appeals.

“There is nothing … problematic about measurement”

.
L. Ballentine (1996)

“… there is no collapse of wave packets in reality. Do not
believe in fairy tales!”

.
G. Ludwig
[
113
, p. 104]

“A state vector … does not evolve continuously between
measurements, nor suddenly “collapse” into a new state vector
whenever a measurement is performed”

.
A. Peres
[
134
, p. 644]

“This “reduction” … is not a new fundamental process, and, … has nothing … to do with measurement”

.
L. Ballentine
[
15
, p. 244]

“The mystifying notions arise from attributing physical reality to
the “jump” at a given time
t
”

.
G. Ludwig
[
110
, p. 327]

“Really bad books … claim that the state of the physical system
… collapses into the corresponding
u_{n}
. This is sheer nonsense.
(Finding appropriate references is left as an exercise for the
reader.)”

.
A. Peres (2003)

Englert
[
50
, p. 8]
does particularly object to the
“folklore that “a measurement leaves the system in the relevant
eigenstate” … It is puzzling that some textbook authors consider
it good pedagogy to elevate this folklore to an “axiom” of quantum
theory”. See also the 2
-
nd epigraph to sect.
2
.

The point here, put very briefly, is that the measuring ‘problem’ is
one of principle, not of practice. Expressed by Bell’s words, “the
word [measurement] has had such a damaging effect on the discussion,
that … it should now be banned altogether in quantum mechanics”

[
20
, p. 216]
. J. Bub and I. Pitowsky do insist in the book

[
148
, p. 453]
that presumptions “about the ontological
significance of the quantum state and about the dynamical account of
how measurement outcomes come about, should be rejected as
unwarranted dogmas about quantum mechanics”.

Another example of circular logic is the critiqued
[
85
,
167
,
168
]
meaning of the phrase “an ensemble of similarly
prepared systems”
[
12
,
85
,
148
]
. The revision of
this (by and large correct) idea, as was set forth above, does
actually demonstrate that, like in the ensemble approaches, “quantum
mechanics is a
statistical
theory”
[
129
, p. 2]
,

[
113
, p. 123]
,
[
125
, p. 223]
,
[
12
,
15
,
202
,
6
,
111
,
113
,
160
,
127
]
with
a frequency content of the randomness and the classical logic

[
113
]
but with a different math-calculus of the statistical
weights. The “different” is due to the fact that the theory is not
tied, as in the classical description, to the notion of an
observable quantity, and the
\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}
’s are calculated from the
‘other/abstract’ numbers
[
27
]
. However, for the same reason,
emphasizing a close resemblance with the statistical mechanics

[
54
,
116
]
,
[
52
, p. 72]
and ‘explanations’
with playing cards/dice, coins/balls/…/urns/‘socks’

[
20
, Ch. 16]
or with the classical phenomena—unusual as
it may sound—are in error. The case in point is not a drastic
dismissal of the classical ideas, but rather a ‘quantum audit’ of
the classical-physics language
[
28
, sect. 8]
. The correct
‘audit’ of the classical is a
re
creating of this very
classical:

(
114
)

\displaystyle\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0pt\wavy{classical phenomena}\penalty$\rceil$}\rightarrowtail\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptclassical events/objects\penalty$\rceil$}\rightarrowtail\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptmicro world\penalty$\rceil$}\rightarrowtail

\displaystyle\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptmicro-event\penalty$\rceil$}\rightarrowtail\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptquantum
micro-event\penalty$\rceil$}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\rightarrowtail$}\hss}\raise 5.59721pt\hbox to11.11115pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle(!)$}\hfil}}\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0pt\emph{abstract} click\penalty$\rceil$}\rightarrowtail

\displaystyle\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptabstraction
${\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mkern-1.0mu\mkern-7.0mu\leadsto$}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox to8.33339pt{\hfil\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}\hfil}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$\penalty$\rceil$}\rightarrowtail\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0pt$\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}$\penalty-\hskip 0.0ptbrace \eqref{obj}, \ldots\penalty$\rceil$}\rightarrowtail\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptstate $|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle$\penalty$\rceil$}\rightarrowtail

\displaystyle\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptobservable concepts\penalty$\rceil$}\rightarrowtail\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptobservable numbers\penalty$\rceil$}\rightarrowtail\cdots\rightarrowtail

\displaystyle\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptstatistics, the concept of a mean\penalty$\rceil$}\rightarrowtail\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptstate, objects, \ldots, physics\penalty$\rceil$}\rightarrowtail

\displaystyle\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0pt\wavy{classical phenomena}\penalty$\rceil$}

and, consequently, creation of the classical concept of a measuring
process. Thus, this scheme along with quanta’s statistics and

LVS
-mathematics all add up to a positive answer to Wheeler’s
question: “Is the entirety of existence, rather than being built on
particles or fields of force or multidimensional geometry, built
upon billions upon billions of elementary quantum …, … acts of
“observer-participancy,” …?”
[
154
, p. 286]
,

[
64
]
.

10.4.
Interpretations and self-referentiality

Although we have not yet touched on other significant
attributes—the means over statistics, operators, and products of

{\mathbb{H}}
-spaces will be considered in their own rights—it is clear
that the need to quest for a description in terms of hidden
variables is also eliminated. Even from a formalistic perspective,
the proof of the presence/absence
[
131
,
45
,
70
]

of these ‘physical’ quantities should be attributed to the semantic
conclusions of meta-theory (
=
physics)
[
142
]
, i. e., to
theorems
about
formal theory rather than to theorems of its

inner
calculus. In our case, and more generally, the formal
theory is the syntactical axioms of
QM
. The corollaries of such
axioms are inherently unable to lead to statements about
interpretations
[
142
]
, since theorems
about

object-theory itself is not provable by means
of
its
object-language
[
159
,
142
]
. In a word, the nature and
interpretation of axioms are not recovered from the very axioms or
from the replacement thereof by the other ones.

A similar line of reasoning has accompanied
QT
for quite a while:
“claim that the formalism by itself can generate an interpretation
is unfounded and misleading”
[
13
, p. 38]
. It is known
that even the mathematics itself cannot be grounded in a
self-contained way
[
68
]
(!),
[
60
,
96
]
,

[
82
, p. 201]
. All of this stands in stark contrast
with the known statement of DeWitt to the effect that “mathematical
formalism of the quantum theory is capable of yielding its own
interpretation”
[
43
, pp. 160, 165, 168]
or that
“conventional statistical interpretation of quantum mechanics thus
emerges from the formalism itself”
[
43
, p. 185]
. In
particular, if we take account of the fact that it is not the theory
itself, but only its formal interpretation that determines the very
semantic terms truth/falsity/provability of sentences (K. Gödel).
In turn, “interpretation … allows a certain freedom of choice”

[
87
, p. 310]
. See also
[
22
]
and specifically
Ch. III in
[
96
]
. In other words, the subconscious striving
for ‘to interpret’—and transporting the macro into the
micro—is the very thing that prevents us from truly gaining an
understanding of quantum mathematics.

In any case, the fact that we were initially constructing the
set-theoretic model (cf.
[
22
]
) rather than an
interpretation simply eliminates the problem or, at most, transfers
it into the domain of questions about micro-transitions

\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.90286pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}
and
{\mathfrak{T}}
-family as entities being employed
(see Remark
2
). This is the domain of questions that invoke
the set theory and touch on ontological status of sets at all

[
60
, sects. V.8 and 9(!)]
. Be this as it may,
logic—formalized or non—does not allow us to make statements
about statements, much less a statement that refers to itself. The
self-referentiality (“von Neumann catastrophe”) is almost the chief
trouble
[
126
,
129
]
encountered in quantum foundations.

All of this, of course, does not depend on whether interpretation is
built in a strictly formalized form
[
159
]
or in a
physically natural one. In effect, the issue of
interpretations—in the rigorous definition sense

[
142
, Ch. 2]
,
[
159
]
,
[
169
]
—is simply
nonexistent. Accordingly, the demystification of the known and the
quest for ontological interpretations to
\mathfrak{a}
-coordinates of
the
|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle
-vector
[
9
,
56
,
78
]
—the wave
function—is no longer a problem, and with it disappears the
Feynman question of “the
only
consistent interpretation of
this quantity”
[
55
, p. 22]
. See also M. Leifer’s review

[
106
]
and extensive list of references therein.

11.
Closing remarks

… quantum mechanics has been a rich

source for the invention of fairy tales

— G. Ludwig & G. Thurler

[
113
, p. 122]

I simply do not know how to change quantum mechanics

by a small amount without wrecking it altogether …

any small change … would lead to logical absurdities

— S. Weinberg (1994)

11.1.
Language and ‘philosophy of quanta’

Remembering and continuing sect.
1.3
, it is generally
tempting to infer that when creating the theory, we may not rest on
any meanings that are tacitly associated with the typical
terminology—no matter physical or mathematical—and on the
tacit assumption that customary concepts are substantially correct

[
82
]
.

One should also be very cautious about the wording of statements
concerning the phenomena outside the everyday experience. One means
that even the very natural utterances—‘here/there, electron

with
Alice/Bob’ (locality), ‘big(ger)/small(er)’, ‘let there
be a two-particle
\mathcal{S}
’ (quantitative statements), ‘subsystem

\mathcal{S}_{1}
in such-and-such system
\mathcal{S}
, consisting of …’
(statements about structure)—are de facto “(apparently)
“plausible” conclusions from the observed phenomena”

[
110
, p. 334]
. These have comprised an equivalent of a
measurement/preparation (
[
129
, pp. 195–196]
and
sect.
3.1
) and of physical (pre)imagery, and thereby
imitate the way of thinking and schemes of classical mechanics; see
also the 2
-
nd epigraph to sect.
6
.

•

The “particle, here/there, big/small, this/that/another one,
before/after”, and the like are
already
the ‘illegal’
observation’s numbers of sorts and a premature arithmetization,
i. e., this is
already
the subconscious quantifying the
micro-events or the arrays thereof by a theory, and classical
(
18
)–(
19
) at that.

Reality’s attributes are yet only slated to create. Say, when we
decrease the particles in experiments and reach the atomic level, we
still stay in the atomistic paradigm of the particle and of numbers:
the objects having mass, their coordinates, degrees of freedom,
etc. This is a mistaken intuition. Very informally, we should
‘religionize themselves’ to the quantum micro-events, while the
return to the words ‘particle/…/macro’ must be performed by a new
reasoning mechanism. It comprises, apart from the quantum-
LVS
apparatus (sect.
8.1
), the re-creating the very classical
concept of the particle as schematized in (
114
).

At the other extreme is an attempt to ‘hurry up’ and bring the
reasoning to a Hilbert-space theory or to the quantum mixtures
(
105
). As in sect.
6.5
, all this may well be
incorrect
[
119
]
. A source of antinomies is in the implicit
implying, i. e., in the eclectic—this we stress once
again—confounding the observations, clicks, numbers, physics,
time, math, and imagination, followed by the
uncontrollable

lexical-‘branching’ such as
replacing the symbol
\mathbin{\hat{+}}
with a
meaning
taken from reality. For instance, the emerging the word
‘simultaneously’ in the sequence
\lceil
the
(\mathbin{\hat{+}})
-superposition of
multiple states
\rceil

\rightarrowtail

\lceil
simultaneously
\rceil

\rightarrowtail

\lceil
quantum parallelism
\rceil

[
172
, p. 26]
.
W. James has underscored that the “
viciously privative
employment of abstract characters and class names
is, …, one of
the great original sins of the rationalistic mind”

[
64
, p. 547]
. This results in the sense messes, well-known
no
-
go (meta)theorems
[
20
,
69
]
, the locality
‘problem’ in
QM
, and paradoxes such as the
epr

[
110
, sect. XVII.4.4]
or the jocular Bell question: “Was
the world wave function waiting to jump for thousands of millions of
years … for some more highly qualified measurer — with a
Ph.D.?”
[
20
, p. 117]
,
[
103
, p. 18]
,
[
107
,
97
]
. As to the no-go theorems, Ballentine remarks that “the
growing number [thereof], combined with some peculiar terminology,
has led to confusion … A woefully common feature, … each
protagonist had some interpretation of the quantum state in mind,
but never stated clearly what it was”
[
17
, pp. 2 and
6]
. Ludwig, echoing Ballentine, asks: “But what do we
mean by the notion of a state?”
[
109
, p. 5]
.

Clearly, the quantum-clicks do not depend on whether personified
homo sapiens interpret the arrays thereof, or a biological observer
such as a “Heisenberg–Zeilinger dog”
[
95
, pp. 171–174]
,

[
50
,
23
]
does simply perceive. The
observer—without his “subjective features”
[
82
, pp. 55,
137]
or “the anthropomorphic notions “specifying” and
“knowing””
[
134
, p. 645]
—is just a formally logical
element
O
in theory. Without numbers,
solely a quantitative theory is not possible (sect.
6.5
),
because the entire terminology becomes indefinite.

Thus, once a mathematics and
unambiguous

language—spectra, means, and macroscopic dynamical models—have
been created, not only is there no longer a need to call on the
‘otherworldly’, eccentric, or anthropic explanation ways, but the
very presence of a certain share of mysticism, of subjectivity, and
of (circum-)philosophy
[
197
]
—“a philosophical

Überbau
”
[
50
, p. 12]
—in quantum foundations
becomes extremely questionable. Ludwig is much more thoroughgoing in
his assessment of the language games—the “philosophical
gymnastics”
[
111
, p. 79]
.

Eventually, we have no longer any freedom to invent exegeses of the
quantum-postulate as ‘a Bible‘ or “a sacred text”

[
64
, p. 1038]
. Moreover, the liberty to ask questions is no
longer there, since the created object-language of states, of
spectra, and of frequencies narrows down the entire admissible
lexicon. It is able to generate questions that are not only
ill-posed but must, as in sect.
6.5
, be qualified as
“meaningless”
[
130
, p. 422]
. For example, those that are
based on a (human-beings’)
intuitive
taking the term
observation or questions about ‘the underlying nature of reality’.
As we mentioned earlier, the notion of ‘a physical level of rigor’
(in reasoning) and the physical justification will not help us with
regard to the grounds of
QT
. Another example is the attempts at (or
“to refrain from”) “tying description to a clear hypothesis about
the real nature of the world” (Schrödinger (1933)) and, in
general, the question of ‘how it should function’ at the
micro-level. See also
[
113
, p. 100]
on “reality”.

In the classical framework, the language sentences are always
interrelated, since
all
of them, one way or another, handle
the observational notions. In the famous Como address, N. Bohr had
remarked that “every word in the language refers to our ordinary
perception”. These notions, in medias res, form our natural speech
when describing experiments but are inadequate in the quantum

[
82
]
(!). That is, these concepts do not make clear
the fact that behind the
QT
are some structureless
abstracta—we believe that these are

\{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.90286pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{},\;\cup\}
and procedures
(
112
)—rather than an ‘improved’ physicomathematical
axiomatics or sophisticated math vehicles; e. g., non-commutative
calculus.

The language intuition usually makes it easy for us to do away with
paradoxes the semantic closedness causes. However, the quantum
situation is just the one of a misuse of the vocabulary, i. e., when
contradictions are inevitable, and this unlimited source of
confusion demands a
controlling the language over itself
. One
does create the other (‘relative’) languages within itself

[
36
]
; at first, the language of quantum mathematics and
thereafter the language of math-physical description and of
classical physics, followed by the language of the semantic
interpretations. This is just what we call the metamathematics and
math-logic
[
96
]
, discriminating between metamathematics and
philosophy
[
142
]
. If this is not the case—the ‘quantum
conclusions’ from thinking (even if partly/implicitly) in terms of
physical influences between the classical objects (Deutsch’s “bad
philosophy”)—then we obtain an everlasting source of paradoxes,
since human intuition has roots in the classical world and is a
rather problematic and personal category. A. Stairs calls upon
“Don’t trust intuition”
[
166
, p. 256]
because it is not
meant for
QM
.

Inasmuch as the conceptual autonomy in quantum fundamentals is
minimal (sect.
2
), the quantum scheme of things must commence
with an extremely ‘ascetic’ language (Remark
10
), and it
should be independent of our intuitive knowledge, which “tend to
declare war on our deductions” (van Fraassen). To avoid collisions
between theory and meta-language, the subconscious striving of the
natural language to include one in the other has to be limited.
Einstein adds also the situations when “er führt dazu, überhaupt
alle sprachlich ausdrückbaren Sätze als sinnleer zu erklären”

[
47
, p. 33]
. A. Leggett’s comments on “pseudoquestions”
and “gibberish” at the end of sect.
1.2
may then be
strengthened so that the meaninglessness by itself should become a
constitutive element of language, including the language of
‘philosophy of quanta’.

•

The

rudimentary quantum (meta)mathematics creates
the notion of a
prohibited
statement/phrase/question, one
that is devoid of meaning. This are sentences that involve the
classical analogies in the circumvention of 1) the

|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\rangle
-representatives to the non-interpretable abstraction

|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\rangle
and of 2) the numerical quantities’ nature
(sect.
9.1
).

It is appropriate at this point to quote the ’t Hooft remark “I go
along with everything [Copenhagen] says, except for one thing, and
the one thing is you’re not allowed to ask any questions” and the
Einstein reasoning on page 669 in the collected articles

[
149
]
: “One may not merely ask … not even ask what this
…
means
”. See also Heisenberg’s discussion of the problem

\lceil
language
\rightleftarrows
concepts
\rceil
on pages 48–54 in

[
81
]
, his work
[
83
]
, the pages 234–235
in
[
149
]
with Bohr’s appeals regarding the “necessity of a
radical revision of basic principles for physical explanation … revision of the foundation for the unambiguous use of elementary
concepts”, and his comments on words “phenomena”, “observations”,
“attributes”, and “measurements” on p. 237.

The literature on this subject, even taking only the qualified
sources into account, is vast
[
1
,
18
,
7
,
42
,
51
,
56
,
63
,
69
,
86
,

,

,

,
132
,
148
,
152
,
154
,
70
,
198
,
202
,
103
,
85
,
168
,
9
]
and abounds with
terminology—“words,
ostensibly
English” (A. Leggett

[
103
, p. 300; emphasis ours]
)—that defies translation into
the language of events or of concretization: observer’s
consciousness, parallel/branching universes/worlds, free will,
catalogue of knowledge, world branch, and also such collocations as
rational agent, information (
“Whose”
and “about

what
?”
[
20
]
, by “Bell’s sardonic comments”

[
85
, p. 262]
) has been recorded/transmitted/(not)reached an
observer (Wigner’s friend), teleporting a state,
many-minds/worlds/words, quantum psychology, psycho-physical
parallelism (in this connection, see
[
35
, p. 86(!)]
), and
many other “bad words” by Bell. He italicizes them on p. 215 of

[
20
]
.

Of course, “without philosophy, science would lose its critical
spirit and would eventually become a technical device”

[
9
, p. 800]
but, on the other hand, “the concept of the
free will cannot be defined by indications on devices”

[
112
, p. 151]
, and “one must not confuse physics with
philosophy”
[
50
, p. 12]
. Furthermore, yet, we should like
to remind a Heisenberg attitude
[
83
]
on “a
misconception … [and ‘possibility’] to avoid philosophical
arguments … and the way of thinking of … physicists who
insisted on not dealing with philosophy”. Namely, “[w]e can not
avoid using a language bound up with the traditional philosophy”.
One cannot but mention the Rovelli article
[
143
]
that is
entirely devoted to this topic. Therefore, “[i]t must be our task to
adapt our thinking and speaking—indeed our scientific
philosophy—to the new situation” with regard to
abstract

meaning of the linear quantum addition
\mathbin{\hat{+}}
and quantum math
altogether; all of the quotations are from pages 32 and 37–38 of
work
[
83
]
.

As concerns the attitudes towards
QM
—at the suggestion of
M. Tegmark in the 1990’s, there even carried out polls and
statistical analysis of their correlations
[
156
]
. There
are also known attempts to involve here the biology of
consciousness/brain
[
168
,
176
]
,

[
151
, sect. 6]
,
[
152
, Ch. 9]
. Regarding them,
however, there have been not merely sceptical but quite the opposite
opinions
[
187
, sects. 17.5–6]
,

[
112
, sect. XII.5(!)]
. Of special note are Ballentine’s
remark “to stop talking about “consciousness” or “free will”” on
the last page of the preprint
[
17
]
and Popper’s
criticism as to “the alleged …
intrusion of the observer,
or the subject,
[or of consciousness]
into quantum theory

… based on bad philosophy and on a few very simple mistakes”

[
139
, pp. 11, 17, 42; everything as in the original]
with an
appeal “to exorcize the ghost called “consciousness” or “the
observer” from quantum mechanics”
[
139
, p. 7]
. “[Q]uantum
mechanics is a physical theory, not psychology”

[
129
, p. 83]
.

11.2.
Math-‘assembler’ of quantum theory

As a result, we gain “a contribution to philosophy, but not to
physics”
[
117
, p. 86]
. At the same time, the proposed math
‘
\cup
-assembler’

\boxed{{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{j}\not\approx{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}_{k}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.90282pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mkern 1.0mu,\qquad\text{$\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.0mu\left\lgroup\right.\mkern-4.0mu$}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Xi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-2.5mu\left.\right\rgroup\mkern-4.0mu$}$\penalty-\hskip 0.0ptbrace \eqref{split'}\kern 0.55557pt,\qquad$(\mathbin{\mathchar 8799},\in,\cup)$\penalty-\hskip 0.0ptlogic \eqref{union}--\eqref{cup}}}

is quite sufficient for creating the object-language. Giving a
natural form to it would be acceptable, however, it is clear that
the set-theoretic
\cup
-base of the language cannot be avoided

[
22
,
60
]
. Nevertheless, the syntactically more formal
description of the sequence
\lceil
transitions
\rightarrowtail

brace
\rightarrowtail
numbers
\rceil
is surely of interest until the way
of looking at quanta’s mathematics is harmonized with the
math-logic. This would turn, however, all the above material into a
pure-logic text, which we eschew in the present work. It is probably
for this reason that the very important and extremely thorough
works

⋇
⋇\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\divideontimes}}

⋇
⋇\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\divideontimes}}

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\divideontimes}}

Pre-theories, 76 axioms
[
111
, p. 241]
,
ordered sets, morphisms, absence of the word superposition in
monographs
[
109
,
111
]
, the (valid) criticism of
“theories of … so-called states”
[
113
, p. 78]
, etc.
by
Günther Ludwig
[
109
,
110
,
111
,
112
]
and by
his school are often left out of the literature on quantum
foundations. Among other things, in spite of explicit pointing out a
“solution in principle of the measuring problem” in

[
111
, p. V]
and subtitle “Derivation of Hilbert Space
Structure” of
[
111
]
, name of this author has not been
mentioned in the detailed reviews
[
106
,

,

]
and
even in the books
[

,

,

,

,

,

]
.

11.3.
Well, where’s probability?

An answer to this question in
quantum
elements is brief
enough—nowhere. “There is no probability meter”
[
148
, p. 185;
S. Saunders]
, and relationship of this concept with
empiricism is unique
[
15
, p. 46]
—the statistical
proportions
\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{k}
. Cf. the famous de Finetti (1970) claim that
“probability does not exist”
[
63
]
and A. Khrennikov’s
remarks to the effect that “
the only bridge between “reality”
and our subjective description is given by relative frequencies
”

[
64
, p. 139]
and that “Experimenters are only interested in
… frequencies”
[
93
, p. 36]
. Or, more carefully stated by
von Mises’ words,

“If we base the concept of probability,
not on
the notion of
relative frequency, … at the end of the calculations, the meaning
of the word ’probability’
is silently changed
from that
adopted at the start to a definition based on the concept of
frequency”
[
125
, p. 134; all the emphasis ours]
.

Indeed, suppose that the word frequencies has been banned

[
33
, p. 44]
in substantiating the
QT
-elements; so also
have the usage of the words ‘over/repetition/…/statistics’. Then
the questions do immediately arise: why the Kolmogorovian axiomatic,
and why does it have this very quantification? i. e., why
zero/one/…/positive? why not the
(-1\ldots 1)
-interval? whence the
single-case probability postulates? … subjectivity? Well, what is
the quantification thereof, and what does subjectivity do in the
natural-scientific theory?

“… it is very doubtful that quantum probabilities can be
introduced as a measure of our personal belief. Well, it may be
belief, but belief based on frequency information”

.
A. Khrennikov; Växjö Conference (2001)

One way or the other, quantum foundations
would demand an
interpretation
of Kolmogorov’s axioms (besides, these are not
categorical in contrast to
LVS
), and the latter, in turn, demand
interpreting the concept of the number—an axiomatic add-on

over
the
ZF
theory
[
100
]
.

Bearing in mind the primary nature of numbers and nontriviality of
their emergence in physical theory (sect.
7.2
), it is not
just impossible to avoid the statistical weights
\mathtt{f\mkern-1.0mu}_{k}

[
135
, p. 25]
. Logic forbids them from being subsidiary with
reference to probability in any definition: “probability is the
picture for reproducible frequencies; and it is the [only]

prescription
for a
correct
experiment”

[
112
, p. 144]
. Pauli, among the few, had been “convinced
that

•

the concept of ‘probability’ should not occur in the fundamental
laws of a satisfying physical theory”.

.
(an excerpt from his 1925 letter to Bohr)

Ensemble empiricism, for its part, is self-sufficient, and the only
conventionality within it is an infinite number of repetitions. In
this connection, we cannot agree with a statement of theorem III in
van Kampen’s work
[
91
, p. 99]
and with further comment as
to “a single system” and “calculation of spectra”. At the same time,
for formalizing the infinite, there is an appropriate axiom

in
the
ZF
-theory
[
100
,
80
]
.

To say all this still informally, any non-statistical/non-ensemble
framework for what we have been calling
QM
-probability does
explicitly or implicitly—if the expression may be
tolerated—‘parasitize’ on statistics by addressing the words
“repetitions, the multiplied, …” and, at the same time, does
‘attract the empirically vague justifications’ in terms of
anthropomorphic surrogates: potentiality, tendency, propensity, the
amount of ignorance, subjective uncertainty
[
148
]
or
likelihood, degree of belief, and the like
[
56
]
. But even from
philosophical point of view “probability is a deeply troublesome
notion”
[
154
, p. 78; L. Hardy]
, that is supported by the
vast literature on this subject
[
94
]
(!),
[
174
]
(!),

[
171
, pp. 41–43]
,
[
161
, Chs. 3–4]
,
[
9
,
3
,
42
,
95
]
. According to Deutsch, D. Papineau calls
“this state of affairs … a scandal”
[
148
, p. 550]
.

An Einsteinian “scientific instinct”
[
85
, p. 174]
against the
probability is very well known
[
87
]
, and Pauli, again, had
been recollecting his (Einstein’s) frequent remarks in this regard:
“One can’t make a theory out of a lot of ‘maybe’s’ [
=
probably]
… deep down it is wrong, even if it is empirically and logically
right”. More to the point, the question of what exactly is meant by
a probability
event
, i. e., “Probability of
what

exactly?”
[
20
, p. 228]
is also a matter of principle. The
answer to it, as seen above, is this: ‘not of the classical events’,
i. e., “[n]ot of the …
being
”
[
20
, p. 228]
such as
‘
QM
-cats’, ‘particle is here/there’, ‘roll of the dice’, and the like.
An excellent text about probability and the aspects of the
probability-physical constructs is the work
[
5
]
. Its
‘verdict’ concerning the treatment of this concept
[
5
, sects. 4.5
and 8]
is clearly Misesian
[
125
]
, i. e. the
“
ensemble
and
frequency
”
[
94
, p. xiii]
.

Thus to sum up, the philosophy/axioms of probability or its ‘quantum
deformations’ should not be present in
quantum
foundations.
There cannot be hidden details underlying the quantum probability
because the “details” imply some terminology with a classical
content. Quantum probability is the statistical regularity. It comes
from Kollektivs
[
125
]
of abstracta (
34
) and may
only be a shortened term for the relative “frequencies in long runs”
(von Neumann); “the Einstein hypothesis” by M. Jammer

[
88
, p. 441]
. The realistic/physical/…/pictorial
adjectives and descriptive supplementations to the term “long runs”
are prohibited. This is why the conventional tractability of the
quantum-postulates’ mathematics, i. e., the calculation of

probabilities for the classical
events to occur in the
reality—‘alive/cat/…/imploded/bomb’—“is not adequate”
neither as a doctrinal point of departure nor as a post-math
interpretation. It presents us with a circulus vitiosus of
re-exegeses. Fuchs, referring to de Finetti’s words in an interview
with Quanta Magazine (4 June 2015), prognosticates that this
conception “will go the way of phlogiston”. The “not adequate” is a
R. Haag quotation, and he expresses this “conviction”, applying it
even to “the conceptual structure of standard Quantum Theory”

[
73
, p. 743]
.

The ultimate conclusion completes Remarks
2
and
7
.
If we accept the set-theoretic eye on things then sect.
5.1
,
by all appearances, provides a positive answer to the question about
the rigidity of
QT

[
38
]
—“change any one aspect, and
the whole structure collapses”
[
1
, p. 1]
; see also the
second epigraph to this section. At least, it is hard to imagine

what any other
axiom-free way of turning empiricism into
quantum mathematics would look like, as soon as we abandon the
primitive minimality of the scheme

\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptdistinguishable micro ${\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$\penalty-\hskip 0.0ptevents\penalty$\rceil$}\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{+}}}\text{$\lceil$\penalty\hskip 0.0ptensembles of abstracta
${\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\Psi\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.90286pt{\hfil\hbox{$\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1081\mathchar 1099$}\hfil}\hss}\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}$}}{\mkern 1.0mu\underline{\mkern-1.0mu\alpha\mkern-1.0mu}\mkern 1.0mu}{}$\penalty$\rceil$}\;.
